# 017 — W5 close-out: guards that failed open, tests that could not fail, and what the services finally showed

**Date** 2026-10-06, with additions of 2026-10-07, 2026-10-08, 2026-10-09, 2026-10-10, 2026-10-12, 2026-10-13, 2026-10-19, 2026-10-22, 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24 (each dated) · **Status** resolved, with three things open: first `migrate-preview` run pending; §2.5 not fixed; §5.2 data only, awaiting a ruling. §2.7's two findings (2026-10-09) are both fixed: the first, that nothing ran `audit` or `trivy` when nothing was pushed, on 2026-10-19 by `.github/workflows/advisories.yml`, merged on 2026-10-23 and green on its first run; the second, an unknown guide's Open Graph card drawn on demand and cached, on 2026-10-19 by `dynamicParams = false` on both image routes, re-measured on Next 16.4.1 on 2026-10-23. §5.6's finding 2 was ruled on on 2026-10-07, fixed by 2026-10-08, and that fix was repaired on 2026-10-09 after a review found it still lost answers; it had not been run on production when that was written. §5.8's defect (the part edit form wrote one part's value to another) was fixed on 2026-10-09; that fix was repaired the same day, after a review found that it deleted a stored value and that two writes of one bike could overwrite each other, again on 2026-10-10 after a second review found that its record of the form's own writes did not survive a route change, once more that day after a third found that record shared by every guest bike, and on 2026-10-12 after a fourth found text typed for one guest bike written to the bike another page had put in its place (that repair was verified the same day with nothing blocking, and the three small things left were closed on 2026-10-13 with no change to the product); none of it is deployed, and three things it leaves as found await a ruling: leaving a number field that holds unreadable text, the order of two writes of one field in transit together, and what else still follows the guest bike's slot rather than the bike (the guest import's clear above all). §5.9's defect (one over-long answer on a guest's to-fix list hid the whole list, and over 120 characters blocked the import of the bike — live on production) was found on 2026-10-08, ruled "fix before the tag" and fixed on 2026-10-13; not deployed · **Wave** W5 (launch)

`.debug/016` was about files whose only reader was a service. This note is about
what was written to close those gaps — a boot check, a build preflight, two
workflows, an advisory rule, a launch checklist — and it has one finding, many
times over: **each was correct for the case its author ran, and nothing had run
the other case.**

| The case that had been run                                     | The other case                                                                                                       | §   |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- |
| one green `audit`                                              | six runs in ten minutes on one unchanged tree: two red                                                               | 2.6 |
| a page on a refused server: 500                                | a file on the same server: 200, the `window.__va` chunk included                                                     | 3.1 |
| a health check that retried past every 500                     | a healthy server never answers one: an early request is held                                                         | 3.2 |
| `vercel-build.sh` with `VERCEL_ENV` set                        | without it: every guard off, the build green                                                                         | 3.5 |
| a "stays silent" test that spied `console.error`               | Prisma's own line goes to `console.log`                                                                              | 3.9 |
| the switch mistyped while the secret was empty                 | with the secret present: the script went on to migrate                                                               | 4.2 |
| a ref-guard test fed the pooled fixture                        | the guard's `exit 1` deleted: 51/51 still green, the pooled guard supplied the exit                                  | 4.4 |
| the validator's triggers, filtered on `renovate.json`          | Renovate's own pin bump edits only the script: the job would not have started                                        | 4.7 |
| `bash scripts/ci.sh`                                           | `npm run ci:local`: exit 127 on this machine                                                                         | 5.1 |
| the plan's Lighthouse command for production, as written down  | read against the configuration: it would start a local server first (`-p 3100`, or `-p 80` with `LHCI_BASE_URL` set) | 5.4 |
| `npm run dev` on a checkout that had already been built        | on a clean clone: the first checkup page answers 500, and after it every route does                                  | 5.6 |
| a guest import fed a hand-written checkup that carries `steps` | the checkup the wizard really stores has none: it is skipped, on production too                                      | 5.6 |
| the fix for it, fed a checkup answered under ONE scope         | a run opened on one part and finished through another link: 1 answer of 34 imported, and the other 33 cleared        | 5.6 |
| a re-import of the same checkup, reported "already imported"   | the same checkup carried on since the first import: reported the same on its id alone, and cleared                   | 5.6 |
| the part form, rendered with a part set by hand                | the part changed under the open tab: the fork's travel shown, then written, for the dropper post                     | 5.8 |
| the fix for it, fed numbers a field can read                   | `45.` on the way to 45.5, and the tab pressed: the stored width deleted, the write answering ok                      | 5.8 |
| that repair's record of writes in flight, with ONE repo object | the route left and come straight back to — a second object over the same bike: the value typed last not sent         | 5.8 |
| the next repair's record, keyed by bike, with two SAVED bikes  | two guest bikes one after the other under `/velo/local`: the second inherits the first's record, and a no-op is sent | 5.8 |
| two guest bikes one AFTER the other, the first one's page gone | the second stored by another page UNDER the first one's form: what was typed for the first is written to the second  | 5.8 |
| a list fed hand-written lines, its form driven by its selects  | a product title pasted into the form's text field: at the 65th character the list is gone, the import with it        | 5.9 |

Most were found before merge by adversarial review (#16 and #17: four lenses,
every finding re-checked by a second reviewer, the fixes attacked again); the
rest by walking `docs/deploy.md` §5 and executing the lines an agent can run
(§5.5 says which were not) — the last two rows on 2026-10-07, from a clean
clone.

Every claim below is **measured** (its command, its date, who ran it), **read**
(a source line or a vendor's documentation, with the date it was fetched) or
**not observed**. The two pull requests' own notes ended "nothing here was
observed on Vercel" and "nothing here has run on GitHub or against Neon"; §3.10
and §4.11 are what the first deployments and the first workflow run have shown
since, and what nobody has seen yet. **A fifteen-minute reading** is this
opening, §1, §3.10, §4.11, §5 and §6; the rest is the evidence.

**Who measured.** **[I]** the implementer of a pull request; **[D]** its
documentation slice, re-running on the branch's final tree; **[R]** the
four-lens review and its second verifiers; **[O]** the orchestrator in the
closing session; **[W]** the browser-driven walk from a clean clone that the
orchestrator delegated on 2026-10-07 (§5.6). Where a thing was measured more
than once the numbers are given separately; they agree.

**Versions**, read off the installed packages: Next 16.3.6, Prisma 7.10.0,
`@auth/core` 0.41.3, Vitest 4.1.11, renovate 44.108.1 (the pin in

## 2. Advisories: a gate that reads a live feed

[`audit-ci-allowlist.md`](../audit-ci-allowlist.md) holds the detail: every id,
range, exposure paragraph and compatibility note. This section is only what two
days taught about the gate itself.

### 2.1 `audit` turned `main` red three times in two days

| Push to `main` (UTC)   | CI run      | What failed                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Answer                                             |
| ---------------------- | ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| 2026-10-04 17:03 (#12) | 37219094191 | `audit`, on `braces`                                                                                                                                                                                           | allow-listed (no patched release); landed with #13 |
| 2026-10-06 09:32 (#13) | 37443674634 | `audit` on four ids; `trivy fs scan` on one of them (`source-map-js`)                                                                                                                                          | PR #14                                             |
| 2026-10-06 15:54 (#14) | 37491401212 | `audit`, on GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w (`sharp`, high), published 2026-10-06T13:43:57Z — six seconds after PR #14's last `audit` job had started (13:43:51Z, run 37473100340, head `a01ce76`; it passed at 13:44:42Z) | PR #15                                             |

No commit caused any of the three: the gate fetches advisories live, by design.
`main`'s CI for `d0acb87` was all green; `npm audit` on the final tree finds 19
vulnerable packages under six ids, all allow-listed.

### 2.2 The lowest-release rule met a release inside the 7-day hold

`source-map-js` 1.2.2 (GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q) is the first patched release and the
only one, so "the lowest release that clears the advisory" and "hold every
update for 7 days" could not both be obeyed. It was **5 d 21 h** old when
committed; the hold clears 2026-10-07T14:08Z.

- **Who decided.** Three independent reviewers said merging inside the hold was
  the maintainer's decision. The maintainer was asked and answered "Merge now";
  the ruling is a comment on PR #14, which merged at 15:54:16Z on 2026-10-06,
  about 22 hours before the hold cleared.
- **What was checked instead.** All 18 installed files are byte-identical to the
  upstream tag `v1.2.2` (commit `0a1d334`, which is the registry's `gitHead`).
  `audit-ci-allowlist.md` says why this is not a precedent.

### 2.3 An override on a package that is not the vulnerable one

`sprintf-js` (GHSA-hp3w-g68c-fv3c) has no patched release, so there is nothing
to pin it to. PR #14 removed it with the scoped override
`"js-yaml@^3": { "argparse": "^2.0.1" }` — `argparse@1` is what brought it in.
The other three ids were in-range `npm update`s: `compression` 1.8.1 → 1.8.2
(GHSA-vc2v-76pw-4v95), `proxy-addr` 2.0.7 → 2.0.8 (GHSA-jqcg-44mw-7w3h),
`source-map-js` 1.2.1 → 1.2.2. Seven lockfile entries moved in all.

### 2.4 An exact pin where `npm update` would have floated

`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30.0 → 1.31.0 (GHSA-6qxp-vccf-f47h, published
2026-10-06T15:35:44Z). Its parent's range already admits the patch, so the rule
says `npm update` — which takes the newest release a range admits: 1.32.1, one
day old. It is pinned **exactly** at 1.31.0 through `overrides`.

`sharp` 0.35.4 → 0.35.5 (GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w) was a plain `npm update`: 27
lockfile entries, `sharp` and 26 `@img/*`. Unlike everything else these two days
touched, `sharp` IS in the deployed server's traces.

### 2.5 Renovate reads an override as a dependency — found, not fixed

Renovate's Dependency Dashboard (issue #11) proposes updates for existing
`overrides` entries. Read on 2026-10-06: `toml` ^5.0.0, `uuid` ^14.0.0 and
`mysql2` 3.24.4. Read again on 2026-10-07: those three under "Awaiting
Schedule" (`mysql2` now 3.24.5), and two more under "Pending Status Checks" —
`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.31.0 → 1.32.1, the release §2.4's exact pin was
written to hold back, and a lockfile update of `basic-ftp` to 6.2.2. The only
entry `renovate.json` caps is `argparse`, below 3 (added with #14). Nothing
was changed for the others. (Since 2026-10-24 a second one is capped: the
exact `jsdom > undici` pin, below 9, added with it — `audit-ci-allowlist.md`,
“Ten advisories of 2026-10-23”. The others are still uncapped.)

### 2.6 The gate flapped while an advisory propagated

While GHSA-6qxp-vccf-f47h was still arriving — 85 to 95 minutes after its
publication at 15:35:44Z — `npx audit-ci --config audit-ci.json` failed **2
runs of 6** on one unchanged tree within ten minutes (17:00Z–17:10Z): the
registry does not serve a new id to every request at once. On a day advisories
are landing, one green `audit` run is not a verdict.

### 2.7 Six on `next` at once, and nothing on GitHub was red (2026-10-09)

Added 2026-10-09 **[O]**, on the branch `w5/advisories-3`: Next 16.3.6 →
16.3.8, with Node v24.16.0, npm 11.13.0 and trivy 0.71.0. All times UTC;
the detail is in `audit-ci-allowlist.md`, “Six advisories on `next`, published
2026-10-07”.

| When               | What                                                                                                           |
| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 2026-09-30 16:07   | `next@16.3.8` is published. The branch that became #12 takes 16.3.6 instead: lowest release, and past the hold |
| 2026-10-07 14:52   | `main`'s last CI run starts (37640409895, `9ae46bf`) and passes, `audit` and `trivy fs scan` included          |
| 2026-10-07 16:07   | 16.3.8 clears the 7-day hold                                                                                   |
| 2026-10-07 20:30   | six advisories on `next` are published within 78 seconds, every range ending at `<16.3.8`                      |
| 2026-10-08 and -09 | the nightly `Perf` runs on `main` pass (37761960705, 37916337229); they run neither gate                       |
| 2026-10-09 13:52   | on a fresh `npm ci` of `9ae46bf`: `audit` exit 1 on five ids, `trivy` exit 1 on CVE-2026-94483                 |
| 2026-10-09 13:55   | the pin at 16.3.8: `audit` exit 0, six runs of six; `trivy` exit 0 at 14:47                                    |

Three things about the gate, and one about the bump.

- **The gate reads the feed only when something runs it** — measured. For 41
  hours `main` was red by its own rule and green on GitHub. No push, so no
  `CI` run; the two workflows with a `schedule:` are `perf.yml` (nightly) and
  `codeql.yml` (weekly), and neither runs `audit` or `trivy`. Dependabot's
  open alerts, read at about 14:45Z on 2026-10-09, were #4 and #12
  (`extract-zip`) and nothing on `next`. Renovate's dashboard (issue #11) was
  last updated at 2026-10-07T14:54Z, before the advisories, and still listed
  “update next + react to v16.3.7” under Awaiting Schedule. What showed the
  six ids was a local `bash scripts/ci/audit.sh` (reported in the brief for
  this branch, re-measured at 13:52Z). **Found here; fixed on 2026-10-19**
  **[I]**, by the maintainer's ruling between three options (a workflow of
  its own, two more jobs in `perf.yml`, or relying on the alerts):
  `.github/workflows/advisories.yml` runs `scripts/ci/audit.sh` and
  `scripts/ci/trivy.sh` on `main` every day at 05:23 UTC and on dispatch,
  with ci.yml's exact `run:` lists, and its report job opens one issue on the
  first red run on `main` and comments on it on every later one — never
  closing it, because of §2.6. Its three contexts are non-blocking, and must
  stay so: a context that only a schedule produces never reports on a pull
  request, so it could not be a required one — the reason `.debug/016` §2
  gives for path-filtered ones. `CLAUDE.md` ("Quality gates") has the shape
  and the tests that hold it. What relying on the alerts would have given,
  read on 2026-10-19: Dependabot opened alerts for four of the six
  (GHSA-cjq9-62q9-8jv4, -4jqv-mc3x-m676, -f87g-xv8r-7p7x, -39w2-rjm5-chcv;
  alerts #16–#23, one per manifest) on 2026-10-16 between 16:14Z and 17:04Z,
  8 d 20 h after publication, and none for GHSA-mcj8-r9mp-w47p or
  GHSA-3w37-wq28-93x7. Renovate's pull request #28 (`next` 16.3.8,
  `[security]`) followed at 2026-10-17T07:25Z, 9 d 11 h after, and was red in
  46 s on every job that runs `npm ci`: it moved `package.json` and not the
  lockfile (`Invalid: lock file's next@16.3.6 does not satisfy next@16.3.8`,
  run 39013603559) — not looked into further here. **First run** **[I]**, after
  #31's merge (`fdb24c2`, 2026-10-23 15:09Z): a dispatch on `main`, run
  39975945661, at 15:10:12Z — `npm audit (scheduled)` green in 26 s,
  `trivy fs scan (scheduled)` in 19 s, then `advisory report (scheduled)` in
  5 s, which logged "both gates green on refs/heads/main: nothing to report"
  and wrote no issue. **Not observed yet**: a scheduled start (the cron, and
  how late an off-the-hour one starts — the two on the hour started 4.6 to
  7.5 h late in every run read on 2026-10-19), a red run, and the issue it
  writes. **Reported since, not read** (2026-10-24 **[I]**): the brief of the
  branch that answered it says the scheduled check of 2026-10-24, at 09:37Z,
  was red and wrote issue #39 — which would be the first red run and the first
  issue. That branch read neither the run nor the issue (§2.8).
- **`npm audit` proposes the newest release, not the lowest** — measured. Its
  `fixAvailable` named 16.4.0, 2 d 19 h old and inside the hold; all six
  ranges end at `<16.3.8`, 8 d 21 h old. Read the range, not the proposal.
- **The release the rule declined on 2026-09-30 was the fix** — read. §2.2 was
  the rule meeting a release inside the hold; this is the same rule keeping
  the tree on 16.3.6 for nine days after the vendor had shipped the fix. On
  2026-09-30 no public advisory named 16.3.8, so there was nothing for the
  rule or for `audit` to see. Its release note, read on 2026-10-09, calls it a
  security release and lists the six; whether it said so that day was not
  established. The rule is unchanged, and whether it should change is the
  maintainer's ruling.
- **What a patch bump of the framework was checked against** — measured,
  because 16.3.8 is not a small patch: 79 files differ outside source maps,
  `dist/esm` and `dist/compiled`, among them the response cache, the
  file-system cache and `next-server.js`. (a) The lockfile: twelve entries,
  the Next family, nothing else. (b) The four facts `instrumentation.ts` rests
  on (§3.1, §3.2): `instrumentation-globals.external.js` and
  `router-server.js` are byte-identical in the two tarballs, `start-server.js`
  and `bin/next` differ by the version string alone, and a refused boot was
  measured again on 16.3.8 — `/api/health`, `/fr`, `/fr/connexion`,
  `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml` and `/favicon.ico` 500, `/tree-drawings.json`
  and a chunk 200, the process alive, one `[env] contract enforced` line on a
  clean boot. The fourth fact, the held request, was read again and not
  re-polled. (c) The build, `main`'s tree against this one in both hooks
  modes: the same route table, 65 / 63 files through `bundle-guard`, +95 B
  gzip on each budgeted route, the boot tier 4/4. (d) A fresh build of each
  release behind `next start`: 44 requests to 39 URLs, identical line for
  line.

**One thing changed, on disk.** A self-hosted server's on-demand cache entries
moved from beside the prerendered files to
`.next/server/route-cache/<kind>/<sha256 of the owning route>/$/…`. Nothing in
this repository reads them.

**One thing was found that the bump did not change; it was fixed on
2026-10-19** (below). `/fr/guides/<unknown>/opengraph-image` answered 200
with the generic card on 16.3.6 and on 16.3.8 — `MISS`, then `HIT`, and two
files written per unknown slug — while the guide page beside it answered 404:
`page.tsx` sets `dynamicParams = false` and the image route exported no
segment option of its own.

**Not run [O]:** e2e, perf and Lighthouse. `bash scripts/ci.sh` and
`ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1 NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS=1 bash scripts/ci/build.sh` passed
on the result; the browser tiers on the pull request are the first on 16.3.8.

#### The two card routes, fixed (2026-10-19)

Added 2026-10-19 **[O]**, on the branch `w5/og-image-dynamic-params` (then on
`w5/advisories-3`; rebased onto `main` on 2026-10-23, below): Next 16.3.8,
Turbopack, Node v24.16.0.
Each side measured on a fresh `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1 NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS=1
bash scripts/ci/build.sh`, then `next start` with `AUTH_URL` on the served
port, and curl.

**Why the page's option never reached the card** — read in
`node_modules/next`. An image route is a route handler of its own: Next wraps
the file in a `GET` that re-exports every named export, so an option has to
be exported by the image file itself. The handler answers 404 when the
prerender manifest marks its route `fallback: false` and the path was not
prerendered. On the old build, `.next/prerender-manifest.json` had `false` for
`/[locale]/guides/[slug]` and `null` (render on demand) for
`/[locale]/guides/[slug]/opengraph-image` and `/[locale]/opengraph-image`.
With `export const dynamicParams = false` in both image files, all three are
`false`.

**The site card had the same shape, past the proxy.** As recorded on
2026-10-09, `/de/opengraph-image` answers 307 to `/fr/de/opengraph-image`,
which is a 404 from the catch-all page. But `proxy.ts`'s matcher excludes any
path with a dot in it (`.*\..*`, so that files pass straight through), and a
first segment with a dot reaches `[locale]` with no proxy in front of it:

| Request                                             | Before                                                    | After                     |
| --------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| `/fr/guides/does-not-exist/opengraph-image` ×2      | 200 `image/png` 30 760 B, `MISS` then `HIT`               | 404 `text/html`, no cache |
| `/en/guides/check-flux-capacitor/opengraph-image`   | 200 `image/png` 31 487 B, `MISS`                          | 404 `text/html`, no cache |
| `/fr/guides/a.b/opengraph-image`                    | 200 `image/png` 30 760 B, `MISS`                          | 404 `text/html`, no cache |
| `/x.y/guides/check-brakes-disc/opengraph-image`     | 200 `image/png` 40 129 B (the FR card), `MISS`            | 404 `text/html`, no cache |
| `/de.x/opengraph-image` ×2                          | 200 `image/png` 48 149 B (the FR card), `MISS` then `HIT` | 404 `text/html`, no cache |
| `/de/opengraph-image`                               | 307 → `/fr/de/opengraph-image`, 404                       | the same                  |
| `/{fr,en}/guides/check-brakes-disc/opengraph-image` | 200 `image/png` 40 129 / 36 818 B, `HIT`                  | the same                  |
| `/{fr,en}/opengraph-image`                          | 200 `image/png` 48 149 / 43 656 B, `HIT`                  | the same                  |
| `/fr/guides/does-not-exist` (the page)              | 404                                                       | 404                       |

**On disk**, the same 16 requests left 18 files under
`.next/server/route-cache/APP_ROUTE/` on the old build — ten for the five
unknown pairs, eight for the four known cards, which 16.3.8 copies there on
their first read — and 8 on the fixed one, the known cards only.

**What the fix removed.** Both image files used to fall back instead of
refusing: the guide card drew `site.name` when `findGuide` found nothing, and
both cards drew the default locale for an unknown one. With the option set,
neither branch can run (`generateStaticParams` lists only pairs that exist),
and the build did not need them: 219 of 219 static pages, the route table
identical line for line, 94 guide cards and 2 site cards prerendered, and the
four known cards in the table the same byte count as before. Each fallback is
now a `notFound()`, as in `page.tsx`'s `resolve()`, kept because `findGuide`
and `hasLocale` have to narrow their types somewhere.

**The log.** A card's 404 prints nothing: six of them added no line to the
server log. A page's 404 still prints `Error: Internal: NoFallbackError`, the
noise `.debug/003` recorded on 16.3.4.

**Tests.** `guides.spec.ts`, "an unknown guide has no Open Graph image either",
in FR and EN, and `seo.spec.ts`, "an unknown locale has no Open Graph card",
run once because its subject is a locale that is neither. Against the old
build, both were red (`Expected: 404, Received: 200`; the second on
`/de.x/opengraph-image`). Against the fixed one, both spec files passed 53 of
53 on each of `desktop-chromium`, `mobile-chromium`, `mobile-landscape` and
`no-webgl`. `mobile-narrow` does not match these specs. `mobile-webkit` was
not run: Playwright 1.63's WebKit build is not installed on this machine.

**Gates [O]**, on the final tree: `bash scripts/ci.sh` passed, twelve steps
of twelve in 167 s; `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1 NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS=1 bash
scripts/ci/build.sh` passed, with 219 of 219 static pages, `bundle-guard` on
65 files and the boot tier 4/4. On that build, with Playwright starting its
own server, the two spec files passed 212 of 212 across the four projects
above. Perf, Lighthouse and the rest of the e2e suite were not run.

**Re-measured on Next 16.4.1** (2026-10-23 **[O]**), after the branch was
rebased onto `main`'s `a257f05`, in the same way.

- **Before the fix**, on `main` itself, 16.4.1 behaved as 16.3.8 did, line for
  line: both card routes `fallback: null`, every unknown pair in the table 200
  at the same byte counts, `MISS` then `HIT`, and 18 files on disk.
- **With the fix:** all three routes `fallback: false`; every unknown pair 404,
  with no cache header; 8 files on disk, the known cards; and the four known
  cards 200 `HIT` at the same byte counts. The build had 219 of 219 static
  pages, a route table identical to `main`'s, 94 guide cards and 2 site cards
  prerendered, `bundle-guard` on 63 files (16.4.1's count with the hooks on)
  and the boot tier 4/4.
- **The two spec files** passed 212 of 212 on the same four projects, and
  `bash scripts/ci.sh` passed twelve steps of twelve in 170 s on the rebased
  tree.
- **The log is the one thing that differs.** 16.4.1 prints no
  `NoFallbackError` line for any 404, card or page. A page 404 under a first
  segment with a dot renders the root not-found page, and that prints Next's
  `metadataBase` warning once per process. On a fresh server,
  `/x.y/guides/check-brakes-disc` printed it, and `/de.x/opengraph-image`
  after it added nothing.

**Not observed:** a webpack build (GHSA-f87g-xv8r-7p7x was about webpack
builds ignoring exactly this option, and 16.3.8 is that fix; no build here
has used webpack), and Vercel, where an unknown pair should get the same 404
from a `fallback: false` route.

## 3. The environment contract, at the Vercel build and at boot (PR #16)

`lib/env.ts` had declared the contract since W0 and nothing ran it. W5 wires it
twice — `instrumentation.ts` at every server start, `scripts/check-env.ts`
during the Vercel build — and moves Prisma's `error` log from stdout to an event
so that the rate limiter's expected P2025 can be dropped. Three sentences the
first version wrote down as measured were false: "every request is a 500"
(§3.1), "a healthy server answers one 500 for a moment" (§3.2), "one P2025 per
fresh key" (§3.9).

**The review.** Four lenses (the backlog's text; production boot; tests and
gates; the Prisma log). No finding against the design survived as a blocker;
fifteen should-fix findings were confirmed and fixed; two more reviewers
attacked the fixes (fourteen smaller items, thirteen applied — not applied:
forwarding arguments through `npm run lhci`). The review is what added
`scripts/check-env.ts`, the refusal of an absent or unknown `VERCEL_ENV`, and
the `[env] contract enforced (VERCEL_ENV=…)` line.

**Build under test** in §3.1–§3.8: `.next/BUILD_ID` `AQp4izPPR4hTfQ8LkTkSO`,
made 2026-10-06 16:40:36 (zone not recorded) by
`ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1 NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS=1 bash scripts/ci/build.sh` — a
HOOKS-ON build, which is why a chunk carrying `__va` exists in §3.1.

### 3.1 A failed `register()` is not an exit code, and a refused server still serves files

**Read** in the installed `node_modules/next/dist` (16.3.6; line numbers checked
[D]):

- `server/lib/router-utils/instrumentation-globals.external.js:54` —
  `if (process.env.NEXT_PHASE === 'phase-production-build')` returns early:
  `register()` does not run during `next build`. `:46` — a missing hook file
  (`ENOENT`, `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`) is "no
  instrumentation", silently.
- `server/next-server.js:499` —
  `this.prepare().catch((err)=>{ console.error('Failed to prepare server', err) })`:
  the rejection is logged and swallowed in the constructor.
- `server/base-server.js:488` — `await this.prepare();` at the top of
  `handleRequest`; `:1046-1052` — `preparedPromise` is cleared only in `.then`,
  so a rejection is kept and re-awaited by every request.
- `server/lib/start-server.js:188-189`, `:199-200`, `:221-223` — the request
  handlers await `handlersPromise`; `:443` `process.exit(1)` is not reached.
- `bin/next:65,84` — `process.env.NODE_ENV = process.env.NODE_ENV || defaultEnv`,
  `defaultEnv` being `production` for every command but `dev`.
- `server/lib/router-server.js:455` — `serveStatic(...)` answers
  `nextStaticFolder` / `publicFolder` outputs before any `invokeRender`.
- `server/route-modules/route-module.js:373` and
  `build/templates/middleware.js:137` —
  `await ensureInstrumentationRegistered(...)`: route handlers and the Node
  proxy, the two paths Vercel uses, await the hook too. Read; on Vercel only a
  PASSING hook has been observed (§3.10).

**Measured** [D], `node <scratch>/slice-c/serve-probe.mjs refused 3000` (the
probe is described at the end of §3): `next start` on a free port with
`.env.test` underneath, `NODE_ENV` removed, `VERCEL_ENV=production` and
`ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1`; four clients polling eight paths from the moment of
spawn, for 3 s after the first answer.

```
mode=refused next=16.3.6
first answer: 500 for /fr/connexion at 416 ms after spawn
connection errors before the port was bound: 136
  /_next/static/chunks/<chunk> 200: 1424
  /api/health 500: 1422
  /favicon.ico 500: 1424
  /fr 500: 1422
  /fr/connexion 500: 1423
  /robots.txt 500: 1424
  /sitemap.xml 500: 1424
  /tree-drawings.json 200: 1424
total 500s: 8539
static chunk: 4862 chars, contains __va: true
process alive at the end: true
"Ready in" printed: true; "Failed to prepare server" lines: 2; Ready before the failure line: true
"[env] contract enforced" lines: 0
EnvValidationError issue lines (names and rules only):
  - ENABLE_TEST_PAGES must never be set on a deployment (VERCEL_ENV=production)
  - NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS must never be set on a deployment (VERCEL_ENV=production)
  - NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_LOGIN must never be set on a deployment (VERCEL_ENV=production)
```

(All three flags are listed because `.env.test` sets all three.) [I] measured
the same with its own probe: about 1 520 answers per path in 3 s, same split,
process alive, the failure line logged twice. [R] did twice more, adding `/en`,
`/fr/velo/demo`, `/fr/dev/bike3d`, `/api/auth/session`, `/api/auth/providers`,
`/_next/image?…` and an unknown path to the 500 column and a CSS file under
`/_next/static` to the 200 column.

**So**: no exit code, no crash; Next prints "Ready" first. Every page, route
handler and metadata route answers 500 for as long as the process lives;
**files under `/_next/static` and `public/` are still served** — on this
hooks-on build, the 4 862-character chunk that carries `window.__va` among them.
A boot refusal keeps every page from loading a poisoned bundle and leaves the
chunk one URL away, which is the real reason `NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS` is refused
when the bundle is BUILT (`scripts/vercel-build.sh`, step 2). Case 2 of
`tests/boot/env-contract.test.ts` holds it — `/api/health` 500 and
`/tree-drawings.json` 200 on the same refused server — as an observation, not a
requirement: the case says what to flip if a future Next answers 500 there too.

### 3.2 A request that arrives early is held: a 500 from `/api/health` is a verdict

**Measured** [D], same probe, three healthy servers, four clients from spawn:

```
mode=deployment   (VERCEL_ENV=production, three flags "0")       first answer 200 at 437 ms; 2 759 answers, total 500s: 0
mode=preview      (VERCEL_ENV=preview, no AUTH_URL, no Google)   first answer 200 at 325 ms; 2 901 answers, total 500s: 0
mode=local        (no VERCEL_ENV, three flags on)                first answer 200 at 318 ms; 2 968 answers, total 500s: 0
```

Each had 72–80 connection errors first: before the port is bound the connection
is REFUSED, and that is the only thing worth retrying. An earlier run the same
day gave 3 187 / 3 193 / 3 048 answers, zero 500.

- [I]: zero 500 in about 2 700 answers per server (production, preview, local),
  first answer at 394–473 ms.
- [R]: zero 500 in about 45 000 answers across 12 boots, eight clients from
  spawn, database on a closed port (so the route's own answer was 503),
  including one slow start (`Ready in 12.5s`, first answer at 12 879 ms).
- [R], decisive: with the compiled hook made to sleep 4 s before `getEnv()`, a
  healthy server's first answer came at 4 740 ms and was the route's own;
  requests sent from about 120 ms WAITED. On a refused server with the same
  delay the first answer was a 500 at 4 303 ms: the 500 appears only after
  `register()` rejects.

**So**: `/api/health` itself only returns 200 or 503
(`app/api/health/route.ts`), and a 500 from it is never a boot race.

**In the tests.** `healthResponse`, which retried past every 500 for 90 s,
became `firstResponse` + `expectHealthy`. [I]: with every child refused
(`AUTH_SECRET=too-short npx vitest run --project boot`) the two must-boot cases
fail in 366 ms and 361 ms with the `EnvValidationError` in the message. Before,
the same refusal took 90 114 – 90 348 ms to report [R].

### 3.3 The one line a passing check prints

`instrumentation.ts`: after `getEnv()` succeeds, and only when `VERCEL_ENV` is
set, `console.info("[env] contract enforced (VERCEL_ENV=<value>)")`. [D], on
the built hook: 1 line for `production`, 1 for `preview`, 0 for a local server,
0 for a refused one.

Why it exists [R]: calling Next 16.3.6's `ensureInstrumentationRegistered` on
scratch copies of the build with a poisoned environment —

```
a-nohook        (hook file absent):                RESOLVED (server would serve); lines Next logged: 0
b-hook-only     (hook entry, chunks absent):       RESOLVED (server would serve); lines Next logged: 0
c-no-env-chunk  (lib/env chunk absent):            REJECTED [ChunkLoadError]
d-complete:                                        REJECTED [EnvValidationError]
```

A deployment that passed the contract and one whose bundle never contained the
hook both answer 200; the line is the positive evidence.
(`.next/server/instrumentation.js.nft.json` lists the hook's three chunks and
Next's adapter, `build/adapter/build-complete.js`, merges them into every node
output, so the bundle was expected to be complete. Since observed: the line is
in Vercel's runtime log, once per function cold start — §3.10.)

Pinned by `tests/unit/deploy/instrumentation.test.ts` (7 cases); the boot tier's
cases 1, 3 and 4 hold it on the built hook.

### 3.4 The build-time preflight, and what `vercel-build.sh` now refuses

**The gap** [R]. With `npx`/`npm` stubbed, the previous `vercel-build.sh` under
`env -i VERCEL_ENV=production ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1 NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_LOGIN=1` and
no `POSTGRES_*`, `AUTH_*` or `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` printed
`▶ prisma migrate deploy (VERCEL_ENV=production)`, ran the migration, the build
and the bundle guard, and exited 0. `register()` is skipped during a build
(§3.1), so a scope's VALUES were first evaluated by the deployed server — after
promotion, after the production migration — and a pull request's preview only
evaluates the Preview scope.

**The order now** (`scripts/vercel-build.sh`): (1) `VERCEL_ENV` must be
`production | preview | development`; (2) `NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS` off
(`1 | true | yes`, any case, is on); (3) `npx tsx scripts/check-env.ts`; (4)
`npx prisma migrate deploy` only when `VERCEL_ENV=production`, then
`npm run build`, then `npx tsx scripts/bundle-guard.ts`. Steps 1–3 each exit 1
before anything is migrated or built.

**Measured** [D], under `env -i` with fixture values (`db.invalid` hosts, a
made-up secret; no real value was read):

```
$ env -i PATH=… HOME=… bash scripts/vercel-build.sh
✗ VERCEL_ENV is not set, and this is the Vercel build entry point.
  Every deployment guard keys off it: without it the test-flag refusal is off
  and a production deployment would not migrate.
  On Vercel: Settings → Environment Variables → the System Environment Variables
  checkbox (Vercel's docs: "Enable access to System Environment Variables")
  must be ticked — then redeploy.
  Not on Vercel: run `npm run build` (or `bash scripts/ci/build.sh`), not this script.
exit=1

$ env -i … VERCEL_ENV=staging bash scripts/vercel-build.sh
✗ VERCEL_ENV=staging is not production, preview or development.
  lib/env.ts accepts only those three and refuses to boot on anything else
  (see the comment on VERCEL_ENV there before changing either side).
exit=1
```

`npx tsx scripts/check-env.ts` under `env -i`, on a fixture base (both database
URLs on a `db.invalid` host, a made-up secret of 32 characters or more,
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`) plus what each row names. Fourteen rows are
`<scratch>/slice-c/check-env-probe.sh`, run twice on the final tree with
identical output; the 31-character, scheme-less and leading-space rows were run
by hand.

| Environment                                                                | Result                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `VERCEL_ENV=production`, complete (`AUTH_URL` + Google pair)               | `check-env: environment contract satisfied (VERCEL_ENV=production, isProduction=true).` exit 0                                                           |
| `VERCEL_ENV=preview`, no `AUTH_URL`, no Google pair                        | `… satisfied (VERCEL_ENV=preview, isProduction=false).` exit 0                                                                                           |
| `VERCEL_ENV=development`, same                                             | `… satisfied (VERCEL_ENV=development, isProduction=false).` exit 0                                                                                       |
| `VERCEL_ENV=production` without `AUTH_URL` and the Google pair             | `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID is required in production` / `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET is required in production` / `AUTH_URL is required in production`, exit 1               |
| `VERCEL_ENV=preview` + `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1`                               | `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES must never be set on a deployment (VERCEL_ENV=preview)`, exit 1                                                                       |
| `VERCEL_ENV=production` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_LOGIN=yes`                     | `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_LOGIN must never be set on a deployment (VERCEL_ENV=production)`, exit 1                                                               |
| production, 31-character `AUTH_SECRET` + `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1`             | BOTH listed: `AUTH_SECRET must be at least 32 characters (npx auth secret)` and the flag, exit 1                                                         |
| production, `AUTH_URL=example.test` (no scheme) + `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_LOGIN` | `AUTH_URL Invalid URL` / `AUTH_URL must start with http:// or https://` and the flag, exit 1                                                             |
| production, `POSTGRES_URL` with a LEADING SPACE                            | `POSTGRES_URL must be a postgresql:// connection string`, exit 1 (`lib/db/env.ts` trims and would connect)                                               |
| preview + `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` MISSING            | only `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL Invalid input: expected string, received undefined` — the flag is NOT listed — then `… the next build may name more.`, exit 1 |
| `VERCEL_ENV=staging`                                                       | refused on `VERCEL_ENV` (`Invalid option`, the line under this table), exit 1                                                                            |
| `VERCEL_ENV=preview` + `VERCEL_TARGET_ENV=staging`                         | satisfied, `isProduction=false` (parses as a preview)                                                                                                    |
| production, Neon's `DATABASE_URL` / `DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED` names only     | refused on `POSTGRES_URL` and `POSTGRES_URL_NON_POOLING`, exit 1                                                                                         |
| no `VERCEL_ENV`, `NODE_ENV=production`, three flags on, Google pair set    | satisfied, `isProduction=true` — the local production server, by design                                                                                  |
| no `VERCEL_ENV`, `NODE_ENV=production`, Google pair EMPTY                  | `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID is required in production` / `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET is required in production`, exit 1 — the README's `.env.local` under `next start`       |
| no `VERCEL_ENV`, `NODE_ENV=development`, Google pair empty                 | satisfied — `npm run dev`                                                                                                                                |
| no `VERCEL_ENV`, `NODE_ENV=development`, Neon's names only                 | refused on both `POSTGRES_*` names (missing), exit 1                                                                                                     |

```
VERCEL_ENV Invalid option: expected one of "production"|"preview"|"development"
check-env: refused for VERCEL_ENV=<scope>. Fix the variable(s) named above in THAT scope — Vercel → Settings → Environment Variables — then redeploy.
```

The second line ends every refusal. The message is built from zod's issue paths
and messages: names and rules, never a value (pinned with a canary secret in
`tests/unit/deploy/vercel-build-guard.test.ts`,
`never prints a value when it refuses`).

- **A missing variable hides the cross-field rules.** zod skips `superRefine` on
  a type-level issue (a missing key, a non-numeric `BCRYPT_COST`); a too-short
  secret, a scheme-less URL, a non-postgres URL or an out-of-range cost do not
  hide them ([I], and the table). `parseEnv` is unchanged by ruling —
  `lib/env.ts` differs from the branch's previous commit in comments only [I] —
  so `check-env` says "the next build may name more" instead.
- **`check-env` has one caller.** Not `npm run build`, not
  `scripts/ci/build.sh`: CI builds without a production secret
  (`tests/unit/deploy/migrate-on-deploy.test.ts`,
  `keeps the contract preflight out of every build that is not Vercel's`). CI's
  contract is not weaker for it: `instrumentation.ts` evaluates it on every
  `next start` CI does (boot check, boot tier, Playwright's web server,
  Lighthouse).
- **The contract is stricter than what merely works.** Auth.js accepts any
  non-empty secret (`node_modules/@auth/core/lib/utils/assert.js:59`,
  `if (!options.secret?.length)`), the contract wants 32 characters;
  `lib/db/env.ts` trims the database URLs, the contract anchors
  `^postgres(ql)?://` on the raw value. By NAME all three scopes parsed ([R],
  [I], [D]); nobody on the branch could see the values. The first production
  build was the first evaluation of Production's values, and it passed (§3.10).
  A refused value fails that BUILD; the fix is the value, never the rule.

### 3.5 `VERCEL_ENV` absent: every guard failed open at once

Every guard keys off `VERCEL_ENV`: the contract's flag refusal and production
requirements (`lib/env.ts`), the hooks refusal and the migrate gate
(`scripts/vercel-build.sh`), `scripts/bundle-guard.ts`.

[R], before the fix: `vercel-build.sh` with stubs, `NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS=1`
and no `VERCEL_ENV` → `▶ skipping prisma migrate deploy (VERCEL_ENV=unset)`, the
build, exit 0. [D], on started servers (the probe of §3.1):

```
mode=local                  no VERCEL_ENV, three flags ON, Google pair present   → every path 200, 0 "[env]" lines     (fails OPEN: the flags are accepted)
mode=preview-no-vercel-env  no VERCEL_ENV, the Preview scope's variables         → 500 for every page; first answer 500 at 204 ms
    - AUTH_GOOGLE_ID is required in production
    - AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET is required in production
    - AUTH_URL is required in production
mode=preview                VERCEL_ENV=preview, the same variables               → every path 200; "[env] contract enforced (VERCEL_ENV=preview)"
```

That pair makes a preview a free probe. A preview has no `AUTH_URL` and no
Google pair, so a hook that ran WITHOUT seeing `VERCEL_ENV` would refuse the
server. A preview whose `/api/health` is not 500 shows the variable reaches the
running server — provided the hook ran, which is what the line in the runtime
log shows. Both were observed on the first preview (§3.10).

**Vercel's documentation**
(`/docs/environment-variables/system-environment-variables`, page dated
2026-07-15, fetched 2026-10-06): "To enable these environment variables to your
deployments: … Select **Environment Variables** in the sidebar. Select the
**Enable access to System Environment Variables** checkbox." `VERCEL`: "An
indicator to show that system environment variables have been exposed to your
project's Deployments." `VERCEL_ENV`: "Available at: Both build and runtime …
The value can be either production, preview, or development."

**Now**: `vercel-build.sh` exits 1 without a known `VERCEL_ENV` (§3.4), so a
deployment that cannot see the variable fails its build instead of shipping
unguarded. The old test `leaves the flag alone when VERCEL_ENV is unset`
(expected status 0) is gone; three cases replace it.

**Not observed**: what Vercel hands a build or a function with the checkbox off;
whether unticking it affects deployments already running; the dashboard's own
label (only the documentation was read).

### 3.6 "A Vercel Custom Environment does not boot" was unfounded

The first version stated, in `lib/env.ts` and `docs/deploy.md`, that a Custom
Environment puts its name in `VERCEL_ENV` and so 500s every request, and advised
widening the enum to `z.string().min(1)`.

Vercel's documentation (same page as §3.5): `VERCEL_ENV` is "production,
preview, or development"; `VERCEL_TARGET_ENV`: "The system or custom environment
that the app is deployed and running on. The value can be either production,
preview, development, or the name of a custom environment." Custom environments
are "available on Enterprise and Pro plans" (`/docs/deployments/environments`).

Measured: the enum fails CLOSED (`VERCEL_ENV=staging` → refused, §3.4), and the
documented shape (`VERCEL_ENV=preview` + `VERCEL_TARGET_ENV=staging`) parses as
a preview. `tests/unit/db/env.test.ts` pins both
(`refuses a VERCEL_ENV it does not know: the enum fails closed`).

**Not observed**: what `VERCEL_ENV` holds on a real custom-environment
deployment — the project has Production and Preview only, and its plan was not
checked. The widening advice is removed everywhere: an unknown value read as
"not production" would drop the `AUTH_URL` and Google requirements.

### 3.7 The boot tier (`tests/boot/env-contract.test.ts`)

Four cases, one build, no build of its own:

1. `never serves a deployment that carries a test flag`
2. `still serves a file from a refused server, which is why the build refuses the hooks`
3. `boots a local production server with every test flag on`
4. `boots a deployment that satisfies the contract`

- **Runs** [I]: `npx vitest run --project boot` → 4 passed, 5.18 s (per case
  2.65 s, 0.37 s, 1.54 s, 0.51 s); the same inside
  `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1 NEXT_PUBLIC_TEST_HOOKS=1 BOOT_PORT=3917 bash scripts/ci/build.sh`
  (exit 0, 20–24 s warm). A database WAS reachable: `.env.test`'s
  `velo_atelier_test` on the shared local container, read-only (`SELECT 1`).
- **Without a database** [I] (`POSTGRES_URL*` pointed at a closed port):
  2 failed, 2 passed in about 4 s; cases 3 and 4 fail in 486 ms and 484 ms on
  `the first answer from /api/health was 503 {"ok":false,"db":false}`. Not a
  90 s wait, and not "twice": that sentence in `CLAUDE.md` was wrong before the
  change too ([R]: 515 ms and 498 ms on the old code — only a 500 was retried).
- **It tests the build on disk, not the working tree** [R]. With `getEnv();`
  replaced by `void getEnv;` in `instrumentation.ts` and no rebuild, the tier
  was 3/3 green; after a rebuild it failed.
  `tests/unit/deploy/instrumentation.test.ts` is what fails on the edit;
  `scripts/ci/build.sh` always builds first.
- **The `.env.local` trap** [R], reproduced twice in scratch checkouts (symlinks
  to the tree plus `cp .env.example .env.local`). Case 4 REMOVED the three flags
  from the child's environment; `next start` loads `.env.production.local`,
  `.env.local`, `.env.production` and `.env` and fills in every key the
  environment lacks (read in `node_modules/@next/env/dist/index.js`), so the
  "clean deployment" got all three back as `1` and was refused — after
  90 348 ms. Green in CI and in a fresh worktree, red beside the README's
  `.env.local`. [I], after the fix (flags pinned to `"0"`):
  `[flags removed] first /api/health status: 500 …` /
  `[flags zero] first /api/health status: 200; refusal lines: none; enforced line: true`.
- **The only link to a gate** is one line of `scripts/ci/build.sh`
  (`npx --no-install vitest run --project boot`): the unit job names its four
  projects, coverage only merges blobs, pre-push skips the build.
  `tests/unit/deploy/boot-tier-gate.test.ts` (3 cases) fails if the line is
  commented out, moved before the boot check, or followed by `||`.

### 3.8 A local production server: `npm run start`, `npm run lhci`

[D], `mode=readme-start` (no `VERCEL_ENV`, Google pair empty — the README's
`.env.local` shape):

```
first answer: 500 for /robots.txt at 205 ms after spawn
  /api/health 500 · /fr 500 · /fr/connexion 500 · /robots.txt 500 · /sitemap.xml 500 · /favicon.ico 500
  /tree-drawings.json 200 · /_next/static/chunks/<chunk> 200
"Ready in" printed: true; "Failed to prepare server" lines: 2; Ready before the failure line: true
  - AUTH_GOOGLE_ID is required in production
  - AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET is required in production
```

[R] measured the same from a real scratch checkout holding
`cp .env.example .env.local` and nothing but `PATH` and `HOME` in the
environment (`✓ Ready in 114ms`, then the refusal). `npm run dev` is
`NODE_ENV=development`: the pair is not required (§3.4's table).

`npm run lhci` was `lhci autorun`, whose `startServerCommand` is
`npm run start -- -p ${PORT}` and whose ready pattern is
`Ready in|started server on|Local:` (`lighthouserc.cjs`) — so from that
`.env.local` it would have audited a server answering 500. It is now
`bash scripts/ci/lighthouse.sh`, the script CI's `lighthouse` jobs already ran
(`ci.yml:362`, `perf.yml:198`; no workflow calls `npm run lhci`).

**Which database** [D]. Sourcing `scripts/ci/_lib.sh` in an `env -i` shell and
doing what `lighthouse.sh` does fills eight variables from `.env.test`
(`POSTGRES_URL`, `POSTGRES_URL_NON_POOLING`, `AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_TRUST_HOST`,
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET`, `BCRYPT_COST`,
each printed as `<NAME> <- .env.test (was unset)`), then:

```
database name: velo_atelier_test (both URLs), host localhost:5432; AUTH_URL=http://localhost:3100
the three test flags: unset (never read from the file); BCRYPT_COST=4
```

An exported variable beats every `.env*` file `next start` loads, so a local
`npm run lhci` audits a server on the `_test` database, not `.env.local`'s;
export `POSTGRES_URL` and `POSTGRES_URL_NON_POOLING` to point it elsewhere.
Arguments after `npm run lhci --` are no longer forwarded
(`npx --no-install lhci autorun` takes none in the script).

**Run since** [O]: `bash scripts/ci/lighthouse.sh` end to end, 8 URLs × 3 runs,
twice — 2026-10-06 20:11–20:21Z, and a second run started about 20:23Z. Each
time the server it started was audited on all eight URLs (§5.2 has the numbers
and the two assertions that failed, in both runs); `npm run lhci` itself exited
127 on this machine, for a reason unrelated to the contract (§5.1). The
wiring is pinned by `tests/unit/deploy/env-contract-defaults.test.ts` (8 cases,
three of them the wiring); [R] simulated the CI lighthouse job's environment and
saw 200s with the script's two lines and `AUTH_URL is required in production`
without them. The branch left one thing unverified: whether the plan's
Lighthouse command for production starts a local server. By the configuration's
own arithmetic it would (§5.4).

### 3.9 Prisma: the limiter's expected miss, counted, and where the marker is read

**Event shape** (read, `node_modules/@prisma/client/runtime/client.js`, 7.10.0):
`logEmitter.emit("error",{message:r.message,target:t.clientMethod,timestamp:new Date})`
inside `handleAndLogRequestError` — at the THROW site, with no error code. With
`"error"` or `{ emit: "stdout", level: "error" }` in `log`, Prisma registers its
own listener, which ends in `console.log("prisma:error", message)`: stdout, TWO
arguments, no `[prisma]` prefix.

**How many** [I], real PostgreSQL (`velo_atelier_w5fin_test`), an own client
that keeps its events, injected clock, identical under `NODE_ENV=production` and
`test`:

```
no row yet:                    events=2 targets=["authAttempt.update","authAttempt.update"] allExpected=true result={"ok":true,"retryAfterMs":0,"count":1}
window still open:             events=0
window still open (again):     events=0
window expired:                events=1 targets=["authAttempt.update"]
three fresh buckets (a first sign-in): events=6 … allExpected=true
```

So: **two P2025s per fresh key** (`bump()` and `reopen()` both miss —
`lib/security/rate-limit.ts:91` and `:99`), **one per expired window**, none for
an open window, **up to six on a first sign-in** (three buckets:
`lib/auth/authorize.ts:147-155`; "up to" because the per-IP limiter is the
allow-all one for a local address outside production). The first version wrote
"one per fresh key" and listed "a key whose window is still open" as a miss;
both were wrong. [R] counted the same on PGlite behind the real adapter.

**The two formats** [I] (no `errorFormat` option; Prisma picks `minimal` when
`NODE_ENV === "production"`, `colorless` otherwise), `↵` standing for a newline:

```
minimal:    ↵Invalid `prisma.authAttempt.update()` invocation:↵↵↵An operation failed because it depends on one or more records that were required but not found. No record was found for an update.
colorless:  ↵Invalid `db.authAttempt.update()` invocation in↵<abs path>/lib/security/rate-limit.ts:91:26↵↵  88 };↵  89 const bump = () =>↵  90   orNull(↵→ 91     db.authAttempt.update(↵An operation failed because … required but not found. No record was found for an update.
```

In both, the engine's sentence is the last line, with no trailing newline. The
`pretty` format was not observed.

**The latent hazard, and the fix** [I]. The marker was tested against the WHOLE
message. A real client on a refused port, the call placed under a comment
quoting the wording:

```
NODE_ENV=production … phrase anywhere=false | isExpectedNotFoundLog=false
NODE_ENV=development … phrase anywhere=true | isExpectedNotFoundLog=true
NODE_ENV=test … isExpectedNotFoundLog=true
```

A P1001 (database unreachable) on `authAttempt.update` read as the expected
miss, and was dropped, outside production. `lib/security/rate-limit.ts` has no
such comment (grep), so it was latent. `isExpectedNotFoundLog` now tests the
LAST NON-EMPTY LINE only (`engineSentence`); after: `false` in all three modes,
last line `Can't reach database server at 127.0.0.1:1`. The integration tier
runs with `NODE_ENV=test`, so it holds the framed format; production's frameless
one is held by the recorded messages in `tests/unit/db/log.test.ts`.

**The regression test that was missing** [R] → [I]. The first "stays silent"
test spied `console.error` and kept `[prisma]`-prefixed lines, so it could not
see Prisma's own `console.log("prisma:error", …)`: it passed on `main`'s
configuration with the noise line written. Replaced by
`writes nothing about the rate limiter's expected miss, on any console channel`
(`tests/integration/prisma-error-log.test.ts`): the real limiter through the
shared singleton on a fresh key, `console.log`, `info`, `warn` and `error`
recorded, all arguments joined, no prefix filter.

Mutations on `lib/db/prisma.ts` [I], each restored (`cmp` identical), against
the 3 tests of `tests/integration/prisma-error-log.test.ts`:

| Mutation                                                     | Result                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `log: ["error"]`, `$on` kept                                 | 1 failed — the new test, on two `prisma:error` lines (`:91:26`, `:99:26`)                                                            |
| `[{ emit: "event", … }, { emit: "stdout", level: "error" }]` | 1 failed — the new test, same two lines; unit 10/10, `tsc` 0 and ESLint 0 all stayed green                                           |
| `main`'s exact shape (`log: ["error"]`, no `$on`)            | 2 failed (both singleton tests)                                                                                                      |
| `client.$on("error", () => {})`                              | 1 failed — `prints a real failure` (`expected [] to have a length of 1`)                                                             |
| filter removed from `reportPrismaError`                      | 1 failed — the new test, on two `[prisma] authAttempt.update:` lines; the unit test `says nothing about the expected miss` fails too |

On `lib/db/log.ts`: whole-message match → unit 3 failed, 7 passed; no `trimEnd`
→ 1 failed, 9 passed; the file is at 100 % lines, statements, branches and
functions from its unit file alone. Kept from the first version, still true [R]:
with the listener's body replaced by `() => {}`, 43 real `[prisma]` lines
vanished from a CI run with all 389 integration tests green — which is why the
body is `reportPrismaError` (one call, unit-testable) and the wiring is pinned
separately. `lib/db/prisma.ts` differs from the branch's previous commit in
comments only [I].

**Two sharp edges, written down, not changed:**

- `event.message` is printed verbatim, and for a `PrismaClientValidationError`
  it includes the call's arguments. [R], canary values, production format:
  `user.create` with an unknown field → the line carries the e-mail and the hash
  (OLD 649 bytes on stdout, NEW 658 on stderr; the difference is the prefix).
  Not a regression; not to be forwarded unredacted to a third-party sink. The
  Auth.js `account.create` path is unreachable with `@auth/core` 0.41.3
  (`defaultAccount` whitelists seven fields, all in the schema).
- The listener belongs to the client, and the client survives module
  re-evaluation on `globalThis.__veloAtelierPrisma`. [I], after re-evaluating
  `lib/db/prisma.ts` and `lib/db/log.ts`:
  `same client=true listeners before=2 after=2 same listener functions=true`
  (2 = Prisma's own no-op + ours). An edit to `lib/db/log.ts` needs a dev-server
  restart. Not observed under `next dev` itself.

### 3.10 On Vercel: what has been observed, and what still has not

The pull request's own note listed six things nobody had seen on Vercel. Two
deployments have since answered some of them.

**Observed** [O], 2026-10-06.

Preview deployment `dpl_4MS8nEKgrqYoUoVVrkoWeD5wh17f` (PR #16, commit `d57a8dc`):

- build log: `▶ environment contract (VERCEL_ENV=preview)` →
  `check-env: environment contract satisfied (VERCEL_ENV=preview, isProduction=false).`
  → `▶ skipping prisma migrate deploy (VERCEL_ENV=preview)`;
- runtime log on the first request (cold start, `GET /api/health`, 17:56Z):
  `[env] contract enforced (VERCEL_ENV=preview)`;
- `/api/health` → 200 `{"ok":true,"db":true}`; `/fr/connexion` → 200.

Production deployment `dpl_8fUtvWonaB42YUxGCwha8aBsmn8F` (`main` @ `a754257`),
build log:

```
19:28:08Z  ▶ environment contract (VERCEL_ENV=production)
19:28:08Z  check-env: environment contract satisfied (VERCEL_ENV=production, isProduction=true).
19:28:08Z  ▶ prisma migrate deploy (VERCEL_ENV=production)
19:28:11Z  3 migrations found in prisma/migrations
19:28:13Z  No pending migrations to apply.
19:28:53Z  ✓ no test hooks — 0 hit(s)
19:28:54Z  Build Completed in /vercel/output [54s]
```

- runtime log, region `cdg1`, 19:29Z:
  `[env] contract enforced (VERCEL_ENV=production)` on the first request to each
  of `/api/health`, `/fr` and `/fr/connexion` (one line per function cold
  start);
- `/api/health` → 200 `{"ok":true,"db":true}`; `/fr`, `/en`, `/fr/connexion`,
  `/en/guides/check-brakes-disc`, `/fr/velo/demo`, `/robots.txt` → 200;
- no dashboard change was needed.

What those lines settle:

1. **The build is handed its scope's variables.** `check-env` parsed the Preview
   scope and the Production scope and was satisfied by both. Production's
   values, which nobody on the branch could see, pass the contract as written.
2. **`VERCEL_ENV` reaches the build and the running server**, with the project's
   settings as they are. A preview holds no Google pair, so a preview that boots
   shows `VERCEL_ENV` reaching the running server (§3.5).
3. **The `[env] contract enforced` line is in the runtime log, once per function
   cold start**: the deployed bundle contains the hook, and it ran (§3.3).

Also seen in the production runtime log, unrelated to the change: on each cold
start `pg` warns that the SSL modes `prefer` / `require` / `verify-ca` are
treated as `verify-full` today and will adopt weaker libpq semantics in
pg-connection-string v3 / pg v9; it suggests `sslmode=verify-full` explicitly.
The Neon strings in Vercel use `sslmode=require` (inferred from the warning; the
strings were not read).

**Still not observed anywhere:**

1. **A refused build** — a contract violation on Vercel — and so whether a
   failed build leaves the previous production deployment on the domain.
   Vercel's documentation (`/docs/deployments/environments`, page dated
   2026-09-17, fetched 2026-10-06): "When a production deployment succeeds,
   Vercel updates your production domains to point to the new deployment".
2. **What a refused DEPLOYMENT serves on Vercel.** Expected: static assets and
   prerendered routes come from the CDN without invoking a function, so a page
   may keep answering 200. In the build on disk **205 routes are prerendered**
   (`/fr`, `/en`, all 47 guides in both locales, each with its
   `opengraph-image`, …; none under `/connexion`, `/dev` or `/api`) and **3 are
   outside the proxy matcher** (`/favicon.ico`, `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml`) —
   counted [D] from `.next/prerender-manifest.json` against the matcher in
   `.next/server/functions-config-manifest.json`. Whether the proxy function
   turns the other 202 into 500s was not observed. `/api/health` — a route
   handler, `force-dynamic`, `no-store` — answering **500** is the diagnostic
   that does not depend on any of this.
3. **`VERCEL_ENV` with the system-variables setting off** (§3.5).
4. **A Custom Environment** (§3.6).
5. **Instant Rollback**, on this project. Vercel's documentation
   (`/docs/instant-rollback`, page dated 2026-07-07, fetched 2026-10-06):
   project overview → Production Deployment tile → **Instant Rollback**; "Hobby
   users can roll back to the immediately previous deployment"; "Vercel won't
   update environment variables if you change them in the project settings and
   will roll back to a previous build"; "After a rollback, Vercel turns off
   auto-assignment of production domains. This means new pushes to your
   production branch won't go live automatically" — undone by **Undo Rollback**
   on the production tile or by promoting a deployment.

### 3.11 Gates, tests, mutations, and the probe

- **Gates on the branch** [O] (tree identical to `main` @ `a754257`):
  `bash scripts/ci.sh` 12/12 in 149 s (3487 tests); boot tier 4/4; Playwright
  against a build made with the e2e flags — desktop-chromium 431 passed / 65
  skipped (4.0 min), mobile-chromium + no-webgl 874 passed / 59 skipped
  (6.5 min), mobile-landscape + mobile-narrow 548 passed / 24 skipped (7.6 min).
- **Counts** [D], on the branch:
  `npx vitest run --project unit tests/unit/db tests/unit/deploy` → 11 files,
  151 tests, 0 failed, 5.77 s; `tests/boot/env-contract.test.ts` 4;
  `tests/integration/prisma-error-log.test.ts` 3 ([I]: 3 passed, twice, 4.58 s
  and 4.76 s). `tests/unit/deploy/migrate-on-deploy.test.ts` has grown since
  (PR #17); none of these was re-run for this note.
- **Mutations** [I]: 25, each failing at least one test, every in-place file
  restored byte-identical — `lib/env.ts` (7, in memory), `instrumentation.ts`
  (4, in memory), `scripts/vercel-build.sh` (4), `scripts/check-env.ts` (2),
  `scripts/bundle-guard.ts` (1), `scripts/ci/build.sh` (2), the `lhci` wiring
  (2) and the BUILT hook chunk (3). The point is what survived before [R]: six
  of the seven `lib/env.ts` mutants passed all 35 tests that then existed, and
  with the bundle guard's deployment refusal made unreachable 187 files / 3 036
  tests stayed green. No coverage threshold, no `include` and no existing
  assertion was loosened ([R], [I]).
- **Where the rules live**, as merged in `a754257`: `CLAUDE.md` ("Contracts to
  respect": the **Prisma** entry and the **`lib/env.ts` is the environment
  contract, and two things run it** entry; "Quality gates": the `boot` project)
  and `docs/deploy.md` §2. This note is the evidence, not the rule.

**The started-server probe** (`<scratch>/slice-c/serve-probe.mjs <mode> 3000`),
so the numbers can be reproduced. Environment = `.env.test` parsed, then the
shell's, then the mode's overrides; `NODE_ENV` deleted (the CLI's production
default is the thing under test); `VERCEL_ENV` deleted unless the mode sets it.
It runs `node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next start -p <free port> -H 127.0.0.1`
from the repository root (no `.env.local` in that worktree). Four concurrent
clients loop over `/api/health`, `/fr`, `/fr/connexion`, `/robots.txt`,
`/sitemap.xml`, `/favicon.ico`, `/tree-drawings.json` and one chunk under
`/_next/static/chunks/` (the one containing `__va` when the build has it) from
the moment of spawn; a failed connection is counted and retried after 5 ms;
polling stops 3 s after the first HTTP answer. It prints names and rules, never
the value of a variable, and its only database access is `/api/health`'s
`SELECT 1`.

| Mode                    | Overrides on top of `.env.test`                                                             |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `refused`               | `VERCEL_ENV=production`, `ENABLE_TEST_PAGES=1`                                              |
| `deployment`            | `VERCEL_ENV=production`, the three flags `"0"`                                              |
| `preview`               | `VERCEL_ENV=preview`, `AUTH_URL`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET` empty, flags `"0"` |
| `local`                 | no `VERCEL_ENV`, the three flags `"1"`                                                      |
| `readme-start`          | no `VERCEL_ENV`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET` empty                               |
| `preview-no-vercel-env` | no `VERCEL_ENV`, `AUTH_URL` and the Google pair empty, flags `"0"`                          |

An empty string counts as "not set" for the contract (`blankToUndefined`) and as
DEFINED for Next's dotenv loader, which is why the probe blanks a variable
rather than removing it.
