## 4. Two workflows, and what their first version could not refuse (PR #17)

Two `docs/backlog.md` W5 entries — "Nothing in the repository reads
`renovate.json`" and "A Neon branch that is not `production` is never migrated"
— were closed by `.github/workflows/renovate-config.yml` +
`scripts/ci/renovate-config.sh` and `.github/workflows/migrate-preview.yml` +
`scripts/ci/migrate-preview.sh`. Both worked. The review then found that the
first version of each could be made to do nothing, or the wrong thing, with
every test green: the shape `.debug/016` is about, applied to the fix for
`.debug/016`.

**The review.** Four lenses, every finding re-checked. Three blockers, all fixed
(§4.1, §4.3, §4.4); about twenty should-fix items fixed; two more reviewers
attacked the fixes (nineteen smaller items, all applied).

Measurements below were made on `origin/main` + #16 + the squash of `w5/ci-ops`
(`b2c46c8`) + the review fixes; [I] also ran both scripts under bash 5.2.21 +
GNU sed 4.9 in a cached container with `--network none`. Every run used a
stand-in `npx` and fixture hosts under `.invalid` unless it says otherwise.

### 4.1 Nothing told production's connection string from preview's (blocker)

**First version** [R]. `scripts/ci/migrate-preview.sh` compared the secret's
host with nothing but `-pooler.` and a character class. Run with a clean
environment (`GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`, `GITHUB_REF=refs/heads/main`,
`PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS_ENABLED=1`), a DIRECT string on a production-shaped host,
and a stand-in `npx` that prints `No pending migrations to apply.` and exits 0:

```
:: prisma migrate deploy → ep-quiet-river…/neondb
Datasource "db": PostgreSQL database "neondb", schema "public" at "ep-quiet-river…"
:: preview branch migrated                                   exit=0
```

and the step summary read "`prisma migrate deploy` applied to the Neon `preview`
branch (`ep-quiet-river…/neondb`)". Green, labelled `preview`, "applied"
although nothing was pending. `docs/deploy.md` §4.5 said "Nothing in the
workflow can tell the two endpoints apart" — true of the code, and a choice: the
probe already computed the 14-character prefix, and `.debug/016` §3 already
publishes both prefixes and records that the by-hand procedure checked the host
first.

**Now** [I], re-run [D] with the same environment and
`NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT=ep-plain-block`:

```
:: NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL is not the preview endpoint — `preview` was NOT migrated.
:: Nothing was contacted.
::   the secret's host starts:  ep-quiet-river…
::   NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT says:  ep-plain-block
[exit=1]
```

The match rule, each case run [D] against a preview-shaped host:

| `NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT`  | Result                                                                                              |
| ------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ep-plain-block`         | accepted                                                                                            |
| `ep-plain-block-<id>`    | accepted (the whole first label)                                                                    |
| `EP-Plain-Block`         | accepted (both sides lower-cased)                                                                   |
| `ep`                     | refused — prints `… says:  ep`                                                                      |
| `ep-plain`               | refused — prints `… says:  ep-plain`                                                                |
| empty, secret present    | refused — `NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT is empty — refusing to migrate a database nothing has identified.` |
| `https://ep-plain-block` | refused — `NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT is not an endpoint name: letters, digits, '-' and '.' only,`       |

`ep` and `ep-plain` are the point of the `ep-<word>-<word>` condition on the `-`
continuation: without it a prefix both endpoints share would pass ([I]: the
mutation "`-` continuation from any value" fails one test).

### 4.2 The switch was read only when the secret was empty

**First version** [R]. `PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS_ENABLED` was inspected inside
`if [[ -z "$NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL" ]]`. The documented bootstrap sets the
secret and the variable in one sitting, so at the keyboard the secret is never
empty. With the secret present and the switch `true`, `0` or unset, each run
printed `:: prisma migrate deploy → …` and went on; `grep -c "is set to"` = 0.
Three sentences were false: the script's "fails now, while the maintainer is
still at the keyboard", `docs/deploy.md`'s "fails the job on the spot", and the
commit message's "a failure at the moment it is set". The forgotten variable was
the real hole: green now, and back to the green SKIP the day the secret
disappeared.

**Now**, every cell run [D] (`GITHUB_REF=refs/heads/main`):

| Secret  | Switch                             | Result                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------- | ---------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| absent  | unset                              | exit 0 — ``:: WARNING SKIP — NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL is not set — the Neon `preview` branch was NOT migrated.`` + the bootstrap, ending "never onto a command line." |
| absent  | unset, `NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT` set | exit 0 — the same SKIP (the endpoint variable alone arms nothing)                                                                                                    |
| absent  | `1`                                | exit 1 — ``PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS_ENABLED is 1 but NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL is empty — `preview` was NOT migrated.``                                                      |
| absent  | `true`                             | exit 1 — `PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS_ENABLED is set to 'true'; the only value that arms it is 1.`                                                                            |
| present | `true`                             | exit 1 — the same line (this is the cell that used to migrate)                                                                                                       |
| present | unset                              | exit 1 — ``NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL is set but PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS_ENABLED is not 1 — `preview` was NOT migrated.``                                                    |
| present | `1`                                | goes on to the endpoint assertion                                                                                                                                    |

The SKIP message used to end "Until then, migrate it by hand (§1)" — it sent the
reader to a command with the connection string inline. It now ends with the
dispatch.

### 4.3 The secret was job-level `env:` (blocker)

**First version** [R]. `NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL` sat in
`jobs.migrate-preview.env`, exported to `actions/checkout`,
`actions/setup-node` and `npm ci`. `package-lock.json` has 11 entries with
`hasInstallScript`, the root included ([R] twice, [I], [D]: two nested
`esbuild`, `@parcel/watcher`, `@prisma/engines`, `@swc/core`, `esbuild`,
`fsevents`, `msw`, `prisma`, `unrs-resolver`, plus the root's `postinstall` and
`prepare`). A test REQUIRED that placement: moving the entries to the step
failed "hands the script the secret AND the switch …", and a `uses:` step handed
the secret through `with:` passed all 51 tests.

**Now** [I]. The three names are on the `env:` of the one step that runs the
script; job `env:` is `HUSKY: "0"`; the checkout has
`persist-credentials: false`. Three tests hold it, the second by removing the
migrate step from the parsed workflow and asserting that what is left mentions
neither `secrets.` nor the secret's name. [I]'s mutations (secret also at job
level; at workflow level; on the install step; through `with:`; a fourth name on
the migrate step; `persist-credentials: true`; `npm ci --ignore-scripts`) each
fail one test. This is the first `secrets.*` reference in any workflow of the
repository ([R], [D]: `grep` finds only `migrate-preview.yml`).

**What it is not**: an isolation. The migrate step runs `prisma`, `dotenv`
(`prisma.config.ts` imports it) and `lib/db/env.ts` with the string in their
environment. And `--ignore-scripts` was NOT added to that `npm ci`: nobody
verified that `migrate deploy` finds its schema engine without
`@prisma/engines`' install script.

### 4.4 Two refusal tests could not tell a refusal from a warning (blocker)

[R], 88 mutations over the first version: 60 caught, 28 survived. The class that
mattered: with the `exit 1` deleted from the ref guard (its three log lines
kept), all 51 tests stayed green, and the mutated script — run by hand from
`refs/heads/w5/some-feature` with a direct `.invalid` string — printed the three
refusal lines and then `:: prisma migrate deploy → …`, Prisma's datasource line
and a P1001. The test fed the POOLED fixture, so the pooled guard supplied the
exit 1. Same for the pooled guard: only Prisma followed it, and Prisma also
exits 1. Both tests asserted `status === 1` plus a substring of the guard's own
message. A prefix-match guard (`!= refs/heads/main*`) survived too: one negative
ref was tried.

**The fix is a stand-in `npx` that answers 0** [I]: a guard that only warns then
ends in a green "migration", and `nothingMigrated()` also reads the stand-in's
own log, which a refusal leaves empty. Six negative refs (`w5/some-feature`,
`main-backup`, `main-x`, `refs/tags/main`, `refs/pull/14/merge`, empty) and a
positive control. [I]: 82 mutations over the two scripts, the two workflows and
the required-checks lists, each restored and compared byte for byte; 81 caught.
The one survivor is removing `2>/dev/null` from the probe alone, because the
probe now also catches its own exceptions (removing both IS caught).

**The trap for anyone writing such a test** [R], re-measured [I]. As measured —
before §5.1 changed that line — `scripts/ci/_lib.sh` ran `nvm use --silent 24`
whenever `$HOME/.nvm/nvm.sh` existed, and nvm PREPENDS its own `bin`. With the
real `HOME` and a stub-only `PATH`, `command -v npx` after sourcing `_lib.sh`
returned nvm's `npx`, ahead of the stub. For `renovate-config.sh` that is a unit
test downloading ~350 MB of Renovate. Spawn with `HOME` = an empty directory and
assert a marker the stand-in prints ([D] used that shape for every run in §4);
that advice does not depend on §5.1's change, and the trap was not re-measured
after it.

One fixture also sat on the real `aws.neon.tech` domain under the preview
endpoint's first two words; every fixture host is now under `.invalid`
(RFC 2606).

### 4.5 The URL probe, and what the log shows

**First version** [R]. The probe printed `url.pathname` unvalidated. A value
whose password holds an unencoded `@` followed later by `/` parses with the text
before that `/` as the authority: the script logged the rest of the password and
the whole real host, and Prisma repeated it as the database name — and, for a
password starting with digits then `/`, printed those digits as the port.
GitHub's masking matches the whole secret value, not a substring. Reachability
is low: it needs a password Neon's console does not generate.

**Now** [I]: exactly one raw `@`, a non-empty username, a hostname within
`[A-Za-z0-9.-]`, a path matching a bare database name, no `host` query key —
otherwise ONE generic line,
`:: NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL is not a connection URL with a host`. Run [D], each
exit 1 with only that line: a password with a raw `@`; with digits then a raw
`/`; with a raw `?`; with a raw `#`; a `psql '…'` paste; a `?host=` parameter. A
password with `%40 %2F %3F %23` and Neon's
`?sslmode=require&channel_binding=require` is ACCEPTED (the password is never
decoded). Across all of [D]'s runs the fake password and both fake endpoint ids
appeared 0 times in the output (`grep -c`).

**`?host=` is dialled instead of the URL's host** [I], Prisma 7.10.0:
`Datasource "db": … at "first-host.invalid"` then
`P1001: Can't reach database server at second-host.invalid:5432`. With one, the
endpoint asserted and the endpoint dialled would differ. `hostaddr` was tried
the same way and is ignored; `port` was not tried; neither is refused.

**What the log shows:**

- The cut is the first **14 characters** of the host, not "the first two words":
  two words only because both endpoints are `ep-` + 5 + 5 letters. [R]:
  `ep-shy-sun-<id>` logs `ep-shy-sun-a1b…`, `ep-delicate-butterfly-…` logs
  `ep-delicate-bu…`. [D]: `localhost` (9 characters) is printed whole.
- Prisma's output goes through `sed` (the full host; and the first DNS label on
  its own when longer than 14 characters) then `tee`. [R], against a loopback
  stand-in that only refuses connections: a server FATAL naming a bare endpoint
  id was printed verbatim by the first version, which redacted the full host
  only. Whether Neon's proxy words anything that way: not observed.
- The password did not appear on any path [R] could reach without a database:
  P1001, P1000 (three wordings), P1013 (twice), a server `XX000`, an unparseable
  value. The ROLE name does:
  ``P1000: Authentication failed against database server, the provided database credentials for `<role>` are not valid.``
  The old doc sentence "Prisma masks them, and a failed run says
  `(not available)`" was not reproduced by anyone as written; `(not available)`
  appears only when the server's message is not in PostgreSQL's stock wording.
- `pipefail` is load-bearing through both filters ([R]: `set +o pipefail` before
  the pipe exits 0 on a P1001; [I]: caught by two tests).

### 4.6 The success path, which nothing had executed

The first version's test file said "the happy path is still absent — it needs a
Neon branch". It needs an `npx` that exits 0, or a PostgreSQL.

[D], the unmodified script with the real Prisma 7.10.0 against the local
integration database (`velo_atelier_w5fin_test`, already migrated), outside
Actions, `NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT=localhost`:

```
:: prisma migrate deploy → localhost/velo_atelier_w5fin_test
Loaded Prisma config from prisma.config.ts.

Prisma schema loaded from prisma/schema.prisma.
Datasource "db": PostgreSQL database "velo_atelier_w5fin_test", schema "public" at "localhost:5432"

3 migrations found in prisma/migrations


No pending migrations to apply.
:: prisma migrate deploy exited 0 on localhost/velo_atelier_w5fin_test
[exit=0]
```

The summary file holds one sentence, then those same Prisma lines in a fenced
block:

```
`prisma migrate deploy` exited 0 on `localhost/velo_atelier_w5fin_test`, the endpoint `NEON_PREVIEW_ENDPOINT` names. What Prisma printed, host cut to its first 14 characters:
```

A failed migration writes no summary [D]: stand-in exiting 1 after a P1001 line
— exit 1, host redacted in the P1001 too, no summary file.

[R], the first version against an EMPTY throwaway local database (created and
dropped by the reviewer) — the lines that matter, in order:

```
2 migrations found in prisma/migrations
Applying migration `20260911071112_init`
Applying migration `20260921090547_checkup_symptoms_done_reason`
All migrations have been successfully applied.
```

exit 0; second run `No pending migrations to apply.` **So the dispatch IS the
bootstrap, and no by-hand migration is needed first.**

`N migrations found in prisma/migrations` counts the directories in the
checkout, not rows. [D]: on that database
`select migration_name, finished_at, rolled_back_at from "_prisma_migrations" order by started_at`
returns the three names, each finished, none rolled back; `count(*)` = 3. The
table's columns, read off it: `id`, `checksum`, `finished_at`, `migration_name`,
`logs`, `rolled_back_at`, `started_at`, `applied_steps_count`.

**Tests**, as counted [D] on that tree: `tests/unit/ci/required-checks.test.ts`
30 (was 24), `tests/unit/ci/renovate-config.test.ts` 11 (was 7),
`tests/unit/deploy/migrate-on-deploy.test.ts` 35 (was 20),
`tests/integration/migrate-preview.test.ts` 1 (new).
`npx vitest run --project unit tests/unit/ci tests/unit/deploy` — 8 files, 121
passed, 6.54 s; the integration file — 1 passed, 2.70 s; unit + security — 128
files, 2462 passed, 16.00 s; `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0. The merged files hold
more (on `863cd0c` a `grep` finds 2 `it(` in the integration file and 40 in
`migrate-on-deploy.test.ts`); they were not re-run or re-counted for this note. On the branch as merged [O]: `bash scripts/ci.sh` 12/12 in 151 s,
3545 tests (tree identical to `main` @ `863cd0c`).

### 4.7 The Renovate validator

- **What it replaces.** `.debug/016` §4 gave
  `npx --yes --package renovate -- renovate-config-validator renovate.json`:
  unpinned, and — handed a filename — validating it as a GLOBAL config, a wider
  schema than the one the service applies (`scripts/ci/renovate-config.sh`'s
  header has the measurement). The script pins `renovate@44.108.1` and passes
  `--no-global`.
- **The bump of the pin never ran the job** [R]. Both triggers filtered on
  `renovate.json` alone; Renovate's bump of `RENOVATE_VERSION` edits
  `scripts/ci/renovate-config.sh` and nothing else. The script's comment and
  `docs/deploy.md` §4.4 said "validated by this very job", and a test pinned the
  path list to `["renovate.json"]`. Now three paths on both triggers
  (`renovate.json`, the script, the workflow file), still path-filtered, still
  out of `REQUIRED_CHECKS`.
- **Its verdict could be thrown away** [R]: `|| true` after the validator, an
  `if !` around it, `continue-on-error` on the job or the step, and `if: false`
  on the job each left 51/51 green. Now the script is executed against a
  stand-in `npx` exiting 1 and 0, and the workflow text may hold neither
  `continue-on-error` nor an `if:` key.
- **Timing.** "Takes several minutes even warm" was false. Warm npx cache: 3 s
  and 1 s [R], 5.40 s [I], 4 s [D] (`exit=0`, 560 lines of output); the cached
  tree is 348M [R]. On a GitHub runner [O]: the job's first run, on PR #17,
  passed in 41 s, and its second, on the push to `main`, in 44 s (§4.11).
- **Output on the committed file** [D], 2026-10-06: line 1
  `:: renovate-config-validator --no-global renovate.json (renovate@44.108.1)`,
  line 2 `INFO: Validating renovate.json as repo config`, line 3
  `WARN: Config migration necessary`, line 339 `WARN: Config migration diff:`,
  line 559 `INFO: Config validated successfully against 1 file(s)`, line 560
  `:: renovate.json is valid Renovate configuration`.
- **Blind spot** [R], from reading 44.108.1's `dist/config/validation.js`, not
  from a run: top-level `extends` is not resolved (`resolveConfigPresets` is
  called for `packageRules` entries only), so a misspelt preset should pass here
  and stop the service.
- **Cadence** [R], inferred, not observed: the registry shows 13, 11 and 14
  `renovate` releases on 2026-09-29, -30 and 10-01; with `minimumReleaseAge`
  7 days and Renovate's default `internalChecksFilter: strict` a different
  version clears the hold at every Monday run; the patch-automerge rule needs
  `matchDepTypes`, which a regex-manager dependency does not carry. So: a
  pin-bump PR about weekly, not automerged, running the 20 required contexts
  plus this job's install, holding one of `prConcurrentLimit` 5. No
  `packageRules` entry sets the cadence.
- Not second-verified (a P3 with no verdict): the pin fixes `renovate` and its
  123 exact direct dependencies; their own dependencies are ranges resolved at
  install time (the cached tree held 615 packages).

### 4.8 Concurrency, the timeout and Neon — documentation, not observation

**Concurrency.** Per GitHub's documentation ([R], fetched 2026-10-06) a
concurrency group holds one run in progress and one pending, and a newly queued
run replaces the pending one. So `cancel-in-progress: false` means never
cancelled in flight and never two at once, NOT "every later migration queues".
Harmless between pushes to `main`; a mistaken `--ref <branch>` dispatch can
displace a pending `main` run and is then refused by the script. `queue: max`
exists in the same documentation and was not adopted: nothing here could
validate it short of a real dispatch. The first version asserted the block
nowhere (`cancel-in-progress: true`, the block deleted, a per-run group: 51/51
green); it is now a `toEqual` on the parsed value.

**The timeout.** `timeout-minutes: 10` bounds a hung connection or DDL. It does
not bound Prisma's advisory-lock wait: the pinned engine carries "Timed out
trying to acquire a postgres advisory lock (SELECT
pg_advisory_lock(72707369)). Timeout:" ([R], [I]: `strings` on the schema
engine; the length was not checked). The same engine carries "migrate found
failed migrations in the target database, new migrations will not be applied" —
a run killed mid-migration may need `prisma migrate resolve`. Not reproduced.

**Neon** ([R], Neon's documentation fetched 2026-10-06, paraphrased). A reset
from parent replaces the child's data AND schema with the parent's; the branch's
connection details do not change. A child branch's roles have the parent's
passwords by default; a child's own passwords are preserved across a reset only
from a PROTECTED parent, and protected branches are a paid-plan feature. The
project is on the Free plan (`docs/deploy.md`, "What W5 did"). Consequences
written into `docs/deploy.md` §1 and §4.5, each marked there:

1. "Resetting `preview` from its parent is still the one-click equivalent of a
   migration" was wrong: it copies production's rows into the database every
   pull request's preview reads.
2. After a reset the rotated `preview` password is EXPECTED to revert to
   production's (inferred from the two pages read together; not observed). Order
   afterwards: re-rotate, GitHub secret, both Vercel scopes, redeploy previews,
   dispatch.
3. Before the rotation the preview string carries production's password — and
   `.debug/016` §3 records both endpoints being queried by hand in the W5
   session. Rotating `preview` does not change `production`'s password. Whether
   to rotate production's is the maintainer's decision; it was not checked
   against the live project and nothing here did it.
4. Not observed, per Neon's Prisma guide as summarised to a reviewer: an idle
   compute can outlast Prisma's connect timeout, so a first run red on P1001 may
   be a cold start.

### 4.9 The by-hand fallback

`docs/deploy.md` §1 used to open with `migrate deploy` typed by hand, the string
inline. The only by-hand form left is in its §4.5: `read -rs` into the variable,
`export`, the SCRIPT (so the endpoint assertion, the pooled check and the
redaction still apply), `unset`, then a rotation. [D] ran those four lines in
zsh 5.9 and bash 3.2.57 against the local integration database, the string fed
on standard input: exit 0 and the variable empty afterwards in both. `read -s`
at a real terminal was not exercised. Outside Actions the ref guard says nothing
([R]: run from a feature branch with no `GITHUB_*`, the first version went
straight to `migrate deploy`), so the fallback says "from a clean, up-to-date
checkout of `main`".

`gh secret set NEON_PREVIEW_DIRECT_URL` with no `--body` prompts for the value —
per `gh`'s manual page (2.89.0), read, not run: no agent ran `gh secret`,
`gh variable` or `gh workflow run`.

- A GitHub Environment restricted to `main` for the secret. "Main only" is today

  job-summary line.

  not exist is caught after the fact (the script exits 1 on "Prisma CREATED the

  characters rather than on `ep-<word>-<word>`.

  documentation, not observed).

  (expected roughly weekly, not observed).

- The `.nvmrc` assertion does not cover the two new workflows.
- A pull request that adds a migration previews against the old schema until it
  merges — inherent to one shared `preview` branch; the real fix is the per-PR
  Neon branches entry (issue #24).
- The `customManagers[].fileMatch` → `managerFilePatterns` rename (two entries

## 5. What the launch checklist surfaced on the way

`docs/deploy.md` §5, walked by [O] on 2026-10-06 from 20:02Z, on #17's branch
head (`2a311e5`), whose tree is the one merged as `863cd0c` at 20:21:34Z — and,
for its §5.2 item 1, from a clean clone of `863cd0c` on 2026-10-07 [W] (§5.6).

### 5.1 `npm run lhci` and `npm run ci:local` lose node under nvm, on this machine

**Symptom.** Both exit 127, `npx: command not found`.

**Root cause.** Under `npm run`, npm exports `npm_config_prefix`.
`scripts/ci/_lib.sh` sourced `~/.nvm/nvm.sh` whenever the file existed; nvm
prints

```
nvm is not compatible with the "npm_config_prefix" environment variable
```

and removes node from `PATH` (this machine's nvm directory is a symlink to
another volume). Calling the scripts with `bash` directly works — and that is
how CI and the git hooks call them, which is why nothing had shown it.
`ci:local` was already affected before 2026-10-06; `lhci` became affected when
PR #16 routed it through the script (§3.8).

**Fix**, in the close-out change that carries this note: `scripts/ci/_lib.sh` no
longer sources nvm when a Node of the right major is already on `PATH` — which
is every `npm run` started on Node 24, since npm is running on that Node. It
reads the major of the `node` it finds and reaches for nvm only when there is
none or it is not 24: under a Node of another major it still sources nvm, and
the old failure is unchanged there. When it does source nvm it passes
`--no-use` and then runs `nvm use --silent 24`, so `nvm.sh` no longer inherits
the step script's positional parameters.

`tests/unit/deploy/lib-nvm.test.ts` (four cases, a stand-in `nvm.sh` in a
sandbox `HOME`) executes both directions: the right Node on `PATH` → nvm not
sourced and node still reachable; no Node → sourced; another major → sourced;
sourced with `--no-use` and never the caller's arguments. Each of two mutations
(always source; drop `--no-use`) fails one case.

**Re-run after the fix** [O], on the machine that showed it (between the
evening of 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07; the time was not recorded):
`STEPS="nvmrc" npm run ci:local` → `✓ PASS nvmrc` / `:: ci:local passed`.
Before the fix the same command died with `npx: command not found`. Then the
whole of it, 2026-10-07 (about 14:10Z), on the tree of this change:
`npm run ci:local` → **12/12 PASS in 190 s** (3552 tests; the `boot` tier 4/4),
through npm from the first step to the last. And `npm run lhci`, 2026-10-07
14:15Z–14:25Z: it ran end to end through npm — no `npx: command not found` —
and exited 1 on its own verdict, the two bike-page assertions of §5.2 (run 3
there).

### 5.4 Production was audited with Lighthouse without starting a local server

**Why the plan's invocation would have started one.** §9.5's
`npx lhci autorun --collect.url=https://<prod-domain>/fr` (the form
`docs/deploy.md` §5.2 used to quote) keeps `lighthouserc.cjs`'s
`startServerCommand`, `npm run start -- -p ${PORT}`: `-p 3100` as written, and
`-p 80` with `LHCI_BASE_URL=https://…` in front, because the file derives
`PORT` from that variable — `new URL(BASE_URL).port || "80"`. Either way lhci
would start a local server on the laptop before auditing anything. Read from
`lighthouserc.cjs` (lines 40 and 190); neither form was run.

**What was done instead.** A scratch `lighthouserc.cjs` that `require`s the
repository's and drops `startServerCommand`, with
`npx lhci collect --config=<that file>`: the repository's own collect settings
(applied throttling, mobile form factor), three runs per URL, 20:06Z,
Lighthouse 12.6.1, Chrome 154. Verified that no local server was started: no
`next` process, no "server" line in the log.

### 5.6 A clean clone: `npm run dev` could not serve the checkup, and the guest import drops it (2026-10-07)

`docs/deploy.md` §5.2 item 1: the README's quick start from a clean clone, then
the plan's §9.1 walk. Delegated by [O] and driven in a browser [W], on
2026-10-07.

**Setup.** `git clone --depth 1` of `863cd0c` into a scratch directory;
`npm ci` (exit 0, 52 s, 1697 packages); `cp .env.example .env.local`.

**One deliberate deviation from the README.** `npm run db:setup` was NOT run:
it runs `docker compose up`, and the project's Postgres container was already
running, and shared. Instead a dedicated database, `velo_atelier_walk`, was
created on that server and only the database name was changed in `.env.local`;
then `npx prisma migrate deploy` (3 migrations applied: `20260911071112_init`,
`20260921090547_checkup_symptoms_done_reason`,
`20260930094543_one_open_build_list_per_bike`) and `npm run db:seed`
(`✓ seed complete — users=2 bikes=4 checkups=1 lists=1`). The dev server
started clean (`✓ Ready in 425ms`, `GET /fr 200`). So `npm run db:setup`
itself was not exercised by this walk.

#### Finding 1 — the README's quick start left the checkup route broken (fixed)

**Symptom.** The first checkup page,
`GET /fr/velo/<id>/controle?parts=chain%2Cbrake-caliper-rear`, answered 500:
`Module not found: Can't resolve '@/lib/content/generated/reason-keys'` (also
`…/slugs` and `…/version`). After that one request the Turbopack dev server
answered 500 on EVERY route probed — home, the guides, the bike pages,
`/fr/mes-velos`, … — until `npm run content:generate` was run in the clone; it
then recovered without a restart. The walk's console and server logs hold 83
"Module not found" and 19 responses 500, all from this episode.

**Root cause.** `npm run dev` was `npm run drawings && next dev`.
`lib/content/generated/` is gitignored, the checkup route imports it, and
nothing in the quick start generated it. `npm run build` does — so on a
checkout that had been built the tree was simply there, and nobody had run the
other case. It is the rule `CLAUDE.md` already records for CI ("Every CI job
is a fresh checkout, so a job that compiles or runs code which imports one of
these must generate it first"), one step earlier: the first command a
contributor runs. Production builds are unaffected.

**Fix**, in the close-out change that carries this note: `npm run dev` is now
`npm run drawings && npm run content:generate && next dev`. The added step
takes about 1.2 s.

**How to detect a regression.** `tests/unit/deploy/dev-script.test.ts`
(3 cases) pins that `dev` and `build` both run `content:generate` before
`next`; it fails on the old script.

#### Finding 2 — the guest import dropped the guest's checkup (ruled on 2026-10-07, fixed by 2026-10-08, the fix repaired after review on 2026-10-09; not yet run on production)

**Symptom.** After the walk's import (`/fr/import`, B4 below) the database held
NO `Checkup` row for the imported bike; its `BuildList.checkupId` was NULL;
its 3 list lines had no `checkupItemId`; and all 36 of its `BikePartState`
rows were `UNKNOWN`. The bike and the list ARE imported: the bike is listed in
"Mes vélos" and its list has the same 3 lines.

**Root cause**, read in the source, in two files:

- `lib/guest/schema.ts` — `StoredCheckupSchema` REQUIRES `steps`, and its
  comment says "A state that does not parse is skipped";
- `lib/checkup/storage.ts` — the wizard stores `toStored(state)`, which since
  W3 deliberately has no `steps`.

So the checkup the wizard really leaves in the browser does not parse, and is
skipped.

**Why the tests are green.** Both fixtures hand-write a whole `CheckupState`,
`steps` included, under that key — `lib/guest/schema.test.ts` and
`tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts` — and the e2e spec asserts nothing about a
`Checkup` row. The case that had been run is a shape the wizard does not
write.

**What makes it permanent** — read in the source, not observed by the walk:
`app/[locale]/(protected)/import/GuestImport.tsx` calls `clearGuestState()`
after a successful import, and `lib/guest/schema.ts` removes every guest key,
`va:checkup:local` included. So the checkup the importer skipped is then
deleted from the browser too: it cannot be imported later.

(The four paragraphs above are as they were written on 2026-10-07, before the
ruling: they describe the code as it was found.)

**Status.** FIXED [I], in the change that carries this paragraph, after the
maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-07: fix before the tag. The change was written
over two sessions, 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08, and every measurement in "What
the fix is", "Choices made in the fix" and "An unfinished PARTIAL checkup" was
taken by the second, on 2026-10-08. **That version was then reviewed by two
adversarial reviewers, on 2026-10-09, who returned ten findings — nine distinct
defects, two of which lost a guest's answers again.** They were repaired the
same day; "Found in review" below records each one, what was measured, and
which of the earlier paragraphs it corrects. Where an earlier paragraph and that section disagree,
that section is the code. **Not deployed, and not re-run on production, when
this was written**: Journey B's import has been run again only by
`tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts` — a production build served locally against a
`_test` database — never against `velo-atelier.vercel.app`. Until the change is
deployed, production still drops the checkup.

**What the fix is.**

- **One definition of the stored shape.** `lib/guest/schema.ts` no longer has
  a schema of its own for `va:checkup:local`: it reads the key through
  `peekStoredCheckup` (`lib/checkup/storage.ts`), i.e. `StoredCheckupSchema`,
  the wizard's. That read removes NOTHING — `readStoredCheckup`, the wizard's
  own, removes a value it cannot parse, which here would be the same data loss
  by another road.
- **The payload carries answers, not items.** A checkup in the `GuestState`
  contract is its `guestKey`, its scope with `partIds`, and `answers` /
  `symptoms` / `notes` keyed by step key, `startedAt`, `completedAt` — no
  step, and no part or guide per answer. `strictObject` at every level, each
  id matched by pattern at its column's length, a cap on every array and on
  each of the three records. `GUEST_STATE_VERSION` went from 1 to **2**, and
  that is not bookkeeping: the old collector sends `checkups: []` — a payload
  BOTH shapes accept — and then clears the key. Refusing version 1 makes a
  payload from it a `VALIDATION` that writes nothing and keeps every key.
  (Whether a tab loaded before a deploy can still reach the new action was not
  observed; this is the wall for the case where it can.)
- **The server re-plans** (plan §5.4). `importGuestState` derives the bike as
  before, plans it with `planForBuild` (`lib/content/checkup-plan.ts` — the
  plan `controle/actions.ts` now uses too, extracted from its `planFor`), and
  accepts the payload with `fromStored` / `reconcile`: an answer whose key is
  not in the plan is dropped, and so is a symptom the step does not offer. The
  rows are `answeredItems(state)` (`lib/checkup/items.ts`, extracted from
  `writeItems`, which uses it too). A checkup none of whose answers survives
  writes no row. (**Which plan**: the version of 2026-10-08 planned the SCOPE
  the payload named. Since 2026-10-09 it is the bike's FULL plan — "Found in
  review", R1.)
- **A COMPLETED checkup links and writes part states** (plan §4.2 b): the
  imported `BuildList.checkupId`; `checkupItemId` on every line whose
  `(action, partId)` `deriveBuildList(state)` derives; KO → `BROKEN`, OK →
  `OK` + `lastServicedAt`, SKIPPED → unchanged, on the part each item names.
- **One transaction per bike**, with the row ids the links need generated in
  Node. Before, the bike and everything under it were one nested `create`;
  the links need a list to point at a checkup created beside it, so the
  writes are now `$transaction([bike, …checkups, …lists])`.
- **The answer says what became of each checkup, and the browser clears on
  that answer only.** `GuestImportSummary.bikes[].checkups[]` is `imported`,
  `already-imported`, or `skipped` with a reason (`nothing-kept`,
  `bike-already-imported`, and since 2026-10-09 `other-bike`);
  `settleGuestImport` names the keys to forget — `va:checkup:local` only for
  the first two, or when there was nothing to import — and `clearGuestKeys`
  removes exactly those. `clearGuestState()` is gone. A checkup that stays is
  said to the visitor (`account.import.checkupKept.*`, FR and EN). (**What
  `already-imported` means** was the checkup's id until 2026-10-09 and is its
  content since — "Found in review", R2.)

**Choices made in the fix — decisions, not findings.**

- _An unfinished checkup links nothing._ Its row and items are imported; no
  `BuildList.checkupId`, no `checkupItemId`, no part state. Plan §4.2 b puts
  both under the COMPLETE action, and `saveCheckupAction` leaves an account's
  unfinished checkup the same way. Its `startedAt` is kept to the millisecond,
  so `existingCheckup` recognises the run: a FULL checkup resumed and finished
  on the account is the same `Checkup` row, and `finishCheckupAction` links
  the list then. (A PARTIAL one: see the paragraph after this list.)
- _`COMPLETED` if and only if the payload carries `completedAt`_ — and at
  least one of its answers survived the re-plan. `reconcile` also drops the
  date whenever today's plan asks a question the checkup did not answer; the
  import does not follow it there. A checkup finished before the corpus grew
  a question is still a finished checkup — for the wizard, which never
  resumes a stored checkup that has a `completedAt` (`isFinished`,
  `components/checkup/Wizard.tsx`), and for an account's own `COMPLETED` rows
  — and reopening it would hand a finished checkup to the resume path. The
  date is all the payload decides: the rows, the part states and the links are
  computed from the plan, for the answered questions only.
  `finishCheckupAction` is no stricter: it does not check that every question
  has an answer either (read in `controle/actions.ts`). The first session's
  draft of this fix took `reconcile`'s verdict instead; it was changed before
  anything was committed.
- _One part per item._ When an answered step reports on several parts (17 of
  the 34 steps of a full checkup on the gravel preset do — counted, [I]), the
  status goes to the part its `CheckupItem` row names — the step's first (since
  2026-10-22, for a `PARTIAL` checkup, the first INSIDE its scope: §5.11) — and
  to no other: on that bike a full checkup reports on 24 parts and its rows
  name 18. Rule (b) maps an ITEM's result to a part state, and a row names one
  part; the import before this change did the same, with the part id the
  browser had resolved, which was also the step's first. Within one checkup a
  KO is never undone by an OK on the same part; before, the last answer won.
- _The import is NOT aligned with `finishCheckupAction`_, which writes a KO as
  `ATTENTION`, host-expanded through `statusByPart` (O3 below). That
  difference is a separate ruling; neither action was changed —
  `saveCheckupAction` and `finishCheckupAction` build the same rows and the
  same plan as before, through the two extracted functions.
- _The planner is injected, not imported._ `lib/guest/import.ts` takes
  `{ plan }` the way it takes the database: `planForBuild` reaches the
  compiled corpus (`content-collections`, a generated tree), and the module
  stays plain Node. The action passes the real one; the unit tier passes the
  same `planCheckup` over the guides on disk.

**An unfinished PARTIAL checkup: what the import writes, and what the account
then does with it** — measured [I], 2026-10-08, on PostgreSQL, by replaying the
wizard's own calls (`loadStoredCheckup`, `fromStored`, the reducer, `toStored`,
`saveCheckupAction`) in a scratch test that is not in the repository; not
driven in a browser. For a guest checkup scoped to the chain and the cassette
with one of its two questions answered, the import wrote one `Checkup`
(`PARTIAL`, `IN_PROGRESS`, its `startedAt` to the millisecond, its user-scoped
`guestKey`) and one `CheckupItem` (the chain's KO, with its symptom); no list
link, no part state. The two part ids are NOT written: `Checkup.scope` is
`FULL | PARTIAL` and there is no column for the set. On the account, the
bike page's loader then handed the banner a run to resume (`PARTIAL`, 1
answered) — `ResumeBanner` puts no `?parts=` in a saved bike's link, read in
the component — and `loadStoredCheckup` answered scope
`{ kind: "parts", partIds: [] }`. So the page plans the whole bike (read in
`controle/page.tsx`; 34 questions when replayed), the wizard keeps the imported
answer and that empty scope, and its first autosave posts the empty scope: the server
plans no question for it, keeps no answer, and deletes the run's stored items
— the same row, still `IN_PROGRESS`, with 0 items. **This is the account's own
path for any partial run** (`controle/load.ts`, `saveCheckupAction`); it
predates this change, the maintainer has not ruled on it, and this change
neither alters it nor depends on it. (2026-10-22: ruled on and fixed — §5.10.
An imported partial run resumed through the banner now carries on as a full
checkup, the same row, every item kept.) No test asserts it: the one that holds
"resumed and finished as the same row" imports a FULL checkup. It is recorded
because the import is now a second way into that path. Re-measured on
2026-10-09 after the repairs, by the two reviewers' scratch tests run against
the repaired code (`loadStoredCheckup`, `fromStored`, `saveCheckupAction` on
PostgreSQL): the same — `PARTIAL`, `IN_PROGRESS`, one item written by the
import; scope `{ kind: "parts", partIds: [] }` handed back; 0 items after one
save, same row. One thing changed on the way INTO that path: an unfinished run
whose answers go beyond the parts it names is now written `FULL` (R1), and a
`FULL` run does not take it.

**Found while fixing.**

- zod 4.5.4 SKIPS an own `__proto__` key of a `z.record` instead of failing
  the parse (it does refuse one on a `strictObject`). So such a key in a
  checkup's `answers` is not a `VALIDATION`: it is gone before the plan is
  consulted. `tests/security/guest-import.test.ts` pins what follows — no row,
  and the real answers beside it kept. (`tests/integration/build-lists.test.ts`
  already pinned the same behaviour for a list line's `refinement`.)
- The old test "reports what the winner wrote when two devices race" seeded
  the bike and then imported, so what it exercised was the ordinary "already
  there" read, not the P2002 branch its name describes (read in the test; no
  coverage figure was taken for that branch). The branch is now driven with a
  store whose first ownership read misses, and by three concurrent imports on
  PostgreSQL — where 2 of the 3 answered `already-imported` and one bike, one
  checkup and one list were left.
- A list line's `refinement` is a record with no cap on its entries (a
  40-character key pattern, 120-character values, and the 256 KB budget are
  what bound it). Not changed on 2026-10-08; capped at 24 on 2026-10-09
  ("Found in review", R7).
- `/import` has no `catch` around the action call, before this change as
  after: an import that THROWS (the transaction refused by Postgres, a network
  failure) leaves the page on its spinner. Every key is kept, because clearing
  comes after the answer; the visitor is told nothing. Read in
  `GuestImport.tsx`, not changed here — and since observed in a browser, on
  2026-10-09: with the action's response dropped, the page stayed on "Import
  de votre vélo en cours…" with no error and no retry (the reviewer's run, and
  the same interception in `tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts`, which does not
  assert on the spinner). Two of the ways to get there are closed by R3: a
  U+0000 or a year-0 date no longer reaches the transaction. A third by V1,
  below: half a surrogate pair.

**What happens to a `va:checkup:local` that was kept** — read in the source
[I], except the first line, which the e2e spec observes in a browser:

- it is still there after the import and after a visit to `/mes-velos`; the
  guest banner follows `va:bike:local` alone, so it does not offer the import
  again;
- nothing on a saved bike's pages reads it (`useStoredCheckup(null)` for a
  `db` bike);
- an UNREADABLE value is removed the next time the WIZARD's read runs on that
  key — `readStoredCheckup`, through the resume banner of `/velo/local` or the
  wizard of `/velo/local/controle`, which both need a guest bike to be there
  again. That is the wizard's rule and was not changed; the import no longer
  does it;
- a READABLE one that the server skipped waits for the next guest bike built
  in that browser. Unfinished, the wizard resumes it against the new bike's
  plan (`restore` → `fromStored`). Finished, the wizard treats it as history
  and overwrites it at the first save of a new run. Either way, an import
  made while it is still under the key collects it with the NEW bike, and the
  server re-plans it against that bike — keeping the answers that bike's plan
  asks, or, if the account by then holds a row under its key on another bike,
  writing nothing for it and reporting `skipped: 'other-bike'`: it stays in
  the browser again (until 2026-10-09 that case was reported
  `already-imported`, and cleared — R2).
  The to-fix list has always travelled the same way: replacing the guest bike
  never cleared `va:buildlist:local` either.

**Found in review (2026-10-09), and repaired the same day.** Two reviewers
attacked the version of 2026-10-08 — one in a real browser and on PostgreSQL,
one with mutations and probes of the walls. Each finding below was reproduced
first, by the repairer [I], with the reviewer's own scratch test against commit
`e541e35`, then repaired, then held by a test that fails without the repair.
**[R]** marks a number taken from a reviewer's run and not re-measured.

- **R1 — the stored scope is not the scope the answers were given under
  (blocker; data loss).** The wizard resumes an unfinished run against the plan
  of the URL — `restore` → `fromStored(stored, props.steps, …)` in
  `components/checkup/Wizard.tsx` — and `fromStored` keeps `stored.scope`;
  `toStored` writes it back. So a guest who taps "check this part" on the
  chain, answers one question, leaves, and later opens "Contrôle complet"
  answers the other 33 under a header that says full checkup, and the run is
  stored with 34 answers and `scope: { kind: "parts", partIds: ["chain"] }`.
  The import planned the scope the payload named and kept the answers in THAT
  plan. Measured [I] on PostgreSQL, replaying the wizard's calls: 1
  `CheckupItem` for 34 answers, the checkup reported `imported`, all three
  keys cleared, 2 of the 3 list lines unlinked, one part state. In a browser
  [R], with the real wizard and the workspace's own link: the same, "answers
  given: 34; in the database: 1; in neither place: 33". The variant — a second
  "check this part" on the front caliper instead of the full link — stores
  three brake answers under `parts[chain]` (the wizard's `reconcile` drops the
  chain's): the import reported `nothing-kept`, wrote no checkup, told the
  visitor none of the answers matched a question asked for this bike (all
  three are), and imported the bike — after which the kept checkup can never
  be imported. **Repair**: the answers are accepted against the bike's FULL
  plan, whatever scope the payload names. It asks every question any scope of
  the same bike asks (checked on the seven presets, part by part: 0 keys of a
  scoped plan outside the full one, over more than 200 scopes) and is the
  server's own computation just as much: no step comes from the payload. The
  named scope decides one thing only, the `Checkup.scope` column — `PARTIAL`
  while every kept answer is a question that scope asks, `FULL` as soon as one
  is not. After the repair [I]: 34 `CheckupItem`s of 34, the three lines
  linked, the part states of the whole checkup; the brake variant imported,
  `FULL`, its lines linked. The wizard was NOT changed: browsers already hold
  such states, and its resume is not this change's. `nothing-kept` now means
  what its sentence says — no answer is a question this bike is asked.
  A consequence, measured: an unfinished run whose answers went beyond its
  parts is written `FULL`, and the account then resumes it over the full plan
  as the same row with no answer lost (`tests/integration/guest-import.test.ts`).
- **R2 — `already-imported` was decided by the checkup's id alone (major; data
  loss behind a lost response).** The browser forgets `va:checkup:local` on
  that word. Two sequences made it false. (a) The first import is written by
  the server but its response never reaches the page; every key stays; the
  guest carries on with the SAME run, answers more, creates the list; the next
  `/import` finds the bike, writes nothing (§6.8 AC8) — and reported the
  checkup `already-imported` because a row had its key. Measured [I] on
  PostgreSQL: the account kept `IN_PROGRESS` with 1 item, the browser was told
  to clear all three keys, and the cassette's answer, the completion and the
  list were in neither place; in a browser [R], with the response dropped by
  `page.route`: keys `[null, null, null]`, no sentence. (b) A NEW guest bike
  carrying a run the account holds under another bike: imported without the
  checkup (the key is unique), reported `already-imported`, key cleared.
  **Repair**: on a bike already in the account, a checkup is `already-imported`
  only if the row under its key is on THAT bike and already has every answer
  the import would have written from this copy — same verdict, note and
  symptoms (in any order) — and is `COMPLETED` if the copy is finished. The
  copy is accepted exactly as a first import would accept it (the build the
  payload derives, the same plan), so an unchanged copy compares equal; a row
  with MORE answers (the run carried on in the account) still holds the copy.
  Anything else is `skipped: 'bike-already-imported'`: still not one write,
  the bike and list keys cleared as ruled, the checkup KEPT and the sentence
  shown. On a new bike a held key is `skipped: 'other-bike'` (a new reason,
  with its own sentence in both languages), never a claim that the account
  has it. One read serves both (`heldCheckups`), with the owner in its
  `where`; nothing is derived or planned for a bike that is already there
  unless a row is held under one of the payload's keys. **What this does NOT
  do**: write the later answers. The ruling is that a bike already in the
  account is never written to, so in sequence (a) the answers given after the
  lost response stay in the browser and are not in the account; the visitor is
  told. Before the repair they were nowhere.
- **R3 — two things passed all four walls and were refused by PostgreSQL
  (minor; a 500, no data loss).** A U+0000 in any free string, and an ISO date
  in year 0000. Measured [I], 2026-10-09, on PostgreSQL 16 through Prisma
  7.10.0: `text` answers `22021 invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8":
0x00`; `jsonb` answers `22P05 unsupported Unicode escape sequence`, for a
  value and for a key alike; a timestamp in year 0000 answers `22008 date/time
field value out of range`, and so does the last millisecond before year 1;
  `0001-01-01T00:00:00.000Z` is stored. Each aborts the transaction: the
  action threw, nothing was written, the page stayed on its spinner. The
  header of `lib/guest/schema.ts` said the opposite ("a payload Postgres would
  refuse is refused here first"). **Repair**: the contract refuses U+0000 in
  every string — the bike's and the list's names, notes, refinement values, a
  chosen product's four free fields, and anywhere inside the two untyped
  values (`parts`, where `validateBuild` accepts a part's free-text attribute
  by length alone, and `fit`), keys included — and bounds every date to the
  first instant PostgreSQL stores. The bound is on the instant, so an offset
  cannot walk a year-1 date back into year 0, and it is deliberately not "a
  plausible date": a phone whose clock was reset writes 1970. A stored checkup
  holding either is reported `unreadable` by the collector — kept, and said —
  instead of sinking the bike. The integration test sends each value PAST the
  contract (the transaction throws, no row) and THROUGH it (`VALIDATION`, no
  row, no rate-limit attempt spent).
- **R4 — classic zod was in the chunk `/import` loads (major; a stated rule
  that was false).** `lib/guest/schema.ts` imported `KO_ACTIONS` as a value
  from `lib/domain/schema/procedure.ts`, which is `import * as z from "zod"`.
  The import predates this fix (it is on `origin/main`); the fix's rewritten
  header claimed the module kept classic zod out of the bundle. Measured [I]
  on two builds: with that import, the lazy chunk holding the contract is
  43 054 B (13 262 B gzip) with 43 classic zod class names in it, and the
  build ends "Bundle budget respected"; with the value taken from
  `lib/domain/data/conventions.ts`, 26 186 B (8 789 B gzip) and none.
  **Repair**: `KO_ACTIONS` lives in `data/conventions.ts` with the rest of the
  vocabulary and `schema/procedure.ts` re-exports it (one array, by
  reference).
- **R5 — three rules that no test could fail (major, three findings).** Each
  was a one-line mutation that left every tier green [R]: the payload parsed
  BEFORE the 256 KB wall (the two tests named for that order compared the
  answer only, and the answer is `TOO_MANY` either way); a checkup's
  `startedAt` or `completedAt` loosened to any string; and `server-only`
  imported by `lib/guest/import.ts`, classic `zod` or a generated enum imported
  by `lib/guest/schema.ts` — the last one behind a full build, which stayed
  green with the enum in the chunk. **Repair**: `parseGuestState` is spied on,
  and must not have been called for an oversized payload (and must have been
  for one within the budget); non-ISO and unstorable dates are refused on a
  checkup's two ends and on the bike, at the schema and through the action;
  and `tests/unit/guest/module-graph.test.ts` walks the runtime import graph
  of both files — no classic zod, no generated enum and nothing server-side
  from `schema.ts`; no `server-only`, no `next/*`, no corpus and no database
  client from `import.ts` — with positive controls on three files that do
  reach those things. No ESLint, budget or gate file was changed.
- **R6 — the size wall threw on a value JSON cannot encode (minor).**
  `guestStateBytes` is the first thing the action does with its input and
  `JSON.stringify` throws on a `BigInt`. The decoder of a server action's
  arguments does build one for an argument spelled as one (`parseModelString`,
  read in the installed `react-server-dom-turbopack`); it was NOT sent over
  HTTP, here or by the reviewer. **Repair**: the measure never throws — a value
  with no JSON size is `Infinity` bytes, within no budget, and the answer is
  `TOO_MANY` at the size wall, the parser not called. A value that contains
  itself gets the same answer.
- **R7 — a list line's `refinement` had no cap (minor).** The one record of
  the payload without one: 1 400 entries, 229 601 bytes of JSON in one column,
  were accepted and written [R]. **Repair**: 24 entries, the account's own
  bound (`MAX_REFINEMENT_KEYS` in `liste/actions.ts`, private to a
  `"use server"` module — a test reads the number where it is written). The
  heaviest refinement the contract now allows is inside the 32 KB every JSON
  column is held to (checked).

Two things the reviewers observed and did NOT file, left as they were: an
upper-case copy of the same `guestKey` in one payload is a second checkup (the
key is compared as given); and two DIFFERENT guest bikes carrying the same
checkup id, imported at the same instant, end with one bike imported and the
other request rejected on the unique key (P2002, nothing written for it).

**Found in verification (2026-10-09, a second round), and repaired the same
day.** A verifier attacked the repairs above (commit `0e8d697`), on PostgreSQL
and with mutations of its own. Two findings; each reproduced first [I] with the
verifier's scratch test against that commit.

- **V1 — half a surrogate pair passed all four walls, and R3's own text said
  nothing could (minor; a 500, no data loss).** A string holding ONE half of a
  pair — the first half of an emoji with nothing after it — is a string JSON
  carries (as an escape), zod accepts and the wizard's guard accepts. Measured
  [I] against `0e8d697`, PostgreSQL 16 through Prisma 7.10.0, the payload sent
  as the wire carries it, through `importGuestStateAction`: in a `jsonb`
  column — a list line's refinement value, a chosen product's model, a part's
  free-text attribute — the insert is refused, for the first half and for the
  second half alike (P2007, `invalid input syntax for type json`): the action
  threw, no bike was written, and one of the three hourly attempts was spent
  each time. Through the collector, a stored list line holding one: the
  same throw, all three keys still held — a bike that could not be imported
  while the value was there. In a `text` column nothing throws and nothing is
  kept either: a note sent as `a`, the half, `b` came back as the code units
  `61 fffd 62`, the bike's and the list's names likewise — and the SAME copy
  sent again was then answered `skipped: 'bike-already-imported'`, because
  the row's note is no longer the copy's (R2's comparison, doing what it
  says). **Who writes that U+FFFD is the driver, not PostgreSQL** — a wording
  corrected here after the fact, the outcome above being what was measured.
  Measured on 2026-10-09 by a probe outside the repository, node-postgres
  8.23.0 against PostgreSQL 16.15 — first [R] with the bytes computed by
  Node's own UTF-8 encoder, then [I] with the driver's socket recorded: for
  the parameter `a`, the high half, `b` the bytes written to the socket held
  `61 ef bf bd 62` and not the surrogate's own bytes (`61 ed a0 bd 62`), and
  the value read back was `61 fffd 62` in 5 octets; the surrogate's own bytes
  handed to PostgreSQL as UTF-8 text were refused (22021, invalid byte
  sequence for encoding "UTF8": `0xed 0xa0 0xbd`); and the escaped half in
  `jsonb` was refused (22P02, invalid input syntax for type json: "Unicode
  low surrogate must follow a high surrogate"). So PostgreSQL stores no
  replacement of its own: UTF-8 has no encoding for half a pair, the driver
  encodes it as U+FFFD before PostgreSQL sees anything, and PostgreSQL
  stores what it was sent. Every sentence that said "Postgres stores U+FFFD
  for it" — in `lib/guest/schema.ts`, in `CLAUDE.md`, and in the comments
  and one case title of the three test files — now says that; no assertion
  changed. Meanwhile the header of `lib/guest/schema.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`, the
  action's header and a `describe` of the integration test all stated R3 as a
  rule — "what PostgreSQL would refuse, the contract refuses" — which was one
  case short the day it was written.
  **Repair**, in two halves. The contract refuses a lone surrogate wherever it
  refuses U+0000 — `text()`, and the walk over `parts` and `fit`, keys
  included — with one predicate (`storable`); the test is the regular
  expression class `Cs` under the `u` flag, which matches a surrogate only
  when it is not half of a pair (checked against `isWellFormed()` on eight
  strings; the expression is the older of the two in browsers, and the one
  used). And the COLLECTOR no longer hands one over: every string it
  projects has each lone surrogate replaced by U+FFFD (`wellFormed`) — the
  character the driver was found to send for it anyway — so a note the
  wizard's own cap cut in the middle of an emoji (`slice(0, 2000)` counts
  code units: the fixture is made that way, by the reducer) is `collected`
  and imported, a list line or a pad reference cut that way no longer sinks
  the bike, and the copy compares equal to the rows it wrote: a second import
  of that storage is `already-imported`. Storage is not rewritten. The
  statements were reworded to what they are: a list of measured cases, not a
  proof that the list is complete. **Not done**: U+0000 is still refused and
  not replaced — nothing a person does produces one — so a stored checkup
  holding one is still `unreadable` (R3), and a stored list line holding one
  still makes the server answer `VALIDATION` for the whole payload. And the
  account's OWN writers were not touched or run: a line's refinement
  (`liste/actions.ts`) and a part's free-text attribute (`validateBuild`) are
  taken by length alone (read in the source), so the same string sent to
  those actions by hand presumably throws the same way — not measured, and
  not this change's.
- **V2 — the answer given to the device that LOSES a race was held by no test
  (major; no defect in the code).** When two devices import the same guest
  bike at the same instant, the loser's create is refused by
  `@@unique([userId, guestLocalId])` and it reports through the same
  comparison as a bike found by the first read (`alreadyImported`). Replacing
  that one line by "every checkup of the payload is `already-imported`" — the
  answer on which the browser forgets its checkup — left every committed test
  green: 341 fast cases and 35 on PostgreSQL [R]; reproduced [I] with the two
  unit files of the import, 117 of 117 green under the mutation, and the
  verifier's own case red. Both race tests, the fake's and PostgreSQL's,
  raced IDENTICAL copies, for which that word is true however it is decided.
  **Repair**: tests, no source change beyond a comment. In
  `lib/guest/import.test.ts` the winner imports the run unfinished and the
  loser arrives with it carried on and finished: nothing written,
  `skipped: 'bike-already-imported'`, the checkup key not in the settlement's
  list; and the other way round — the winner had MORE — is `already-imported`.
  On PostgreSQL the same two, through the real client with ONE thing changed:
  the first ownership read misses, so the create is really attempted and it is
  the constraint that refuses it (two ownership reads counted: the miss, then
  the winner's row). That makes the lost race a sequence instead of a matter
  of which of three promises commits first.

24 one-change mutations of this round's repair [I] — the predicate and each
place it is applied, the collector's replacement clause by clause, and three
of the losing path, the verifier's among them — each against the unit,
security and UI tests of the import and against the integration file: 24 of 24
failed at least one test — 20 in both, and 4 in the fast tiers only: the two
about keys and the one about `fit` as a whole, none of which reaches a column,
and "only the first half of a string is replaced", for which the PostgreSQL
fixture has no string with two. Against `0e8d697`'s `lib/guest/schema.ts`, 15
of the new or extended cases are red — 4 in `lib/guest/schema.test.ts`, 1 in
the security tier, 10 on PostgreSQL; with the repair, the import's six files
are 211 fast cases and 49 on PostgreSQL, all green. Each mutated file was put back
from the bytes saved before the mutation, and its sha256 compared.

**How to detect a regression.**

- The repairs of 2026-10-09, each by the test that fails without it — 43
  Vitest cases across six files were red against the sources of `e541e35`, and
  all 239 cases of those files green with the repairs [I]:
  `lib/guest/import.test.ts` ("imports EVERY answer of a run opened on one
  part and finished through the full checkup", the `PARTIAL`/`FULL` column,
  "the full plan asks every question any scope asks", and the block
  "`already-imported` is said of a checkup only when the account holds what
  the copy says"); `tests/integration/guest-import.test.ts` (the same two
  sequences on PostgreSQL, the lost answer, the other bike, "what PostgreSQL
  does not store is a VALIDATION, not a 500" (named "would refuse" until the
  second round found a value it stores as something else), and — adopted from
  the review — a
  full checkup imported and the same one finished natively through
  `finishCheckupAction`, on each of the seven presets: the same `CheckupItem`
  rows and the same list lines, each linked to the same step);
  `tests/security/guest-import.test.ts` (the parser spy, the size wall on a
  `BigInt`, U+0000, dates, the refinement cap); `lib/guest/schema.test.ts`
  (their twins on the schema); `tests/unit/guest/module-graph.test.ts`;
  `tests/ui/guest-import.test.tsx` (the new reason's sentence). The fixtures
  of R1 are made by `resumeGuestRun` (`tests/_helpers/guest-work.ts`): the
  wizard's own resume, `readStoredCheckup` then `fromStored` against the new
  link's plan — which is where the stale scope comes from, not from the test.
- The second round (V1, V2), each by the test that fails without it:
  `lib/guest/schema.test.ts` ("refuses a lone surrogate in every string",
  "collects a note the wizard's own cap cut in the middle of an emoji",
  "hands over no lone surrogate from ANY key");
  `tests/security/guest-import.test.ts` (the same twelve places through the
  action, a whole emoji imported as the control);
  `tests/integration/guest-import.test.ts` (each half in each `jsonb` field
  past the contract and through it; each `text` field read back as another
  character (the driver's U+FFFD); the cut storage imported through the
  collector and found again;
  "answers the device that LOST the race on what the account holds of ITS
  copy"); `lib/guest/import.test.ts` (the two lost races with differing
  copies).
- In a browser: `tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts` has both sequences, in French
  and English — a checkup opened on one part and carried on through the full
  checkup, and through another part, in the real wizard with no fixture, then
  every answer the browser held compared with the rows in Postgres; and an
  import whose response is dropped, more answers in the real wizard, then
  `/import` again: the same rows to the id, the sentence shown, and the
  checkup still under its key byte for byte. With R1's defect put back and the
  build redone, the four first journeys failed (33 answers missing; the
  "nothing kept" sentence on screen); with R2's, the two others (no sentence)
  [I].
- 53 one-change mutations of the repairs [I], 2026-10-09 — the six the
  reviewers had seen survive among them — each run against the unit, security,
  UI and integration tests of the import: 52 failed at least one test at once.
  The one that did not (the verdict left out of the `already-imported`
  comparison: every case then written differed by its note or symptoms too)
  got a test of its own. Run again on the final tree: 53 of 53 red. Each
  mutated file was restored from the saved bytes and its sha256 compared.
  **That count is of the 53 mutations written for it, and no more**: a
  verifier's own — the losing path of a race answering on the key — left
  every test green the same day (V2, above), and [R] 40 others of the
  verifier's were caught, with one more survivor that changes nothing
  observable (the plan does not read a part's attributes).
- The round trip, which is the test that was missing:
  `npx vitest run --project unit lib/guest/schema.test.ts` — "reads the
  checkup the wizard really stores — toStored, under the real key". Every
  fixture of the six guest-import test files now comes from
  `tests/_helpers/guest-work.ts` (the real plan, the reducer, `toStored`,
  `writeStoredCheckup`, `writeGuestBuildList`, `collectGuestState`). The only
  `steps` left in them are in values the tests expect to be refused or
  reported unreadable.
- The rows, in a browser: `npx playwright test --project=desktop-chromium
tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts` reads Postgres after the import — one
  `Checkup`, its items, `BuildList.checkupId`, every line's `checkupItemId`,
  the part states. A second test corrupts the key and expects the toast and
  the key still there. A third has NO fixture for the checkup: the guest
  answers it in the real wizard, which writes both keys itself, then signs up
  and imports — B2 to B4 below, as a test.
- The rows, on PostgreSQL: `tests/integration/guest-import.test.ts` — the
  import with its links, twice, three at once, a failed list write and a
  failed checkup write (no bike row either time), and an unfinished FULL
  checkup resumed and finished through `saveCheckupAction` and
  `finishCheckupAction` as the same row.
- The page: `tests/ui/guest-import.test.tsx` mounts `GuestImport` over a
  storage the wizard's writers filled, with the server action as the only
  stand-in — for each answer the server can give, in French and in English:
  what was sent, which keys are left, which sentence is shown; and a refused
  import leaves all three keys as they were.
- The walls: `tests/security/guest-import.test.ts`; the server's side of the
  rules: `lib/guest/import.test.ts`; the reader: `lib/checkup/storage.test.ts`
  (`peekStoredCheckup`); the rows: `lib/checkup/items.test.ts`.
- **Each of those tests was watched failing** [I], 2026-10-08, against the
  code of that day: 109 one-change mutations of the fix — the reader, the collector
  and the contract, the importer, the action, the page — each run against the
  Vitest tiers that hold it: 167 runs, 166 red. The one green run is a
  mutation of `answeredItems` (the row names the step's LAST part) in the
  security tier, whose checkup has single-part steps only; the unit tier fails
  it. So every mutation failed at least one test. And 7 more behind a
  production rebuild, against the e2e spec on `desktop-chromium`: all 7 red,
  in both languages. The mutation that puts the production bug's effect back —
  the checkup never sent, its key cleared — fails the e2e spec at "a `Checkup`
  row exists", in the fixture's journey and in the wizard's, AFTER the toast,
  the redirect and the cleared keys have all passed: the three claims the old
  spec made. Each mutated file was restored from the bytes read before the
  mutation, and its sha256 compared.
- By hand, on production, once deployed: a checkup made in the wizard as a
  guest, sign-up, `/import`, then look for a `Checkup` row on the imported
  bike — B2 to B4 below.

#### The two journeys

Journey A — signed in, the demo account. All PASS; A4 onward only after
`content:generate` (finding 1).

| Step | What                                                                | Seen                                                                                                                                                     |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A1   | `/` → `/fr`; demo sign-in through the dev-only demo callout         | header "Mes vélos · Camille Démo"                                                                                                                        |
| A2   | "Mes vélos"                                                         | three bikes; the Gravel bike opens, viewer ready                                                                                                         |
| A3   | the chain, by a real click on the 3D canvas and from the parts list | URL `?part=chain`, live region "Pièce sélectionnée : Chaîne", panel "Chaîne"                                                                             |
| A4   | a partial checkup on the chain and the rear caliper                 | 4 steps, the chain KO with a symptom, recap "3 ok · 1 à reprendre · 0 passées", "Créer ma liste"                                                         |
| A5   | the list                                                            | the chain line; a refinement autosaved (`setBuildListItemRefinementAction` 200) that survives a reload                                                   |
| A6   | the Alltricks link                                                  | `https://www.alltricks.fr/C-40598-toutes-les-chaines`, `target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"` — verified by its attributes, not clicked |
| A7   | `/reglages`                                                         | inseam 82 cm → "72,4 cm", saved as `saddleHeightMm: 724`, survives a reload                                                                              |

Journey B — guest.

| Step | What                                                        | Seen                                                                                          |
| ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| B1   | the decision tree, "Je ne sais pas" at each of 12 questions | `/fr/velo/local`, viewer ready — PASS                                                         |
| B2   | a partial checkup (front tyre + chain), two KO              | guest list "0 sur 3 fait" — PASS                                                              |
| B3   | sign-up with a throwaway `…@example.com` account            | PASS                                                                                          |
| B4   | `/fr/import`                                                | bike PASS (listed in "Mes vélos"); list PASS (the same 3 lines); **checkup FAIL** (finding 2) |

**Not verified**: the 3D view of the imported bike. The browser pane stopped
compositing mid-walk — an environment problem, not the app's.

Console and server errors: all from finding 1's episode; none after
`content:generate`; every server action returned 200.

#### Six observations — seen first-hand, not checked against the plan

Recorded for the maintainer's ruling. None was compared with the plan or with
a test, so none is called a defect here.

- **O1.** `window.__va.bike.screenPositionOf('chain')` returned null at the
  default pose (18 other parts had a point); the chain became clickable after
  the pose `close-drive-bb`, as the e2e helper does.
- **O2.** A 3D click leaves the panel on the "Pièces" tab; a list click
  switches to "Infos".
- **O3.** Part badges after an account checkup. The chain went from "À
  remplacer" to "À surveiller" after a KO with the "allongée au-delà de la
  limite d'usure" symptom (the server action maps KO to ATTENTION; the guest
  import maps KO to BROKEN). "Plaquettes ou patins arrière" still showed "À
  remplacer" while its list line said "Marqué fait par un contrôle". And a
  checkup scoped to the REAR caliper stored items against the front caliper
  and lever and turned them "Bon état".
- **O4.** After sign-up the guest lands on `/fr/velo/local/liste` without
  `?spec=…`, which shows "Ouvrez votre vélo : la liste s'appuie sur ses
  pièces." and no import prompt (the prompt is on "Mes vélos").
- **O5.** "Chaîne pour VAE" is a free-text input on a list line (Oui/Non on
  the part panel); the Alltricks link is a category URL that refinement does
  not change, while the Rose Bikes and Decathlon search URLs do change.
- **O6.** A guest partial checkup on the front tyre only produced lines for
  both tyres.

(2026-10-22.) O3's third sentence and O6 were ruled defects and are fixed:
§5.11. O3's first two — a KO written `ATTENTION`, and a hosted part's badge
that does not follow its closed line — were ruled out of that fix and are
entries of `docs/backlog.md`; O5's yes/no box already was one.

---

### 5.7 The Linux container: one test fails one time in four, and the cause is in the page (2026-10-07)

`npm run e2e:docker -- --project=desktop-chromium --project=mobile-chromium`
— the amd64 CI image, emulated on Apple Silicon — on the build of `2a311e5`,
13:13Z–13:58Z [O]: **924 passed, 3 failed, 1 flaky, 64 skipped** in 44.8 min.
All 20 `@snapshot` comparisons passed, which is what this run is the only
local source of.

**Symptom.** The three failures and the flaky one are one test, in both
languages and both projects: `tests/e2e/guides-filter.spec.ts`, "a card opens
its guide". It goes to `/guides`, clicks the first card's link and expects the
guide's URL; `toHaveURL` timed out after 10 s with the URL still `/fr/guides`.
Run on its own with `--repeat-each=3 --retries=0`: 9 of 12 passed in about
3 s, 3 failed. It passed on the host the day before (431 and 874 tests) and in
CI's native Linux matrix on all five pull requests.

**Root cause — read in the source, not proven by an experiment.**
`app/[locale]/guides/page.tsx` renders
`<Suspense fallback={<GuideGrid guides={guides} />}>` around `GuideFilters`, a
client component that calls `useSearchParams()`. On a statically prerendered
route that is the shape `.debug/011` describes: the FALLBACK — the same grid of
cards — is what the HTML holds, and React builds the boundary again on the
client instead of hydrating it. The card a visitor sees first is therefore
replaced by a second, identical one, and a click that lands during the swap is
on a node that is going away. On a fast machine the window is too short to
hit; the emulated container makes it one in four.

**Fix.** None here. It costs a second tap on a slow device and loses nothing,
and the right fix is the one `.debug/011` made for the decision tree (read the
query without `useSearchParams` during render, so the list is hydrated rather
than rebuilt), which is a change to a page, not to a close-out.
`docs/backlog.md` has the entry.

**What it is not.** Not noise to retry past, and not a reason to loosen the
test: the test is right that a click on a visible card should open it.

### 5.8 The part edit form belonged to no part: one part's value written to another, and typed text dropped (2026-10-09)

Found on 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08 by two verifiers working on a copy of
`origin/main` — the code production runs — [R], with jsdom and, for the
saved bike, the real action on PostgreSQL. The maintainer ruled: fix before
the tag, with the fullest of the three options (an identity per part, a
commit when the field is removed, no write without an edit), knowing that
the second half had not been seen in a browser. It has been since: it is
real (below).

**Symptom.** On `/velo/local` and `/velo/<uuid>`, with the "Infos" tab
showing a part opened from the list, a part picked ON THE BIKE changes the
part under the panel without changing the tab (§5.6's O2 saw the half of this
that is by design). The number and text fields of the edit form went on
showing the FIRST part's values under the second part's title — with the
first part's "Enregistré" if it had just been saved — and leaving such a
field, typing nothing, wrote that value to the second part.

- Nothing typed, a stock bike [R, PostgreSQL through the real
  `updateBikePartAction`]: "Fourche" from the list, the seatpost picked on the
  bike, the travel field entered and left: the dropper post's travel went
  from 170 to 140, the server answering ok.
- The same in a browser, on the unfixed build [I, Chromium 153.0.8010.12
  headless, Playwright 1.63.0, projects `desktop-chromium` and
  `mobile-chromium`, a guest bike]: after ONE click — or tap — on the
  seatpost's MESH, the field read 140 under "Tige de selle"; after Tab through
  the form's controls its status read "Enregistré" and `va:bike:local` held
  `seatpost.travel-mm = 140`. The click on a real canvas, which jsdom cannot
  make, was the step both verifiers had to substitute.
- Which fields: every number or text attribute key two parts of a bike share.
  From the catalogue over the seven presets [I]: 11 keys — `travel-mm`
  (fork, dropper post: the only pair that differs out of the box),
  `etrto-width`, `piston-count`, `max-tire-width`, `max-rotor`, `bar-clamp`,
