  `seatpost-diameter`, `seat-clamp-diameter`, and three between two HOSTED
  parts, `pad-model`, `pad-thickness` (the pads) and `valve-length` (the
  tubes). The verifiers listed eight: they required a mesh, and a hosted part
  is never picked on the bike — but `?part=` on a history move selects one.
- Not affected: the demo bike (no form); a part opened from the list after
  another opened from the list (the "Pièces" tab unmounts the form in
  between). Selects and checkboxes always SHOWED their own value — they are
  controlled — but their status line was carried to the next part like the
  others: with the key removed, `tread` saved for the front tyre leaves
  "Enregistré" under the rear tyre's own select [R, then I: 64 cases, below].

**Root cause** — three things, none of which a test could see while every
test of the form rendered it with a part set by hand
(`components/bike/PartInfo.test.tsx`).

1. `PartInfo` rendered `<PartEditForm>` with no key, and the panel does not
   unmount when the selection changes, so React kept the same instance and
   the same `<input>` for the next part. An uncontrolled input does not follow
   a new `defaultValue` once it has a value of its own, and React gives it one
   at mount (`value === element.value || (element.value = value)`, read in
   react-dom 19.2.8, `react-dom-client.development.js:1720`; the run above is
   what it looks like). The per-field status lived in that same instance,
   keyed by attribute key alone.
2. The field committed on EVERY blur, whatever it held, with no comparison:
   a stale field was a write waiting for a focus.
3. A field removed while it has focus gets no `onBlur` from React (next
   paragraph), so the only exit that committed was a blur that reached React
   before the node went.

**What a real browser does when the next press is another tab** [I,
2026-10-09, a probe spec outside the repository; Chromium 153.0.8010.12
headless on macOS, the unfixed build of `1c22923` with the test hooks; a
guest bike, 45 typed in the front tyre's width and the field not left, then
the "Pièces" tab — and again "Actions" — pressed with the pointer]:

- `desktop-chromium`, a mouse click. Events at `document`, capture phase, in
  order: `pointerdown` and `mousedown` on the tab, the field still in the DOM
  and still `activeElement`; then **`change`, `blur` and `focusout` ON THE
  FIELD, the field still connected and `activeElement` already `<body>`**;
  then `focus` on the tab with the field gone; `pointerup`, `mouseup`,
  `click`. `va:bike:local` afterwards: `tire-front.etrto-width = 42`. **The 45
  was lost.** Four runs of four (two tabs, two languages).
- `mobile-chromium`, a tap: `pointerdown`, `touchstart`, `pointerup`,
  `touchend`, then the synthesised `mousedown` and the same `change`, `blur`,
  `focusout` on the field, then `focus` on the tab. Stored: 42. Four of four.
- The committed test of this case failed on that build for the guest bike AND
  for a saved one, whose row was read from PostgreSQL: 42 where 45 was typed.

So the loss the verifiers reproduced in jsdom is real in Chromium, and for a
reason their emulation had guessed: Radix activates a tab on `mousedown`
(`@radix-ui/react-tabs` `dist/index.mjs:123-125`, read), React commits the
change before the press's default action moves the focus, Chromium DOES fire
`blur` on the focused node as it is removed — and React ignores events that
arrive during its own commit (`_enabled = !1` as a commit starts, and
`dispatchEvent` does nothing without it: `react-dom-client.development.js`
lines 13861 and 23582, read). A browser that fired no `blur` at all would
lose the text the same way. Only Chromium is installed here: **WebKit and
Firefox were not run.**

One more thing a browser settled, as a by-product of the mutation runs below
[I]: with the removal flush disabled and everything else in place, 45 typed
for the front tyre followed by ONE click on the rear tyre's mesh still stored
45 for the front tyre on `desktop-chromium`. A mouse press on the canvas
therefore leaves the field before the click selects — the order the
verifiers could only read in camera-controls' source. On a touch screen it
was not observed: the helper lowers the sheet with the keyboard first.

**Fix.** The three rules below are in `components/bike/PartEditForm.tsx`. In
the first version (`620f72e`) that file was the whole change — nothing else
in `app/`, `lib/` or `components/`, tests aside — and this paragraph said so.
It went on saying so, with "`lib/bike/repo.ts` and `updateBikePartAction` are
untouched", through repairs that rewrote the action ("Second review", finding
1). As it stands the fix is also:

- `updateBikePartAction` (`app/[locale]/velo/[id]/actions.ts`), now one
  interactive transaction that takes the bike row's lock before it reads —
  the only interactive `$transaction` in `app/` or `lib/`. Its write path and
  its ways of failing (a wait on the lock, Prisma's transaction timeouts on a
  pool of three connections) are NEW, were read and not measured, and have
  not run on production ("Review of the fix", finding 2);
- `loadToast()` in `components/ui-ext/Notices.tsx`, and
  `components/bike/UnsavedNotices.tsx`, which `BikeWorkspace` mounts (finding
  3);
- `bikeKey()` on every repo of `lib/bike/repo.ts` — which bike it writes —
  and `lib/bike/own-writes.ts`, the form's record of its own writes ("Second
  review", finding 2; it was `bikeParam`, the route's `[id]` segment, until
  the third review found that `local` names a slot and not a bike), with
  `peekLocalBikeId` in `lib/bike/local-bike.ts` to name a guest bike;
- a guest repo built for ONE bike — `localBikeRepo(bikeId)`, handed that id
  by `BikeWorkspace` and by `MeasurementForm` — whose two writes are refused
  once the slot holds another ("Fourth review", finding 1: rule 3 had made a
  write of what `main` only dropped).

Three rules:

1. **The form is keyed by the part** — `PartEditForm` renders its fields
   under `key={partId}` INSIDE itself, not at the call site in `PartInfo`, so
   that no caller can forget it. A new part is a new instance: new inputs, an
   empty status map, and a `partId` that cannot change during an instance's
   life, which is what makes every write carry the part the field was
   rendered for, never the one selected when the write happens.
2. **A write needs an edit.** A number or text field records what was typed
   since its last commit (a ref set on every native `input` event and on
   React's `change` — by `onChange` alone until finding 1's own other case),
   and a blur commits only if there is such a record AND the value is not
   the stored one. Leaving a field unchanged sends nothing. The one exception
   is the one the old code had by accident and visitors may rely on: leaving
   a field whose last value was REFUSED sends it again. (Since the review:
   "the stored one" is what the form was last told, so the comparison is
   skipped while a write of the same field of the same bike is in flight —
   "Review of the fix", finding 4 — and for as long as the page does not say
   what this document's last write of it left: "Second review", finding 2.)
3. **Typed text is committed when the field is removed**, from a
   layout-effect cleanup, where the node is still in the document. It goes
   through a write of its own that touches none of the form's state — no
   status, no transition: the form is going too. What it commits is what
   leaving the field would have: the value typed, or a CLEAR for a field the
   visitor emptied. (Since the review: never text the browser cannot read as
   a number, which the first version sent as a clear — finding 1.)

Two things the three rules needed around them. A write that answers AFTER
its form is gone — rule 3's, or an ordinary save overtaken by the next
selection — has no field to report under: its build is still handed up
(`onSaved`), and a refusal is raised as a notice through the layout's toast
host, naming the part and the field, with the reason and for twenty seconds
(`bike.panel.unsaved`: "Pneu avant · Largeur du pneu : votre saisie n’a pas
été enregistrée."). Before, that refusal was shown under the NEXT part's
field of the same name, or nowhere. (Since the review: a toast TAKEN from
the host, which needs no download, in colours that pass AA, and said by the
page itself when no toast can be had — findings 3 and 9.) A write that does not answer at all is
still the route's `error.tsx` while its form is on screen, and that notice,
without a reason, once it is not. And an untouched field now FOLLOWS the
stored value when it changes under it (a second layout effect; text being
typed is never replaced): without it, the write of rule 3 answering after the
visitor has come straight back to the part left the old number in the field
beside a read-only line saying the new one — and the same after the panel is
rebuilt between its two layouts, for a guest bike too.

**Which exits of a focused field with typed text are covered — and which are
not.**

| The field goes because…                                      | What happens to the text                                            | Held by                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| another part is picked: mesh, silhouette, keyboard, `?part=` | committed to ITS part (by the blur where there is one, else rule 3) | ui (every shared key, both orders), integration, e2e         |
| another tab of the panel is pressed                          | rule 3                                                              | ui, integration, e2e (guest and saved, desktop and touch)    |
| the panel is rebuilt: docked ↔ bottom sheet at 1024 px       | rule 3                                                              | ui (both directions)                                         |
| the sheet is lowered by its handle                           | nothing is unmounted; the press is a blur, so the ordinary commit   | ui                                                           |
| the route changes inside the app (history back, a link)      | rule 3 — a guest bike is in storage before the teardown returns     | ui for the guest bike; e2e for a saved one, since the review |
| the guest bike is replaced by ANOTHER PAGE of the browser    | refused: nothing written, the notice — since the fourth review      | unit, ui, e2e (two pages of one context, both ways)          |
| the page is unloaded: reload, closed tab, another document   | **lost**                                                            | nothing — by decision                                        |

On the route-change row, since the second review: that write can still be in
flight when the visitor is back on the bike, or answered to a workspace that
is gone, and the form mounted there knows of it either way (finding 2 of that
review, with what it does NOT settle — the order two writes of one field are
delivered in).

"Rule 3", in every row, since the review: a value is written; a field the
visitor EMPTIED is cleared (new with rule 3 — before it, an emptied field
that was not left kept its value); **text the browser cannot read as a number
writes nothing**, the stored value stays, and the notice says so.

The saved bike on a route change [I, a probe outside the repository,
`desktop-chromium`]: "Mes vélos" → the bike by its link → a width typed and
not left → history back. Seven runs of seven: one POST (to the page the
visitor went back to, answered 200) and the typed width in PostgreSQL. It
was left out of the committed tests at first because it rests on how Next's
client dispatches an action while it navigates, which its own documentation
calls an implementation detail
(`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/07-mutating-data.md`,
read: "dispatches and awaits them one at a time … may change"). Those seven
runs had NOTHING ELSE IN FLIGHT, and that was the case not run: with another
write of the same bike pending, the two overlapped on the server and one was
lost ("Review of the fix", finding 2). It is a committed e2e now, with a
write in flight — whichever way a later Next dispatches the second, what it
holds is that both end in PostgreSQL.

The page unload [I, the same probe, once each]: after a reload the guest
bike held 42 where 45 was typed, and the saved bike 45 where 46 was. Not
covered because nothing is unmounted, because a saved bike's write would be
a request the browser is free to drop, and because a guest bike saved where
a saved one is not would be a difference this form is built not to have —
and could not report. It was as lost before the fix. A `pagehide` flush is a
candidate for the backlog, with that caveat.

**What did not change**, each held by a test: selects and checkboxes commit
on change as before; a refused value is said under its field and sent again
when the field is left again; an emptied field that is LEFT clears the
attribute (that one REMOVED without being left is cleared too is new: rule
3, above); a save in flight disables its field and reads "Enregistrement…";
the demo bike has no form. First-load JS of `/[locale]/velo/[id]`, from
builds with the test hooks [I]: 241.1 KiB before, 241.6 KiB after the first
version, 242.2 KiB after the review's repairs, against a ceiling of
269.0 KiB that was not touched.

**Tests** [I], and what each was seen failing under. Every one goes through
the panel or the workspace — none renders the form alone. The counts in this
list and in the mutation table under it are those of the FIRST version of
the fix (`620f72e`); what the review added is counted in "Review of the fix".

- `components/bike/PartsPanel.test.tsx`, 142 cases: the real `PartsPanel` on
  the real viewer store, for the guest bike (the real `localBikeRepo`) and a
  saved one (the real `remoteBikeRepo` over an action that answers as the
  real one does). Every part of every preset picked in turn, each given a
  value of its own first (out of the box both tyres are 42 mm wide, and a
  field showing the wrong part's value passes for one showing the right
  one); every ordered pair of parts sharing a typed key — 22, enumerated from
  the catalogue by `tests/_helpers/part-attributes.ts` — with the field left
  first and with the selection changing first.
- `components/bike/BikeWorkspace.test.tsx`, 27 cases: the real workspace in
  both layouts; the part picked with a pointer press on the silhouette, with
  Enter and with Space, and by `?part=` on a history move — the one route
  that changes the selection without taking the focus out of the field.
- `tests/integration/part-form.test.ts`, 5 cases: the real page function, the
  real workspace in jsdom, the real `updateBikePartAction` (spied, not
  replaced) and PostgreSQL. The first two are the verifiers' own sequences
  with the outcome reversed: front 45 and rear STILL 38; no write at all on
  the stock dropper bike.
- `tests/e2e/part-panel.spec.ts`, 4 cases in each language: a part picked on
  the canvas with nothing typed; a width typed and then the other tyre
  picked on the canvas; a width typed and not left and the "Pièces" tab
  pressed, for a guest bike and for a saved one (its row read from
  PostgreSQL, and its POSTs counted: one). Where WebGL is off the part is
  picked on the silhouette with the keyboard.

Mutations of the fix, one change each, the file put back from a saved copy
and its sha256 compared [I]. 26 over the two ui files (169 cases): each
fails at least one. The three the ruling names, per tier:

| Mutation                          | ui (169) | integration (5) | e2e, Chromium, desktop and touch (8 each)                      |
| --------------------------------- | -------- | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| the key removed                   | 113 fail | 2 fail          | 2 fail: the typed-then-picked case ("Enregistré" carried over) |
| the removal flush removed         | 70 fail  | 3 fail          | 4 fail: the tab press, guest and saved                         |
| the no-write-without-edit removed | 129 fail | 2 fail          | 4 fail: both picked-on-the-bike cases (a save reported)        |

Read the first row before trusting the key alone: with the key removed and
the other two rules in place, the nothing-typed sequence of the symptom
PASSES in a browser — the untouched field follows the stored value and the
blur writes nothing. The three rules overlap on purpose, and the key is what
holds the status line and the text typed for another part. All 8 e2e cases
were also seen failing on the unfixed build, except the typed-then-picked
one, which was written after it.

**Not run, not established.** WebKit and Firefox (not installed here; CI's
`mobile-webkit` leg runs this spec and is non-blocking). `npm run
e2e:docker` (the Linux image). Production: nothing here is deployed.

Since measured, by a probe outside the repository [R, and again I on the
repaired build; Chromium 153, `desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium`]: a
press — a click, and a TAP with the sheet half open — on the handlebar's
mesh while the stem's `bar-clamp` field has the caret and 25.4 typed in it.
The press landed on the canvas, the handlebar was selected, the stem held
25.4, the handlebar's 31.8 was untouched, its field read 31.8 and its status
was empty.

**Left as found**, each seen while testing:

- **LEAVING a number field that holds text the browser cannot read clears
  the attribute, and says "Enregistré"** [I, the repaired build, a guest
  bike, `desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium`: `45.` and `4e` typed in the
  front tyre's width, then the Tab key — `input.value` `""`, `badInput`
  `true`, stored 42 before and nothing after, status "Enregistré", no
  notice]. It does on `main` too: that exit is the blur's, older than the
  three rules. The first version of this paragraph said only that, "as
  before" — and was untrue of the exit the fix had ADDED, where the same
  empty value became a clear on a tab press ("Review of the fix", finding
  1). The removal no longer does it; whether the blur should go on doing it
  is the maintainer's to rule on (`docs/backlog.md`, "A number field left
  holding text the browser cannot read").
- The domain keeps a text exactly as given while this form trims what is
  typed.
- `updateBikeFitAction` merges the patch into the fit it reads and writes
  the result back with nothing held in between (read [I] in
  `app/[locale]/velo/[id]/reglages/actions.ts`; NOT measured) — the shape
  `updateBikePartAction` had until the review (finding 2). `docs/backlog.md`
  has the entry.

#### Review of the fix (2026-10-09, the same day)

Two reviewers attacked `620f72e` — the first version of the fix above — in a
real browser [R: Chromium 153.0.8010.12 headless, the built site with the
test hooks, `desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium`, a guest bike in
`va:bike:local` and saved bikes read from PostgreSQL; jsdom through the real
`PartsPanel`; one-change mutations]. Eleven findings, two of them the same
defect seen once in the code and once in this note. Each was run again by
the implementer [I] ON A BUILD OF `620f72e`, with the test that now holds it,
before anything was changed. All were real; none was rejected.

**1. Text a number field cannot read was a DELETION of the stored value**
(blocker). `45.` on the way to 45.5, a lone minus, `4e`: a number input
reports them as an empty `value` with `validity.badInput` set, rule 3 read
the empty value as "the visitor cleared the field", the clear was written,
the write answered ok, and nothing was said.

- [R] guest bike, front tyre at 42, the text typed and the "Pièces" tab
  pressed: `input.value=""`, `badInput=true`, stored afterwards: nothing —
  for `45.`, `4e` and `-`, with a click and with a tap. A saved bike whose
  owner had set 38: one POST, answered 200, PostgreSQL holding nothing for
  the width, and the page showing the catalogue's 42 again. With the removal
  flush taken out, every one of those kept its value: the path was NEW with
  the fix.
- [I] the same on `620f72e` through the committed e2e cases: 8 of 8 failed
  (guest and saved, two languages, two projects) — "Expected: 42, Received:
  undefined" for the guest bike, "Expected: 0, Received: 1" POST for the
  saved one. In jsdom, with the field given the state Chromium reports
  (jsdom has no `badInput`): 6 cases of `PartsPanel.test.tsx`, 6 of
  `BikeWorkspace.test.tsx` and 1 on PostgreSQL failed.
- Repair: the field records `validity.badInput` beside the text, and one
  removed holding unreadable text sends NOTHING; the visitor gets the "not
  saved" notice with the domain's own sentence for a value it refuses. A
  field the visitor really emptied (`value` empty, `badInput` false) is still
  cleared by its removal, as by a blur: that half is a decision, and has its
  own cases (jsdom, and a browser — it passed on `620f72e` too).
- **The repair's own other case** [I, found while re-reading it, then seen
  on its build, `31a711b`, on both projects]. The record was first kept by
  `onChange`, and React fires `onChange` only when the value it tracks has
  MOVED. A field emptied (`value` `""`) and then given a minus (`value`
  still `""`) raised none: it stayed on record as "the visitor cleared the
  field", and the tab press deleted the 42 — "Expected: 42, Received:
  undefined", four runs of four. Selecting the text and typing the minus
  over it, which is what every case above did, goes from `"42"` to `""` and
  was caught. The record is now kept on every native `input` event as well
  (`onInput`), which is told of each keystroke; the e2e case types `-` and
  `.` into a field emptied first, and two jsdom cases hold the sequence in
  both directions (unreadable text deleted again IS a field the visitor
  emptied).
- NOT changed: LEAVING such a field. "Left as found", above.

**2. Two writes of one bike in flight together, and one of them lost**
(major). `updateBikePartAction` read the row, edited the build and wrote it
back with nothing held in between, on the understanding that Next's client
sends one server action at a time. It does while the page stays put. When a
navigation arrives it takes the pending action's turn, and an action
dispatched during it is sent AT ONCE (`dispatchAction`,
`node_modules/next/dist/client/components/app-router-instance.js`, read:
"Navigations (including back/forward) take priority over any pending
actions"). Rule 3's write on a route change is dispatched exactly then.

- [R] saved bike, `desktop-chromium`: the tread select changed, its POST
  held in transit until a second POST is on its way, a width typed and not
  left, history back. Five runs of six lost one of the two edits (four times
  the select's, once the width's); both POSTs answered 200; no notice. And
  with no typed field at all — two selects, then history back — one run of
  four lost an edit: the server's race is older than rule 3, which added a
  way into it.
- [I] the committed e2e case on `620f72e`: 2 runs of 14 lost an edit (1 of 8
  with the default workers, 1 of 6 with one). Directly on PostgreSQL, twelve
  edits of twelve fields sent together — started one event-loop turn apart,
  so that they overlap (the committed case's helper says why not in one
  tick) — nine were not there afterwards, in the one run made, every call
  having answered ok.
- Repair, on the server — nothing in the client orders two writes: the whole
  edit is one interactive transaction whose FIRST statement is an `UPDATE`
  of the bike row, ownership predicate included. That takes the row's lock;
  a second call for the same bike waits on it, and under READ COMMITTED
  (pinned) the read that follows sees what the first wrote. No raw SQL
  (`tests/security/sql-injection.test.ts`), no retry loop, nothing logged as
  an error. A refusal found after the lock is thrown as far as the
  transaction's edge so that the lock's own write — a newer `updatedAt`,
  which "Mes vélos" sorts by — is rolled back with it.
- [I] after: the twelve-edit case 7 runs of 7; the reviewer's two probes on
  the repaired build 7 of 7 each on `desktop-chromium` and 1 of 1 on
  `mobile-chromium`; the committed e2e case green on four projects.
- Limits, read and not measured: the pool has three connections
  (`lib/db/prisma.ts`) and a transaction waiting for the lock holds one, so
  a fourth and later concurrent call for the SAME bike waits for a
  connection, up to Prisma's two-second default, and fails past it. And
  `updateBikeAction` re-derives `parts` from a row it read before its own
  write, with nothing held: a part edit committed in between is written
  over by it. That race is that action's, and is not closed here.

**3. The "not saved" notice needed a download, so it was never shown
offline** (major). The form raised it with its own `import("sonner")`. That
is a chunk the page has not requested — the bundler gives the form's import
a different chunk from the layout host's — so with the network down the
import failed, the visitor was told nothing, and the rejection went
unhandled.

- [R] saved bike, `context.setOffline(true)`, a width typed and not left,
  then the tab, or a history move onto another part: no notice, PostgreSQL
  unchanged, and `pageerror: Failed to load chunk …` — while a server
  answering 500 with the network up did raise it.
- [I] on `620f72e`: the two committed offline cases failed 8 of 8; in jsdom
  the import's rejection was reported by Vitest as two unhandled errors.
- Repair: the layout's host keeps `toast` when its own chunk arrives
  (`loadToast()`, `components/ui-ext/Notices.tsx`), so from a moment after
  hydration the notice is raised from memory. Should even that be missing,
  the rejection is caught and the sentence is shown by the workspace itself
  (`components/bike/UnsavedNotices.tsx`: plain text, the palette's danger
  colour on its paper, fixed to the bottom of the viewport above the sheet).
- [I] after: the offline cases green on four projects. With the host's
  `toast` NOT kept (a one-line mutation, built) they fail 8 of 8 again —
  moving the import into the host's module is not what fixes it; keeping
  what it resolved to is — and on such a build the page's own region was
  seen in a browser, by a probe outside the repository: `role="alert"`, the sentence in both languages, inside the
  viewport and topmost at its own position on `desktop-chromium` and on
  `mobile-chromium` with the sheet fully open, no page error, axe clean in
  both schemes. A first placement, in the flow of the workspace, was painted
  UNDER the open sheet on the phone (at y = 124 px): hence the fixed
  position.
- Not covered: the workspace itself gone (a route change) AND no toast to be
  had — there is nothing left to say it in, and nothing is thrown.

**4. A value typed back while an earlier write of the same field was in
flight was not sent** (minor). 45 on its way (rule 3, the tab pressed); back
on the tyre the field still shows 42; the visitor types 42 to be sure and
leaves the field. Compared with the 42 the form was last told, that is
"unchanged": nothing is sent, and the bike ends at 45.

- [R] jsdom, an action answering one call at a time: stored 45, no status,
  no notice — with the tab, and with an ordinary blur followed by another
  part and back. In the real app with POSTs delayed 2.5 s: PostgreSQL 45,
  one POST.
- [I] on `620f72e`: three cases of `PartsPanel.test.tsx` failed. After: they
  pass, and the reviewer's browser probe reads PostgreSQL 42 with two POSTs
  on both projects.
- Repair: writes sent and not yet answered are counted per repo and
  `part:key`, at module level (the form that sent one is usually gone when
  the question is asked); while the count is not zero an edit is sent
  whatever it compares to. (Per BIKE since the second review — a guest
  bike's own only since the third — and kept past the answer with what the
  write left: a count per repo object, dropped at the answer, does not
  survive a route change — the second review's finding 2.) And a
  field does not take the stored value while its own save is in flight, or
  the earlier write answering first would put ITS number in the field for as
  long as the later one takes.

**Tests, as they stand after the review** [I]: `PartsPanel.test.tsx` 231
cases (142 + 89), `BikeWorkspace.test.tsx` 38 (27 + 11),
`UnsavedNotices.test.tsx` 4, `tests/integration/part-form.test.ts` 6 (5 + 1),
`tests/integration/bikes.test.ts` 4 new cases on the action, and
`tests/e2e/part-panel.spec.ts` 8 new cases in each language (12 in its
"the form belongs to one part" half, 38 in the file): green on
`desktop-chromium`, `mobile-chromium`, `no-webgl` and `mobile-landscape`.

One thing the full gate showed about the tests themselves [I]. The cases
that need a write IN FLIGHT were timed in the first version — an answer
after 60 ms, against the clicks that had to fit before it — and one of them
("a saved bike's field follows the write that answers after the visitor came
straight back") failed once under `bash scripts/ci.sh`, with every project
running: the answer came first. In `PartsPanel.test.tsx` a saved bike's
writes are now HELD until the case answers them (`world.answer()`), so "in
flight" is a state the case is in. Ten cases: six of the first version,
their assertions unchanged, and four of the review's. Each was seen failing
again under the mutation it holds. A held write has one cost: a case that
fails half-way leaves it unanswered, and React entangles async transitions —
the cases after it then wait for ever on theirs and fail too (seen: an
unrelated StrictMode case failing under two mutations). An `afterEach` lets
every held write through.

Mutations of the review's repairs, one change each, the files put back from
a saved copy and their sha256 compared [I]: 25, each failing at least one
test — 21 under the ui tier, 3 of the action under PostgreSQL (the lock
replaced by a read; the lock taken outside the transaction; REPEATABLE READ
in place of READ COMMITTED), and one that only a browser sees (the host's
`toast` not kept: all 328 ui cases pass, the offline e2e cases fail). The 26
mutations of the first version were NOT run again on the repaired file.

**Not run by the review or its repairs**: WebKit, Firefox, the Linux image,
production. The pool limit of finding 2 and `updateBikeFitAction`'s race
were read, not measured.

#### Second review (2026-10-10)

A verifier attacked the repaired fix (`faf4040`) [R: Chromium 153.0.8010.12
headless, the built site with the test hooks, `desktop-chromium` and
`mobile-chromium`, saved bikes read from PostgreSQL; jsdom through the real
`PartsPanel`]. Three findings: one in the code, two in what is written about
it. Each was reproduced by the implementer [I] on `faf4040` before anything
was changed. All were real; none was rejected — and the one in the code had
a second half the review had not seen, found by running its own probe again
after the first repair.

**1. This note's "Fix" paragraph was untrue of the fix** (major, by the rule
that a document does not say what the code does not do). It read
"`components/bike/PartEditForm.tsx`, nothing else in `app/`, `lib/` or
`components/`; `lib/bike/repo.ts` and `updateBikePartAction` are untouched" —
true of `620f72e`, and left standing by the repair that rewrote the action
and edited the rest of that very paragraph. [I] `git diff 1c22923..620f72e
--stat -- app lib components`, tests left out: one file, the form. The same
up to `faf4040`: five — the form, the action (155 lines), `BikeWorkspace.tsx`,
`UnsavedNotices.tsx` (new) and `Notices.tsx`. And rule 2 said "a ref set by
`onChange`" when the ref has been set on every `input` event too since
finding 1's own other case. Both are corrected above: the paragraph now lists
what the fix is made of, the server's half and what was not measured of it
included.

**2. The form's record of its own writes did not survive a route change —
in two ways, and the first repair closed one** (minor). Finding 4's repair
counted the writes in flight per repo OBJECT. `BikeWorkspace` builds its repo
when it mounts (`useMemo`), so a route left and come straight back to is a
second object over the same bike, and the form mounted there knew nothing of
a write the first had sent.

- The sequence: 45 typed for the front tyre and not left; history back (rule
  3 sends 45); forward again at once, to a page the router kept and which
  still says 42; 42 typed to put it right, and the field left — or the
  "Pièces" tab pressed. Nothing was sent, no status appeared, and the bike
  ended at 45.
- [R] jsdom, a new `bikeRepoFor("db", …)` over the same action for the
  second mount: stored 45, one write; the control, the same sequence with
  the SAME repo object, passed. In the real app with every POST delayed
  2.5 s: 9 runs of 14 ended at 45 in PostgreSQL after one POST (both
  projects, the field left and the tab pressed); in the 5 that ended at 42
  the first write had answered before the 42 was left.
- [I] on `faf4040`: the reviewer's jsdom case failed and its control passed.
  The cases written for it: 3 failed in the ui tier (2 of
  `PartsPanel.test.tsx`, 1 of `BikeWorkspace.test.tsx`), and the e2e case
  failed 8 runs of 8 on a build of that commit (`desktop-chromium` and
  `mobile-chromium`, two languages, the field left and the tab pressed):
  `Expected: "Enregistrement…", Received: ""` where the field was left, and
  `{ sent: 1, width: 45 }` for `{ sent: 2, width: 42 }` where the tab was
  pressed. That case times nothing: each POST waits at a gate the case
  opens.
- First repair (`761d602`): the record is the BIKE's. Every repo says which
  bike it writes — `BikeRepo.bikeParam`, the `[id]` segment the bike is
  addressed by: `demo`, `local`, or the UUID (`lib/bike/repo.ts`, one line in
  each of its three constructors) — and the form keys its record by that and
  `part:key`. A repo object lasts as long as the component that built it; the
  bike does not end with it. After it the e2e case passed 8 runs of 8.
  (True of a saved bike. For a guest bike the segment is the word `local`,
  which names a slot and not a bike: "Third review", below, where
  `bikeParam` became `bikeKey()`.)
- **That was half of it** [I, found by running the reviewer's own probe on a
  build of `761d602`]. 11 runs of 12 ended at 42. One ended at 45, with no
  status and one POST, the field having been left 70 ms after it was read
  back as 42: the 45 had ANSWERED by then. A write's answer hands its build
  to the workspace that sent it (`onSaved`); after a route change that
  workspace is gone, and the one mounted again goes on showing the page the
  router kept until Next has fetched it again. In between nothing is in
  flight, the form says 42, and 42 typed there is "unchanged" exactly as
  before. Made to stand still — every RSC fetch issued after the first POST
  was let through HELD, by a probe outside the repository — it was 4 runs of
  4 at 45 with one POST, both projects, the field left and the tab pressed.
  On the connection that makes the first write slow, that window is one more
  request long. (What was held [I, the same probe's log]: the first fetch
  after the POST's response was addressed to the page the action had been
  sent to, `/fr/mes-velos`, followed by prefetches; the bike's page said 45
  only once they were let through.)
- [I] on `761d602`'s form: 2 cases of `PartsPanel.test.tsx` failed; and the
  e2e case's second half — the write answered, every later RSC fetch held —
  failed 8 runs of 8 on a build with that form (both projects, two
  languages, the field left and the tab pressed) while its first half
  passed 8 of 8.
- Second repair, as it stands (`b173d9e` and the commit that follows it): a
  write stays on the form's record past its answer, with what it LEFT in the
  field, and a page that does not say that is not compared with — an edit is
  sent whatever it compares to (`lib/bike/own-writes.ts`, plain TS). "What
  it left" is compared as pages say it: a CLEAR leaves nothing, which the
  build it answers with says by having no such attribute and a page rendered
  later by showing the catalogue's default — the bike is derived again from
  its answers on every load — and either agrees with it.
- **The record is not dropped the first time a page agrees with it** [I;
  reasoned, and made to happen in jsdom — NOT seen in a browser]. In
  `b173d9e` it was: a write was "settled" once the form had been handed what
  it left, and taken off the record there. But a page rendered before a
  write can be delivered after one rendered after it, and the field then
  says 42 again over a bike that holds 45 — the same "unchanged" a third
  time. So nothing is forgotten: a page is in doubt for as long as it does
  not say what this document last wrote in the field, however often an
  earlier page did (`BikeWorkspace.test.tsx` holds it with a page that ran
  before the write, delivered after the one that ran after it; with the
  record dropped at the first agreement that case and four of the record's
  own fail). For the same reason, of several writes of one field the record
  keeps what the last one SENT left, not the last to answer: an earlier
  write's answer can come back after a later one's.
- [I] after, on a build of the final tree: the reviewer's probe, 24 runs of
  24 at 42 (both projects, six repeats); the held-fetch probe, 8 of 8 at 42
  with two POSTs; the committed e2e case, both halves, 48 runs of 48 with no
  retry (8 tests on each of the two projects, three repeats). The ui and
  unit tiers of `components/bike` and `lib/bike`: 577 cases of 577.
- **What the record costs**, and a case pins it so that it stays a decision
  (`BikeWorkspace.test.tsx`): sending is the safe side, so doubt is resolved
  towards one write too many. When a field this document has written was
  changed since by something else — another tab, another device — the page
  no longer says what the record holds, and the first value typed back to
  what the page shows is SENT, although it changes nothing. Its answer is
  what the page says, and the doubt ends there. Focusing a field and leaving
  it is still never a write, whatever is on record. (As written on
  2026-10-10 this paragraph was untrue of guest bikes, for which no other
  tab and no other device was needed: "Third review".)
- **What neither repair does: order two writes.** While the page stays put
  Next's client sends one action at a time (read, as in finding 2 of the
  first review). After a navigation it does not [I, a probe outside the
  repository, the build of `761d602`, both projects, 16 runs, MEASURED]:
  with the removal's POST held in transit, the POST of the 42 left the
  browser as well — two in transit together, 16 runs of 16 (the first
  addressed to the page the visitor had gone back to, the second to the
  bike's). Delivered in the order they were sent, the bike ended at 42 (8 of
  8). Delivered the other way round — the 42 let through first, then the 45
  — it ended at 45, and the page ended saying so: the field read 45 and the
  read-only line "45 mm", under a status still reading "Enregistré" from the
  42, with no notice (8 of 8). The reversal was MADE, by holding requests in
  Playwright; no network was seen to reorder two. `updateBikePartAction`
  applies two writes of one field in the order they reach the row's lock —
  its own test promises "one of them, whole"
  (`tests/integration/bikes.test.ts`) — and nothing in the client keeps a
  second write back until the first has answered. Whether it should is in
  `docs/backlog.md`, for a ruling: the price is a field that waits for as
  long as an earlier write of it hangs.
- Not covered either, by construction. The record is this document's: a
  write sent from another tab, or before a reload, is not on it. And it
  decides what is SENT, not what is shown: in the window above the field goes
  on reading 42 until the page has been fetched again.

**3. A comment contradicted the helper under it** (nit).
`tests/integration/bikes.test.ts` said of the twelve-edit case "sent in the
same tick … the last write is the only one left", above a helper that starts
the calls one event-loop turn apart and says why a same-tick `Promise.all`
cannot be used; and the one unlocked run recorded here lost nine edits of
twelve, not eleven [I, re-read in that run's log]. The comment now says what
the helper does — and so does finding 2's own line above, which said "in the
same turn" of that same run.

#### Third review (2026-10-10, the same day)

A verifier attacked the second review's repairs (`19ab8b8`) [R: Chromium 153

**1. The record of the form's own writes was keyed by the name of a SLOT,
not by the guest bike** (minor). The second review's repair keyed it by

document again: the demo's "Modifier ce vélo" (`forkDemoToLocal`, then

confirmed (`components/decision-tree/Summary.tsx`; read). `BikeWorkspace`
itself keys its viewer on that id. The record did not: the new bike inherited
what this document had written to the one before it.

- The sequence: guest bike A, 45 typed for the front tyre and the field left
  ("Enregistré"); the header's link home; the tree's link to the demo; a
  part; "Modifier ce vélo" — `/velo/local` now shows bike B, a new id, the

- [R] Chromium, the site's own controls only, one document: 4 runs of 4
  (both projects, two repeats). One of them, as logged: B's `updatedAt` was
  `…31.045Z`; after the field was entered and left, `…31.045Z` still, the
  status empty; after 43 and then 42 were typed and the field left,
  `…33.019Z`, the status "Enregistré", the width 42. The control, A not

  emptied in between, did not.
- [I] on `19ab8b8`: the reviewer's jsdom case failed and its control passed.
  The e2e case written for it failed 8 runs of 8 on a build of that commit
  (`desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium`, two languages, two repeats),
  each at `not one byte of the new bike moved`; its control passed 8 of 8;

- Harmless as far as it was pushed [R, and nothing found since]: what is
  written is the value the field shows and the bike already holds; nothing

- **What had kept it from showing**: `forgetWrites()` before every case of

  239, three of them a guest bike's]. And no case had two guest bikes:

- Repair: `BikeRepo.bikeParam` is gone and `BikeRepo.bikeKey()` names the
  BIKE — `demo`, a saved bike's UUID, or `local:` and the id of the guest

  `setAttribute` reads and writes back in the same turn. (Told AS WELL since
  the fourth review, and that last clause is why: a repo that writes back
  whatever it reads wrote one bike's text into another.) It is read with
  `peekLocalBikeId` (`lib/bike/local-bike.ts`), which parses the stored value
  as the read does and, unlike the read, REMOVES NOTHING when it does not
  parse: the question is asked when a field is left, and leaving a field must
  not be what deletes something from storage. Where the slot holds no bike
  the key is `null`, and a field of no bike is on no record at all
  (`fieldOf`, `sendTracked` and `isInDoubt` in `lib/bike/own-writes.ts`): the
  write is sent — the repo reads the same slot in the same turn, and answers
  `NOT_FOUND` — and nothing is kept of it, so that nothing can ever be kept
  under a name two bikes share. In the form, the two places that said
  `repo.bikeParam` say `repo.bikeKey()`, and nothing else of its code moved.
  The cost of asking [I, Node 24 on this machine, 2 000
  parses of each preset's stored bike, about 4 400 characters]: 0.05 ms a
  parse, at most twice each time an edited field is left or removed.
- [I] after, on a build of the repaired tree: that e2e case and its control,
  24 runs of 24 (both projects, two languages, three repeats). The ui and
  unit tiers of `components/bike` and `lib/bike`: 607 cases of 607. Without
  the reset, `PartsPanel.test.tsx` now fails 2 of 242, both a saved bike's
  (every case builds a server of its own behind the one fixed UUID);
  `BikeWorkspace.test.tsx` passes 50 of 50 and `PartInfo.test.tsx` 13 of 13.
  The reset stays — for those — and the cases written for this finding call

- Repair: **a guest repo is built for ONE bike.** `localBikeRepo(bikeId)` —
  and `bikeRepoFor("local", { bikeId })` — takes the id of the guest bike
  its caller is showing. It still reads the slot before every write, which
  is still what keeps two tabs editing the SAME bike from overwriting each
  other; but a payload that is not that bike is, to it, a bike that is gone:
  nothing is written and the answer is `NOT_FOUND`, exactly as for an empty
  slot. The removal's write therefore ends where every refused late write
  ends, in the notice naming the part and the field ("Pneu avant · Largeur
  du pneu : votre saisie n’a pas été enregistrée." — "Introuvable."), and
  an ordinary save by a form that has not yet heard its bike was replaced is
  refused under its own field. `BikeWorkspace` hands the repo
  `localBike.id`, the id its viewer is already keyed on. The text itself is
  LOST — as on `main`, with the difference that it is said: the bike it was
  typed for is no longer in storage, and there is nowhere to put it.
- `bikeKey()` says the same thing the write does: `local:` and the repo's
  own bike while that bike is the one in the slot, `null` once it is not —
  never the key of the bike that took its place, which is what it answered
  between the third review and this one. The form's record of its own
  writes is keyed by it and needed no change: a field of no bike is on no
  record (`lib/bike/own-writes.ts`), so a guest bike's record is only ever
  written by a form of that very bike. One test of the third review's
  repair was CHANGED for that, not added to: `repo.test.ts` held "a guest
  bike REPLACED in the slot … is another bike, to the very repo that was
  built over the first" by expecting that repo to name the SECOND bike. It
  now expects it to name none, and the second bike's own repo to name the
  second: what that case was for — the new bike not being taken for the old
  — is held either way, and the old expectation was this finding.
- **Which writers of the slot are bound, and why** — the rule being that no
  write made for guest bike A may land on guest bike B.
  - `setAttribute` and `setFit`, both, in the repo: the rule is the repo's
    and not one form's. `MeasurementForm` has no commit on removal, so the
    `storage` event cannot make it write; but a save pressed between another
    page's replacement and this page hearing of it was A's measurement in B
    [I, jsdom, the repaired form over a repo whose `setFit` follows the

    nothing had been typed for it; not looked for in a browser — the window
    is the delivery of one event]. The form now builds its repo for the bike
    its inputs were seeded from. Once it HAS been told, the inputs are
    re-seeded from the new bike (as they always were) and the repo is built
    again for it: a save from then on is the new bike's. That half is pinned
    by a case, so that it stays a decision.
  - There is no unbound guest repo left to build: `null` for the id (the fit
    form before storage is read, or with nothing stored) is a repo of no
    bike, and refuses every write without reading the slot.
  - NOT bound, because they are not writes for an existing bike: the two
    controls that store a NEW guest bike (`forkDemoToLocal`, the tree's
    summary). Replacing is what they are for; the tree asks first.
  - NOT bound, and not this change's to bind: **the guest import's clear**
    (`settleGuestImport` → `clearGuestKeys`, `lib/guest/schema.ts`). It goes
    through no repo: once the server has answered it removes `va:bike:local`

    A guest bike stored by another page during the import's round trip is

#### Verification of the fourth repair (2026-10-12), and what closed it (2026-10-13)

A verifier attacked the fourth repair (`c5d3b1c`, the documents at `9efff1f`)
