Nothing blocking, and nothing wrong in the product as it stands; three
things left, closed on 2026-10-13 by one commit that changes no product
behaviour [I].

**1. No committed test held that `BikeWorkspace` builds its guest repo for
the bike it SHOWS** (minor; a gap in the tests). The repair's line is
`repoBikeId = refKind === "local" ? (localBike?.id ?? null) : bikeId`. With
the id read from the slot when the body renders instead
(`peekLocalBikeId()`), [R] `tsc` and ESLint exit 0, the 630 ui and unit
cases of `components/bike` and `lib/bike` pass, the 48 guest cases of
`tests/e2e/part-panel.spec.ts` pass on a build of that tree — and bike A's
edit is written into bike B under "Enregistré". None of the committed
"not told yet" cases renders the body between the replacement and the write,
so each writes through the repo built at mount, which is right either way.
The verifier's probe renders it, by the one thing that rebuilds the panel
with no word from storage — the layout switching across 1024 px — and then
types into the rebuilt panel.

- Closed by adopting that probe into the fourth review's block of
  `BikeWorkspace.test.tsx`, both layouts (60 cases in the file): the bike
  replaced and no event, the layout switched — the field holding the typed
  45 is removed, which is refused and said, once —, then 46 typed in the
  rebuilt panel and the field left: "Introuvable." under it, and not one
  byte of B moved.
- [I, 2026-10-13] at `9efff1f`: 60 of 60. With that one line changed (and
  the import it needs): 2 failed, 58 passed — the two new cases, each at
  `expected 'Enregistré' to be 'Introuvable.'`. The file put back from a
  saved copy, `git diff --quiet` on it.

**2. The fit page: a card drawn for guest bike A saves into guest bike B
once the page was told** (minor; NOT introduced by the repair, and disclosed
by it as "read, not measured"). It is the boundary the repair pinned — once
this page is told, a save is the new bike's — seen from the page: the cards
are drawn by the server from `?spec=`, which names no bike, and stay.

- [R] jsdom, the form alone, on the repaired tree: the sag card of a
  full-suspension e-MTB, a rigid gravel bike stored by another page and the
  event dispatched, 25 typed and saved —
  `sag card still on screen=true; Save -> state="Enregistré" … fit={"sagPercent":25}`
  on the NEW bike, which has no suspension. The same probe over
  `MeasurementForm.tsx` and `repo.ts` of `258de61` printed the same line:
  unchanged by the repair.
- [R] Chromium, the built site, one run on each of the two projects: the fit
  page opened for A by its `?spec=`, another page of the same context taking
  the demo's copy —
  `after B replaced A the inseam field shows ""; 80 saved -> state="Enregistré" slot=<B> fit={"inseamCm":80,"saddleHeightMm":706}`.
- [R] the other half held: text typed for A before the page was told is
  dropped by the re-seed, silently, and never written to B; a save before
  the page is told is refused, "Introuvable.".
- Nothing changed in the product: it awaits the ruling the backlog entry
  already asks for. `docs/backlog.md` ("A guest bike replaced from another
  page…") now carries these measurements where it said "not measured", and
  says that — nothing in the steps needing a field to keep the focus — one
  person with two tabs gets there (reasoned from the probes' steps, which
  drove two pages of one context).

**3. A save refused because the bike was replaced is said under a field that
is removed a moment later** (nit). The un-told blur of finding 1 above reads
"Introuvable." under its field; the page is then told, bike A's workspace is
removed, and the line goes with it. [R, jsdom through the real workspace]:
`after the event: "Introuvable." on the page=false, status line="(no status
line)", toast calls=0, region=null`. The removal's refusal is the one
announced for twenty seconds (and, with the toast unavailable, in the
workspace's own region). The repair's commit title says "refused and said":
true of the removal, and of this path only until the page is told.

- A decision, now written where the form is described
  (`components/bike/PartEditForm.tsx`, "Said under a field that is about to
  go") and pinned: the committed case "the bike replaced and the event not
  delivered yet; 45 typed and the field LEFT" already expected no notice; it
  now also expects, after the event, no "Introuvable." anywhere on the
  page, nothing in the workspace's region, and bike B's field at 42 with an
  empty status [I: green at `9efff1f`]. Raising the notice on this path as
  well was the verifier's alternative, and was not taken: it changes what a
  refused save says. Not looked for in a browser — the window is the
  delivery of one `storage` event.

#### On CI: `45.` is a number to Linux Chromium (2026-10-13, fixed 2026-10-19)

**Symptom.** PR #27's first CI run (Actions run 38401020974) failed four
blocking jobs — `e2e (desktop-chromium)`, `e2e (mobile-chromium)`,
`e2e (mobile-landscape)`, `e2e (no-webgl)` — on one pair of cases, in both
languages and on the retry: "number text the browser cannot read, typed and
not left, deletes nothing when the next press is the parts tab", guest bike
and saved bike. Both stopped at `typeUnreadable`'s own check, before the case
had tested anything: `what the browser reports for "45."` — expected
`{ value: "", badInput: true }`, received `{ value: "45", badInput: false }`.

**Cause, measured.** The same Chromium, 153.0.8010.12, in the same locale
(`fr-FR`, the Playwright config's), reads the same keystrokes differently on
the two platforms. A scratch page with one `<input type="number">`, driven by
the repository's own `playwright-core` in CI's image
(`mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.63.0-noble`, `linux/amd64`) and on this Mac,
desktop and Pixel 7 contexts alike:

| Typed (after select-all)     | Linux, CI's image                     | macOS                     |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| `45.`                        | `value "45"`, `badInput false` — read | `""`, `true` — unreadable |
| `4e`                         | `""`, `true` — unreadable             | unreadable                |
| `-`                          | unreadable                            | unreadable                |
| `-`, the field emptied first | unreadable                            | unreadable                |
| `.`, the field emptied first | unreadable                            | unreadable                |

So the product is not what failed: on Linux `45.` is a readable 45, and what
leaving or removing the field commits for it there is 45 — rule 3 doing what
leaving the field would. The fixture was what assumed one engine: every case
of this section that says "`45.` is unreadable" was measured on macOS
Chromium, and that is still what it says.

**Fix** (test and documents only). The two cases ask the browser first —
`unreadableHere` in `tests/e2e/part-panel.spec.ts`, on a scratch page of the
same context, so the page under test is not touched — run their sequence only
for the texts this engine cannot read, record each one set aside as a
"readable in this browser" annotation, and fail when none is left: they can
no longer pass having typed nothing unreadable. What each case asserts of the
product is unchanged. On CI's Chromium the guest case now runs four of its
five texts and the saved-bike case types `4e`; on macOS, all five and `45.`.
`CLAUDE.md` (the part edit form's bullet) and `docs/backlog.md` ("A number
field left holding text the browser cannot read…") said `45.` was unreadable
without naming the engine; both now name it.

**Runs.** macOS, a build with the test hooks: the two cases on
`desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium`, FR and EN, `--retries=0`: 8 of 8.
CI's image through `npm run e2e:docker` (amd64, emulated on this Mac; the
repository's reproducer), the same two cases on the same two projects: 8 of 8. That they get past `typeUnreadable` there at all is the proof that `45.`
was set aside: typed, it stops the case at that check, as on PR #27.

**How to detect a regression.** A number-field case that types a fixed
string and asserts `badInput` will pass on one engine and stop on another;
type through `unreadableHere`, or assert the engine's verdict first, as these
two do.

### 5.9 One over-long answer hid a guest's whole to-fix list, and could block the import (found 2026-10-08, fixed 2026-10-13, the fix repaired after review the same day)

**Who measured.** **[V]** the verification of 2026-10-08, on a copy of
`origin/main` (`9ae46bf`): the real wizard, list and import in jsdom, and the
real `importGuestStateAction` on PostgreSQL — two passes, the second
re-running the first through the pages' own functions and correcting it where
noted. That copy is gone (a scratch directory, since emptied): what is quoted
from it is its recorded output, and nothing of it was re-run. **[I]** the
implementer, 2026-10-13, on this branch — whose list page, form, merge, list
action and loader were byte for byte `origin/main`'s before the fix
(`git diff origin/main` over those files: empty) — in jsdom, on PostgreSQL
16, and in Chromium 153.0.8010.12 (`desktop-chromium`, `mobile-chromium`) on
builds with the test hooks, one of the product files as they were before the
fix and one after. **[R]** the two reviewers of that fix, 2026-10-13, on its
commit, with probes and one-change mutations of their own — "Review of the
fix", at the end of this section. What they found also corrected sentences
of the section itself; each is marked where it stands.

**Ruling** (the maintainer's, before this was written): fix before the tag —
a per-entry filter when the list is read, the same filter when the import
collects it, and a 64-character limit on the inputs.

#### Symptom

A to-fix list line has a "Préciser la pièce" form. On the lines a gravel
bike's checkup produces it draws, as free-text boxes with no limit, the two
genuine text attributes — `pad-model` ("Référence des plaquettes") on the two
brake-pad lines and `hanger-model` ("Référence de patte") on the rear
derailleur — and the yes/no attributes it has no control for (`e-rated`,
"Chaîne pour VAE"; `clutch`; §5.6's O5).

- **A guest's list, demo bike or their own** [V, jsdom]: 64 characters typed
  are kept. At the 65th — typed, or pasted — the page becomes "Rien à acheter
  pour l'instant / La liste se remplit toute seule à la fin d'un contrôle.":
  0 cards, 0 `role="alert"`, the focus on `<body>`. The same after a reload.
  The card that held the field is gone with the others, so the value cannot
  be shortened; a line ticked on another card disappears with it ("1 sur 3
  fait" before, the empty state after). The bytes are all still in
  `localStorage`, and no screen shows them.
- **Later checkups do not bring it back** [V]: the wizard merges into the
  stored list and carries the answer —
  `[replace chain] [replace brake-pads-front] [replace brake-pads-rear pad-model:99ch]`
  after a second checkup, the same value under a third — and the page stays
  on its empty state. The new findings are never shown.
- **In a browser** [I, Chromium, the unfixed build]: the same.
  `tests/e2e/build-list-long-answer.spec.ts`, written for the fix, 12 runs of
  12 red (two projects, two languages): the four typing runs pass the
  assertion at 64 characters and fail at the next one, the list gone after
  the 65th key press; the eight runs that start from a storage already
  holding a pasted title fail at the first load.
- **A NUMBER field does it too** [I, Chromium, a probe outside the
  repository]. [V]'s first pass had this "not reproduced" — user-event
  rewrites a number field's value through `Number()`, 65 digits becoming 22
  characters — and its second had it in jsdom with the raw value. In the
  browser, on the unfixed build, "Épaisseur de garniture" at 64 digits:
  `cards=3 empty-state=0 stored={"pad-thickness":"64ch:…"}`; one more digit:
  `cards=0 empty-state=1 stored={"pad-thickness":"65ch:…"}`. `maxlength`
  does not apply to that field.
- **What a visitor pastes there** [V]: a product title (99 characters in the
  run; a shorter real one is 70) or its address (138). A visitor who follows
  the field's own help ("J04C") stays far below 64.
- **The import, 65 to 120 characters** [V, PostgreSQL]: accepted. The row
  holds the answer; the list's loader — the column's only reader — returns
  the line without it; and the line's next edit replaces the column (one
  click on its price tier: the row went from `{"pad-model":"<99 chars>"}` to
  `{"brand-tier":"entry"}`).
- **The import, 121 characters or more** [V, PostgreSQL]:
  `{"ok":false,"code":"VALIDATION"}` five times out of five, 0 bikes and 0
  lists written. `/import` shows "Certaines informations ne sont pas
  valides. Corrigez les champs signalés." and keeps the three keys;
  "Réessayer" sends a byte-identical payload. No field is flagged, and the
  one that could be corrected is on a page that shows nothing: that guest
  bike cannot be imported at all. [I, Chromium, the unfixed build]: the same
  journey as a test, sign-up included — 4 runs of 4 red, the "Votre vélo est
  enregistré" toast never shown.
- **A saved bike is not bricked** [V]: the server refuses the edit, the list
  stays, "Modification non enregistrée" is shown, and the field still holds
  the 99 characters.

The demo bike's list (`va:buildlist:demo`) has no import to leave by: for it
there was no way back in the application at all.

#### Root cause

One bound, written SIX times with two values, and missing where the value
is made (this said "five times" until the review: the action's own reader,
the fifth row below, was the copy nobody had counted — or tested):

| Where                                                               | Before 2026-10-13                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| the form's inputs (`RefinementForm.tsx`)                            | no limit                                           |
| the guest list's writer (`writeBuildList`)                          | writes what it is given                            |
| the guest list's parser (`parseBuildList`)                          | 64 — inside the item schema, so it failed the LIST |
| the wizard's merge (`mergeGuestBuildList`)                          | copies the previous line's refinement as stored    |
| the saved bike's action, its schema (`liste/actions.ts`)            | 64 — a `VALIDATION`                                |
| …and its reader for `/acheter?item=` (`storedRefinement`, the same) | 64 — drops the entry                               |
| the saved bike's loader (`liste/load.ts`)                           | 64 — drops the ENTRY, keeps the line               |
| the guest's `/acheter?item=` prefill (`item-prefill.ts`)            | 64 — drops the entry                               |
| the import's collector (`collectGuestState`)                        | forwards what is stored                            |
| the import's contract (`GuestStateSchema`)                          | 120 — refuses the PAYLOAD                          |

The loader had the right rule: a bad entry costs that entry. The parser
applied the same number as a schema check, where a failure is the whole
value's — `BuildList.tsx`'s own comment on `chosenProduct`, two lines below,
says "loses the PRODUCT, not the list" and does it by NOT checking the
product in the schema. The collector had the same flaw twice over: it
forwarded the value to a contract that refuses whole, and its own guard on
the stored list typed `refinement` as a record of strings, so one entry that
was not a string failed the parse of the stored list and the bike was
collected with no list at all [I, unit: three cases of
`lib/guest/schema.test.ts` red on the unfixed files, the list `undefined`]
— a list whose key `settleGuestImport` then names among those to clear
(read).

`BuildList.test.tsx` had the parser, the list and the form, each fed a
hand-written line with a refinement nobody had typed; `RefinementForm.test.tsx`
drove `<select>`s. No test typed into a text field of a list.

#### The fix

**One cap, one reader** — `lib/shop/refinement.ts`, plain TypeScript, no zod
and no import (it is in four bundles, the chunk `/import` loads among them):

- `MAX_REFINEMENT_VALUE_LENGTH` = 64, the length of one answer everywhere an
  answer is bounded;
- `refinementOf(stored)`: the entries that are an answer — a string of at
  most the cap under a key of at most 64 — and `undefined` when there is
  none. It never fails: the loader's rule, word for word, now shared. (That
  was the reader of the fix's commit. Since the review the key must also be
  one the list's action takes — `isRefinementKey` — which is no longer the
  loader's rule word for word: finding 2 below.)

**Where it is applied:**

- _The form._ A free-text input carries `maxLength` (the yes/no attributes
  drawn as a text box included — a proper control for those is the
  backlog's); text that reaches the handler past the attribute is cut the
  same way, never between the halves of a surrogate pair
  (`clipRefinementText`); and `set` hands up nothing longer than the cap,
  which is the wall of a number field — a number cut short would be another
  number, so the 65th digit is simply not taken, and the controlled field
  goes on showing what is stored.
- _The guest list's parser._ `refinement` is no longer checked by the item
  schema; it is read by `refinementOf`, like `chosenProduct` by
  `chosenProductOf`.
- _The merge._ Every previous line's refinement goes through the reader
  before it is carried — derived again or a survivor of an earlier run — so
  a list the wizard writes holds no entry the page drops.
- _The loader and the prefill._ Their own copies of the rule are gone; they
  call the reader. What a saved bike's page is given has not changed. (One
  copy was left, and this note did not know of it: the action's own reader,
  behind the SAVED bike's prefill. Finding 1 below.)
- _The action._ The same two numbers, by name. What it accepts has not
  changed.
- _The import's collector._ `collectRefinement`: the reader first — what the
  list page would not show is not sent — then the contract's own two schemas
  and its cap on entries, so nothing in a line's refinement can sink the
  payload (a key the contract does not take, a U+0000). Its guard on the
  stored list takes `refinement` as it is.
- _The import's contract._ The value's cap is the constant: 64, where it
  said 120.

**What a bad value of a stored line costs**, field by field (the header of
`BuildList.tsx` has the table; `BuildList.long-answer.test.tsx` holds every
row). The rule: a value the visitor gives its FORM to — text typed or pasted,
a number's digits — costs, at most, itself; a value only the application
gives a form to may cost the list. (It read "what only the application
writes may cost the list; what the visitor types or picks costs, at most,
what they typed" until the review, and was untrue of the tick: finding 3.)

| A stored line's…                                                                                                      | …bad value costs        |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| one `refinement` entry: not a string, longer than the cap, or under a key that is not an attribute key                | that ENTRY              |
| `refinement` that is not an object                                                                                    | that line's REFINEMENT  |
| `chosenProduct` whose link breaks §4.4                                                                                | the PRODUCT (unchanged) |
| `partId` this release has no part for                                                                                 | the LINE (unchanged)    |
| the lines after the 80th                                                                                              | those LINES (unchanged) |
| `id`, `stepKey`, `sourceKeys`, `action`, `reasonKey`, `guideSlug`, `sortOrder`, a `partId` that is not a short string | the LIST (unchanged)    |
| `done`, `doneReason` — which the list page's own tick writes too                                                      | the LIST (unchanged)    |
| the envelope: not JSON, an unknown `version`, a bad `updatedAt`, `items` not an array                                 | the LIST (unchanged)    |

The last three rows were left as they were on purpose. The first of them is
the checkup's own output; softening it to "the line" would let a tab of an
older release show the rest of a list written by a newer one and, at its
next edit, write it back without the lines it could not read. `done` and
`doneReason` are NOT only the checkup's — the fix's own version of this
paragraph, and of the header it describes, said they were — and stand on a
narrower ground: the visitor decides when a line is ticked and never what is
stored for it (a boolean, and `manual` or nothing; the merge's word is
`recheck-ok`). That "only the application gives them a form" is a claim
about the writers, so it is held against everything each of them can write:
the FULL plan of each of the seven presets, every question answered KO with
no symptom picked, derived, written by `writeGuestBuildList` and read back
whole [I: 7 cases]; since the review, the same lists closed line by line by
a later checkup's merge [7 cases], and the tick made, unmade and cleared
through the real page [2].

**Three decisions, and why:**

- _A read never writes._ A dropped entry stays in `localStorage` until
  something writes the list. Rewriting on read would make a page that is
  only looked at delete bytes — in a tab left open across a release that
  reads them differently, for one — and a read that removes is how a
  checkup the importer could not use used to vanish (§5.6).
- _Dropped, not cut._ A reader that shortened a stored answer would show
  the visitor something they did not type, and half a number is another
  number. The ruling says filter. The CONTROL is where text is cut, and
  there it is what `maxLength` does to a paste.
- _The contract's cap is the reader's, and a tab loaded before the fix is
  refused from 65 characters._ Such a tab's collector forwards the stored
  answer. From 65 to 120 its payload used to be accepted, leaving in the
  column an answer no screen shows; it is now a `VALIDATION` that writes
  nothing and keeps every key, as an answer over 120 always was, until a
  reload brings the collector that drops the entry and the same browser is
  imported. Accepting it and dropping the entry on the server was the other
  option: it would have kept two numbers on the wire for a tab that — if it
  can reach the new action at all, which was not observed — is one reload
  from working. It is `GUEST_STATE_VERSION` 2's reasoning (§5.6).

#### What becomes of browsers, and rows, already affected

- **A hidden list is visible again at its next load after the deploy, with
  nothing done by the visitor** — demo bike and guest bike alike. Every
  line, with its tick, its chosen product and its other answers; the field
  the long answer was typed in is empty [I: jsdom at 65, 99, 138 and 5 000
  characters, both bikes; Chromium with a 99-character title, both bikes,
  both projects, both languages].
- **The long answer itself is lost to the visitor, and nothing says so.**
  It was shown nowhere before either. Its bytes stay in `localStorage`
  until the list is next WRITTEN — the read leaves the key byte for byte
  [I, jsdom and Chromium] — and leave with:
  - the next edit on the list page — a tick, an answer, "Retirer ce qui est
    fait" — which writes the lines as read [I, jsdom and Chromium];
  - the next finished checkup, whose merge reads what it carries [I, jsdom,
    through the wizard's own write];
  - or, for a guest bike, the import: the answer is not sent, and the list's
    key is cleared once the bike is in the account [I, PostgreSQL through
    the real action at 99, 138 and 5 000 characters; Chromium, sign-up
    included, at 138].
    For a demo bike whose visitor never edits and never runs another checkup
    they stay, inert: nothing reads them into a screen and nothing sends them.
- **Neither the next checkup nor the next edit hides the list again**
  [I: the two cases above assert the list on screen afterwards, and after a
  reload].
- **A guest bike that could not be imported now is**, at the first attempt,
  with its checkup and all its lines; the line that held the long answer
  keeps its other answers, and is an ordinary line of the account — its
  reference typed again, short, is saved beside them [I, PostgreSQL].
- **An ACCOUNT that imported an answer of 65 to 120 characters before the
  fix** still holds it in the row. Nothing changed for it: the loader drops
  the entry, the page shows the line, and the line's next edit replaces the
  column [I, PostgreSQL: `tests/integration/build-lists.test.ts`, a row
  written with 99 characters]. Whether such a row exists on production was
  not queried.
- **A tab loaded before the deploy** still runs the old page: its list stays
  hidden until a reload. If it can reach the new import action — not
  observed — an answer of 65 characters or more is refused, as above.

#### Seen on the way, and left as found

- **An edit on the guest list forgets which checkup wrote it.** The page's
  envelope is `{ version, items, updatedAt }`: the `checkupId` the wizard
  wrote is not written back [I, jsdom: the keys of the stored value after
  one edit; [V] saw the same]. The merge takes a missing id for a LATER
  checkup's list (read in `lib/checkup/storage.ts` and `Wizard.tsx`), so a
  re-run of the same checkup after any edit on the list keeps the lines it
  would otherwise prune. Not measured end to end. `docs/backlog.md`.
- **An imported hand tick loses its reason.** The payload carries `done` and
  no `doneReason`: a line ticked by the guest is `done` with a NULL reason
  on the account [I, PostgreSQL, seen while writing the import's case]. The
  page shows a reason only for `recheck-ok`, so nothing visible changes.
- **The dropped answer is not said**, on the list page or by the import.
  `docs/backlog.md`, with the yes/no attributes still drawn as a text box.
- **A line's OTHER fields can still sink the import.** The collector of the
  fix's commit checked a line's refinement against the contract and nothing
  else of the line: a stored `chosenProduct` the contract refuses was still
  a payload refused whole — and one that was not five strings sent the bike
  with no list at all, which this bullet did not say. The review measured
  both and the product is now collected like the refinement (finding 5
  below). The REST of the line is still forwarded as stored, and measured
  there. "Every line the wizard writes passes" was cited here to the seven
  presets "above" — a block that reads those lines through the list PAGE's
  parser and says nothing of the import's contract. The claim was true
  [R: a probe], and is now a test of its own (finding 7). `docs/backlog.md`.

#### Tests, and what each was seen to fail on

168 Vitest cases and 4 browser tests (each in FR and EN), all [I] — the
counts are those of the fix's commit (`716badf`); the review that followed
added cases to most of these files, and counts them in its own section:

- `lib/shop/refinement.test.ts` (30) — the cap, the reader, the cut.
- `components/build-list/BuildList.long-answer.test.tsx` (71) — the REAL
  `BuildList` and `RefinementForm` over a storage the wizard's writers
  filled, demo bike and guest bike: typing past the cap, pasting a title,
  pasting into a field that holds something, text set past the attribute, a
  number field given 65 digits; a storage already holding 65, 99, 138 and
  5 000 characters, then the next edit, the reference typed again, the next
  checkup; the cost table, row by row; the seven presets' full plans; a
  saved bike; and the helper that stands for the list page's write
  (`listPageEdit`) held to the real page.
- `components/build-list/RefinementForm.test.tsx` (+5) — what the form hands
  up, recorded.
- `lib/checkup/build-list.test.ts` (+8) — the merge.
- `lib/guest/schema.test.ts` (+20) — the collector at 64, 65, 99, 120, 121,
  138 and 5 000 characters, an entry that is not a string, a refinement
  that is not an object, what the contract would refuse, the count, a value
  nested 20 000 levels deep; and the contract at the cap and one over.
- `tests/unit/checkup/list-refinement.test.ts` (19) — the loader on a row
  holding each of those; the readers and the contract at 64 and 65; and
  every question's key, every option's value and every part's number of
  questions, for all seven presets, against the contract's own bounds.
- `tests/ui/guest-import.test.tsx` (+6) — the real `/import` page over such
  a storage, its action answering with the real contract's verdict.
- `tests/security/guest-import.test.ts` (+2) — a hand-made payload over the
  cap is refused whole with no attempt spent; at the cap it is stored.
- `tests/integration/guest-import.test.ts` (+5) and
  `tests/integration/build-lists.test.ts` (+2) — PostgreSQL: the real import
  action, the rows, the loader; a tab of before the fix; the action at the
  cap; a row that already holds an over-long answer.
- `tests/e2e/build-list-long-answer.spec.ts` (3 tests) and one more in
  `tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts` — a guest on the demo bike makes a list in
  the real wizard, types 80 characters, pastes a 99-character title and
  types 70 digits into the number field, reloading in between; a browser
  seeded with what production's browsers hold, demo and guest; and that
  browser's import, sign-up included, rows read from PostgreSQL. The paste
  is made twice: as text inserted without key presses, which is the
  assertion every browser makes, and then — in Chromium — off the real
  clipboard with Ctrl/Cmd+V. Where a browser refuses the clipboard write the
  test records it in an annotation and does not make that second paste; on
  this machine it was made in every run (the four Chromium projects, both
  languages: 8 of 8, no annotation). A Linux runner was not tried.

Two existing cases were CHANGED, not added to, because the contract became
stricter: `lib/guest/schema.test.ts` "caps a line's refinement" and
`tests/security/guest-import.test.ts`'s `refined()` built their values 120
characters long, as "exactly at the cap". They are now built at the constant,
and each file refuses 65 and 120 by name beside it.

**The product files as before the fix, every other file as after** [I]:

- the fast tiers of the folders above: 79 failed, 719 passed. The 79 are
  new cases, and the changed contract case;
- the two integration files: 4 failed, 73 passed;
- the browser: 12 of 12 and 4 of 4, as under "Symptom".

What does NOT fail on the unfixed files, and says so where it stands: the
"unchanged" rows of the cost table and their control; the seven presets; the
helper's pin; the loader's cases and the row that already holds an answer (a
saved bike's read did not change — they fail when the loader stops reading);
the module's own cases; the catalogue's; and "exactly at the cap" wherever
it is asserted.

**Mutations**, one change each unless said, the eight product files put
back from a saved copy and their sha256 compared; fast tiers, 798 cases:

| Mutation                                                                 | Failing            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------ |
| the parser refuses the list for one over-long answer again               | 24                 |
| the parser hands the stored refinement on unread                         | 24                 |
| the text inputs carry no `maxLength`                                     | 4                  |
| text past the attribute is not cut                                       | 3                  |
| `set` stores whatever it is given                                        | 3                  |
| those three together — the form of before, the readers fixed             | 18                 |
| the merge copies the previous lines as stored                            | 9                  |
| the collector forwards the stored refinement                             | 24 (+3 PostgreSQL) |
| the collector applies the page's reader only, not the contract's schemas | 2                  |
| the collector sends any number of answers                                | 1                  |
| the collector's guard reads `refinement` as a record of strings again    | 7                  |
| the collector makes text well-formed BEFORE reading it                   | 1                  |
| the collector applies the contract only, not the page's reader           | **0**              |
| the contract takes 120 characters again                                  | 4 (+1 PostgreSQL)  |
| …and the collector applies the contract only                             | 10                 |
| the one cap is 120                                                       | 33                 |
| the one cap is 63                                                        | 5                  |
| the reader keeps an over-long answer                                     | 40                 |
| the reader answers `{}` for "none"                                       | 7                  |
| the cut may split a surrogate pair                                       | 1                  |
| the loader hands the column on unread                                    | 7 (+1 PostgreSQL)  |
| the prefill hands the stored refinement on unread                        | 1                  |
| the action takes 120 characters                                          | 2 (PostgreSQL)     |
| the action takes 63 characters                                           | 1 (PostgreSQL)     |

The one that fails nothing is not a gap in the tests: with one cap, every
entry the page's reader drops is one the contract's two schemas refuse (a
value over 64; a key over 64 is over the contract's 40), so the reader's
step changes no payload. It is there for the day the two numbers part —
the row under it is that day, produced: the contract at 120 alone fails 4,
and with the collector also holding entries to the contract alone it fails
10, the six more being the cases in which an answer of 65 to 120 characters
is then SENT — four of the collector's, and the import page's two at 99.

**First-load JS**, builds without the test hooks — the build `scripts/ci.sh`
makes — at `9efff1f` (measured on 2026-10-12 by the fourth repair's

**The gates on the fix's commit (`716badf`)** [I, 2026-10-13]: `bash

#### Review of the fix (2026-10-13, the same day)

Two reviewers attacked the fix above on its commits (`716badf` to `554c634`)
[R: jsdom through the real `BuildList` and `/import` page, the real import
and list actions on PostgreSQL 16, Chromium on a build with the test hooks,
and one-change mutations of their own]. Eleven findings: two pairs were the
same defect seen by both reviewers, and two findings about false sentences
are grouped under one number here, which leaves the eight below. Each was
reproduced by the implementer [I] on `554c634` before anything was changed.
All were real; none was rejected. None is a way to hide a list or block an
import by TYPING. What was wrong was one reader the fix had not found, one
rule it had not looked at, a cost this note and the code's header stated on
a ground that was untrue, a citation to a test that did not hold the claim,
and tests that could not see their own fixture.

**1. One reader of a stored refinement did not call the reader — and no test
held its bound** (major). `storedRefinement`, in `liste/actions.ts`, is what
`loadBuildListItemAction` reads the column with: the SAVED bike's
`/acheter?item=` prefill. The fix replaced its two literals by the constants
and left the filter written out, under a CLAUDE.md that said in bold "every
reader of a stored refinement calls `refinementOf`" and a count — "five
literals, four said 64" — that was one short on both numbers.

- [R] its bound set to 5 000 characters: every Vitest tier green (3 825 fast
  cases, 458 on PostgreSQL). The column returned unfiltered: the tiers
  around it green (682 cases, and 23 on PostgreSQL).
- [I] the first of those again on `554c634`: the fast tiers around the
  change (746 cases) and the two PostgreSQL files (77), all green.
- Repair: the function is `refinementOf(value)` and then at most 24 entries
  — the action's own bound on a write, applied to its answer, and its only
  rule of its own. (Corrected 2026-10-22: that count belonged in the reader
  every page reads with, not in this one alone — "After the merge", at the
  end of this section. The function is `refinementOf(value)` and nothing
  else now.) The row is written past every guard and read through the
  action, and through the loader for the same answer: an answer over the
  cap, at it, 5 000 characters; values that are not strings; keys the action
  refuses; a column that is not an object; 30 entries — 8 cases in the
  security tier (the stand-in database) and 9 on PostgreSQL. The mutation
  table below has what each of the two rules costs when it is taken out.

**2. The import took two keys the account's own action refuses: a line
imported with one could never be edited again** (minor; hand-written storage
only). The contract held a refinement key to the catalogue's shape,
`[a-z0-9-]{1,40}`, which `constructor` and `prototype` pass; the action has
always refused both by name. The list's form sends back the WHOLE refinement
it was handed with one answer changed.

- [R, PostgreSQL, real import and real action] a guest's rear pads holding
  `{"constructor":"x","prototype":"y","pad-thickness":"2"}`: imported, the
  page handed all three keys, and the next answer on that line
  `{"ok":false,"code":"VALIDATION"}` — "Modification non enregistrée", for
  good. A tick on the same line: accepted.
- [I, PostgreSQL] the same storage through the real import on the product
  files of `554c634`: the row's keys are `prototype`, `constructor`,
  `pad-thickness`; a payload carrying `constructor` by hand: accepted.
- Repair, in three places that are now one definition (`isRefinementKey`,
  `lib/shop/refinement.ts`: the shape, 1 to 64 characters, and none of
  `__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`):
  - the CONTRACT asks it (and keeps its own 40 characters), so the collector
    — which holds each entry to the contract's schemas — no longer sends
    such an entry, and a payload that carries one by hand is refused whole;
  - the ACTION asks it, in place of the pattern and the three names it wrote
    out. What it accepts has not changed: its cases were not touched;
  - and the READER asks it. This is the one place the repair goes past the
    finding, and it changes what a saved bike's page is handed: the loader
    kept any key of at most 64 characters, and now drops an entry under a
    key the action would refuse. The reason is the one the fix gave for
    VALUES — what a reader hands the page comes back with the next edit —
    and without it a row the old contract let in stays a line nobody can
    edit. [I, PostgreSQL] a row written past every guard with
    `constructor`, `prototype` and `speeds`: the loader returns
    `{"speeds":"11"}`, the form's next edit is accepted, and the column is
    replaced. No other row the application can have written is read
    differently: the action and the contract have always held a key to the
    shape (read).
- The two readers of the saved bike's column had also disagreed about such a
  row — the loader handed the names on, the action's own reader dropped them
  [R]. They are one function now.

**3. The cost table's ground for `done` and `doneReason` was untrue** (major
as a statement; no value found that it mis-costs). The table and the header
of `BuildList.tsx` filed both under "written by `deriveBuildList` and the
merge and by nothing else". The list page's own checkbox writes both
(`BuildItemCard.tsx`), through the same `writeBuildList` as an answer, and
the block that was said to hold "only the application writes them" wrote
`deriveBuildList`'s output only: `false`, never a reason.

- [R] no value the control can write is refused by the parser: ticked and
  reloaded, in jsdom and in Chromium, the list stays.
- Decided, on the true ground, to leave the cost where it was: the visitor
  decides WHEN a line is ticked and never WHAT is stored — a checkbox yields
  a boolean, the page writes `manual` beside it or no reason at all, and the
  merge's word is `recheck-ok`. There is no ill-formed tick to make, unlike
  an answer, whose length was the visitor's. Reading the two fields
  leniently was the other option: it would let a tab still on this parser
  show a list holding a reason a LATER release invented, and write it back
  without it — the trade the fix already refused for `action`. The header
  now says what that costs (such a tab shows no list) and what to do about
  it (read a new word leniently one release before anything writes it).
- The rule above the table is restated to match — "a value the visitor
  gives its FORM to" — and each writer of the two fields is now run and read
  back: the merge closing every line of the seven presets' full lists (7
  cases), and the tick made, unmade and cleared through the real page, both
  bikes (2). The mutation table below has the tick, and then the merge, made
  to write a word the parser does not know.

**4. No recovery case checked that its storage held the long answer**
(minor). "A browser that ALREADY holds an over-long answer" is made by
`listPageEdit`, and the only thing holding that helper to the page compared
them on "J04C" — an answer the FIXED page writes too.

- [R] the helper changed to read each line as the fixed page does before
  writing: 728 fast cases and 77 on PostgreSQL green, over a storage that
  held no long answer at all. Only the browser tier noticed (6 failed).
- Repair: the precondition is asserted where each fixture is made — in
  `BuildList.long-answer.test.tsx`, `lib/guest/schema.test.ts`,
  `tests/ui/guest-import.test.tsx` and
  `tests/integration/guest-import.test.ts` — and the pin has a second half:
  the helper writes WHATEVER the edit returns (an answer, a product and a key
  the fixed page cannot write) and leaves the other lines as they were.
  [I] under the same change of the helper: the last row of the mutation
  table below.

**5. A stored `chosenProduct` could still cost the guest's WHOLE list at
import** (minor; no control of the site writes one). The same flaw as the
refinement's, one field down the same guard, and the backlog entry that
deferred it called it "read" and did not say the key was cleared.

- [R, jsdom] `chosenProduct` of `null`, `{brand:"Shimano"}` and a string, on
  one of the wizard's three lines: the list page shows 3 lines, the payload
  carries no list, and a successful import clears `va:buildlist:local`. A
  product whose link breaks §4.4: 3 lines sent, the payload refused whole.
- [I] the same through the collector's new cases on the product files of
  `554c634` (11 fail: 6 with no list collected, 5 with the product
  forwarded), and on PostgreSQL through the real action: a broken link is
  `{"ok":false,"code":"VALIDATION"}`; `null` and a product that is not five
  strings import the bike with 0 lists and 0 lines.
- Repair, although the ruling did not ask for it — the cost was a list the
  visitor could see, removed: `collectChosenProduct` reads the product with
  the list page's own reader (`chosenProductOf`) and then holds it to the
  contract's schema, and the guard on the stored list takes the field as it
  is. A product that fails costs the PRODUCT; the line is sent without it,
  as the page shows it without it. The reader goes first and is not
  redundant: it keeps a product's five fields and leaves a sixth, which the
  contract — a strict object — would refuse the product for.
- NOT repaired: the rest of a line (`partId`, `action`, `reasonKey`,
  `guideSlug`, `done`, `sortOrder`). [I, a probe outside the repository,
  jsdom] a `sortOrder` of −1 or 10 001, a `reasonKey` or `guideSlug` that is
  not an id: the page shows its 3 lines and the payload is refused whole; a
  `done` that is not a boolean, a `guideSlug` or `sortOrder` of `null`, an
  unknown `action`: the page shows nothing and the bike is sent with no
  list. `docs/backlog.md` has the table, and why dropping a LINE at the
  import waits for a decision.

**6. The catalogue's keys were checked against a copy of the contract's
pattern** (nit). `tests/unit/checkup/list-refinement.test.ts` held "every
question's key … against the contract's own bounds" with a private
`/^[a-z0-9-]{1,40}$/`. [I] the contract's key cut to 5 characters: the seven
preset cases of the block as it was all pass; as it is now — each part's
whole form answered on a line of a real payload and handed to
`parseGuestState`, and each key asked of `isRefinementKey` — all seven fail.

**7. "Every line the wizard writes passes" the import's contract was cited
to a test of the list page's parser** (minor). Here ("Seen on the way") and
in the backlog. The claim was true [R: a probe; I: 43 to 48 lines a preset
against the contract's 50, all accepted] and nothing committed held it. It
is `lib/guest/schema.test.ts`'s now: the seven presets' full plans,
everything broken, stored by the wizard's writers, collected, accepted whole
— 7 cases, which fail [I] when the contract's `sortOrder` stops at 10.

**8. Three sentences that were false** (minor, nit): the backlog's yes/no
entry named a test that names no such box, and listed five attributes where
the form draws SIX as a text box — `quick-release`, on the seat clamp, was
missing [I: a probe over every part of the seven presets; the entry has the
six and their parts]; and a comment of `lib/guest/schema.test.ts` gave
"123 KB" for 1 400 entries that weigh 154 001 bytes [I: counted; the case
now asserts it, and 232 401 for the same entries at 120 characters].

**What a saved bike's pages are handed after this review**, against G5 of

every row the list's own action wrote, and for every VALUE; changed for an

(finding 2). Every pre-existing case of the nine test files this review
touched passes on the repaired product [I: each file's `554c634` version run
against today's product files — 375 cases, 0 failed].

**Tests.** 70 Vitest cases added in the fast tiers and 16 on PostgreSQL,
and a precondition asserted inside five existing fixtures; no existing case
removed, skipped, or asked for less. The existing cases touched beyond that,
and how: in `lib/guest/schema.test.ts`, the row "nothing but a key the
contract does not take" was `{ Speeds: "11" }` under a comment saying the

reworded to what they now hold, their assertions as they were.
With the seven product files as at `554c634` — three changed in their code,
four in a comment — and the tests as they are now [I]: 55 of 816 fail in the
fast tiers, 33 of them only because `isRefinementKey`, which they call, did
not exist, and 7 of 93 on PostgreSQL.

The reader's key rule has one more guard, because of what it would cost if
it were ever wrong: an attribute declared with a key outside the shape would
have its answer written by the form and dropped by every reader. So the
CATALOGUE is asked, not only the forms of seven bikes: every attribute key
of every part, and the price tier's, is `isRefinementKey` and is taken by
the contract (`tests/unit/checkup/list-refinement.test.ts`).

#### After the merge: the count the list's loader did not apply (V1-1, closed 2026-10-22)

The verification of the repaired fix left one minor finding, V1-1, after
PR #27 was merged. **[V2]** below is that verification, quoted from its
record (its probe file is gone with its scratch directory: nothing of it was
re-run); **[I2]** is the implementer of 2026-10-22, on `0ad0982` and after.

- **The defect** [V2, PostgreSQL, the real loader and actions]: a line's
  column written past the guards with N answers, every key an attribute key,
  then what the list's form sends on the next edit — everything it was
  handed, one answer changed. 24: the loader hands 24, the edit is accepted.
  25: the loader hands 25, every edit is `TOO_MANY` until one answer is
  cleared. 30: the loader hands 30, the edit is `TOO_MANY` and so is clearing
  one; a tick is accepted. The action refuses more than 24 answers; the
  reader the page is handed by had no count; the action's OWN reader had one
  — finding 1's repair above put it there — and its answer is never sent
  back. The repair's comment in `tests/integration/build-lists.test.ts`
  pinned the loader as unbounded, on the ground that "no path of the
  application writes a 25th": true of the branch, not of production's import
  contract, which had no cap on a refinement's entries until PR #27.
- **Reach** [V2, read]: hand-written storage only — no form produces more
  answers than a part has questions — imported before PR #27. Not looked for
  on production.
- **Repair** [I2]: ONE count, `MAX_REFINEMENT_ENTRIES` (24), in
  `lib/shop/refinement.ts`, where the 24 was written twice (`liste/actions.ts`
  and `GUEST_CAPS.refinementEntries`, which now IS the constant). The action
  refuses past it as before; `refinementOf` hands over no more than it, so
  the loader, the prefill readers, the guest list's parser, the merge and the
  import's collector all stop there (the collector's own slice went with
  it); the action's reader is `refinementOf` and nothing else. **Which 24**:
  the first by KEY, in code-unit order, never the store's first 24 — a
  `jsonb` column hands keys back shortest first and `localStorage` in the
  order they were written, so the same entries must give the same answers in
  both; the stored order is kept among the survivors, and a row at or under
  the count is read exactly as before. No better choice is in the reader's
  reach: it knows no part, so it cannot tell an answer the form shows from
  one it does not. What it still cannot do: a hand-written row of 24 or more
  answers none of which the form shows refuses a NEW answer (25 sent) until
  a shown one is cleared — the action's count working as designed, on a row
  the action itself would have accepted at 24.
- **Held** [I2]: on PostgreSQL, a 25- and a 30-answer row whose keys are
  ordered differently by the column and by key: the loader and the prefill
  action hand the same 24, the first by key; the form's next edit is
  accepted, and so is clearing one; reading wrote nothing. 24 written: taken;
  25: `TOO_MANY`. The old case now expects the loader's 24 to equal the
  action's. In the fast tiers: the count, a row at it read whole and in its
  order, 25 / 30 / 400 answers read as the first 24 by key and the same in
  the column's order, dropped entries never taking a place; the security
  tier's 30-answer case compares the two readers.
- **Seen failing** [I2]: the reader's count removed — 7 cases fail (3 fast,
  1 security, 3 on PostgreSQL); the reader keeping the store's first 24 —
  5 fail (3 fast, 2 on PostgreSQL).
- **What a saved bike's pages are handed now**: unchanged for every row of at
  most 24 answers; a row holding more is handed its first 24 by key. The
  column itself is not rewritten by reading; the line's next edit replaces
  it.

### 5.10 A resumed partial checkup lost its stored answers (found 2026-10-08, ruled and fixed 2026-10-22)

**Who measured.** **[V]** the verification of 2026-10-08 (candidate N2: a
first agent and a challenger), on a copy of `origin/main` at `9ae46bf`:
the real page function, the real wizard in jsdom, the real actions and
PostgreSQL, quoted from its record — the copy and its test files are gone,
nothing of it was re-run. **[I3]** the implementer of 2026-10-22, on
`w5/partial-checkup`: the same harness written again for this tree
(`tests/integration/checkup-resume.test.ts`), the recording fake, and
Chromium 153 on a production build with the test hooks.

**Ruling** (the maintainer's, final): fix all variants before the tag —
resume on the page's scope, refuse an empty parts scope on the server, and
stop a partial page from trimming an unfinished full run. No schema change.

#### Symptom [V]

Signed in, a saved bike, "Contrôler les pièces sélectionnées", one answer
(stored correctly), then any new load of `/controle` while the run is
unfinished, and one more state change:

- **(a)** a reload on the same `?parts=` (the wizard keeps it in the address
  bar), one more answer: the save answered `{"ok":true}`, the chip said
  "Sauvegardé", and the run's `CheckupItem` rows went from 1 to 0. "Créer ma
  liste" then marked the checkup `COMPLETED` with 0 items, derived no line,
  wrote no part status, re-pointed `BuildList.checkupId` at the empty run and
  went to the list with no error.
- **(a2)** the reload, then "Question précédente" only: the same deletion.
- **(b)** back through the workspace's resume banner, whose link carries no
  `?parts=` for a saved bike: the page planned the FULL checkup, the wizard
  resumed with the empty scope, the same deletion; the run then stayed
  `PARTIAL` / `IN_PROGRESS` with 0 items and was resumed on EVERY later
  `/controle` URL, each answer answered ok and written nowhere, until it was
  finished (empty).
- **(c)** an unfinished FULL run (6 answers) opened under `?parts=`: the
  wizard reconciled it to the 4-question plan and posted `scope: full` with
  the 4 survivors; the server deleted the 3 others. Finished from there: FULL
  / COMPLETED with 4 of 34 answers. The same trimming in `localStorage` for a
  guest.
- **The guest import** [V, the challenger: measured with a hand-written
  payload, since the import carried no checkup then]: an unfinished PARTIAL
  checkup imported as `PARTIAL` / `IN_PROGRESS` with its items; the account's
  banner, one click, and its items — a typed note among them — were gone.
- Guests were not affected by (a) and (b): their scope lives whole in
  `localStorage`, and their banner carries `?parts=`.

#### Root cause [V, and read]

A `Checkup` row stores its scope as `FULL | PARTIAL` only, so
`loadStoredCheckup` returned a partial run with `scope: { kind: "parts",
partIds: [] }`. `restore()` (`components/checkup/Wizard.tsx`) resumed through
`fromStored(stored, props.steps, …)`: the PAGE's questions, the STORED scope,
posted back on every save and on the finish (`toStored`). Both actions
re-planned from the posted scope; a parts scope naming nothing plans no step
(`planCheckup`, pinned by `tests/unit/checkup/plan-corpus.test.ts`);
`acceptedState` then kept no answer, and `writeItems` ran
`checkupItem.deleteMany({ stepKey: { notIn: [] } })` — which Prisma 7.10.0
compiles to `AND 1=1` [V: the SQL recorded] — and created nothing. Variant
(c) is the same `restore()` the other way round: the stored scope was right
(full) and the page's plan narrower, so `reconcile` dropped the answers it
does not ask and the next save deleted their rows.

#### Fix

- **The server** (`controle/actions.ts`): a `parts` scope naming no part is
  refused, `VALIDATION`, before any query — the payload an open tab of the old
  bundle still posts; a payload is accepted WITH every stored answer of the
  run it does not carry (the wizard has no event that removes a verdict, so
  only a page that planned fewer questions, or a tab holding an older copy of
  the run, leaves one out), against the plan those answers bear out
  (`effectiveScope`: the named scope while every answered question is one it
  asks, the full checkup otherwise — refined in §5.12 to "while it holds every
  answer as it was given"); `Checkup.scope` is written from that
  on every save and finish; and a row is deleted only when the bike's FULL
  plan no longer asks its question — never "the rows the payload did not
  carry", and never on a plan that came back empty.
- **The page and the wizard**: the wizard resumes a stored run on the PAGE's
  scope, and the page plans a scope that holds the run (`resumeScope`, since
  §5.12 `resumeTarget`): its
  own `?parts=` when they ask every question the run answered and the run is
  not a full one with answers; the FULL checkup otherwise. A saved bike's page
  decides on the server; a guest's run is in `localStorage`, so the page hands
  the wizard the full plan's keys, and the wizard — without restoring, and
  without saving — leaves for `?resume=full`. A question the corpus dropped is
  not counted, so nothing loops. **Not enough, found in review the same day
  (§5.12)**: "asks every question" let a page resume a run whose questions it
  asks about OTHER parts, and the answers moved there; the rule is now that
  the page holds every answer as it was given, and a guest's run otherwise
  goes back to its own parts.
- **By a full navigation** (`components/checkup/replace-document.ts`). The
  first version used `router.replace`: in Chromium 153 the new page's flight
  answered 200 and the wizard stayed on "Reprise du contrôle en cours…" under
  the old header, the address unchanged — 4 runs of 4 of the two
  `guest-import.spec.ts` cases that take that path, on both projects [I3]; why
  that soft navigation did not commit was not found.
- **Kept on purpose**: `planCheckup` still plans nothing for a parts scope
  naming no part. Planned as the full checkup, a stale empty scope would ask —
  and, on the server, write — every question; the callers decide first (the
  page never builds one, the actions refuse one, `effectiveScope` reads one
  as "not confined", unplanned).

**What the visitor sees** — as of §5.13, which changed the saved bike's
banner, (c2) and full-page cells (§5.12 had changed the two (c2) rows and the
guest's full page):

| Sequence                                                     | Saved bike                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Guest                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| (a) reload on the same `?parts=`, (a2) "Question précédente" | "Contrôle partiel", the run where it was — its address carries `?run=`, its `startedAt`                                                                                                                                                                                               | the same                                                                                                                                                                |
| (b) the resume banner                                        | on the parts its rows tell, with "Un contrôle limité à : … est déjà en cours…"; when they cannot tell, "Contrôle complet" and "Ce contrôle avait commencé sur quelques pièces…", which names the answered questions that name several parts and opens no later than the first of them | the banner carries `?parts=`: as (a)                                                                                                                                    |
| (c) a full run with answers, under `?parts=`                 | "Contrôle complet" and "Un contrôle complet est déjà en cours…"                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | "Reprise du contrôle en cours…", then the same                                                                                                                          |
| (c2) a partial run, under parts that do not hold it          | back on the parts its rows tell, with the own-parts sentence — or the full checkup and the first notice when that checkup takes it over, or when the rows cannot tell                                                                                                                 | "Reprise…", then back on its own parts with "Un contrôle limité à : … est déjà en cours…" — on to the full checkup and the first notice when that checkup takes it over |
| (c2) a partial run, under parts that hold it                 | "Contrôle partiel", the run resumed on them — when its rows tell its parts and the page asks every answer as they did; otherwise as the row above                                                                                                                                     | only under the same parts: as (a)                                                                                                                                       |
| a partial run, the full checkup opened                       | as (b)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | "Reprise…", then as the first (c2) row                                                                                                                                  |

What this still costs is in `docs/backlog.md` ("A saved bike's partial
checkup does not remember its parts") and §5.13: a saved bike's partial run
whose rows cannot tell its parts, opened anywhere but on its own address,
carries on as the full checkup and counts its answers for every part their
question names — the notice says so, names the questions, and the wizard
shows the first of them again. Until §5.12, one reopened under another
selection sharing a question was resumed there, its answer then stored
against the side now picked, with nothing on screen — pinned here as "a
decision", which it was not: the ruling was to fix every variant. Until
§5.13, a saved bike's was carried on as the full checkup even when its row
said which side it was for, and held by any page whose parts contained its
rows' — a both-calipers finding narrowed to the front.

#### What production rows may look like (nothing is rewritten)

- `PARTIAL` / `IN_PROGRESS` runs with NO item — what (a), (a2), (b) and the
  import's banner left. They are the bike's run in progress: the next
  `/controle` resumes one on its scope (or as the full checkup), the first
  save writes its items and its column, and it finishes like any other —
  `tests/integration/checkup-resume.test.ts` holds one. Before the fix such a
  run captured every later checkup of its bike.
- `PARTIAL` / `COMPLETED` runs with no item, with `BuildList.checkupId`
  pointing at one: history, unchanged; the list kept its lines.
- `FULL` / `COMPLETED` runs holding fewer answers than were given (variant
  (c) finished).
- The deleted answers themselves: gone — a saved bike's run had no copy.

Candidates:
`SELECT c.id, c."bikeId", c.status, c."startedAt" FROM "Checkup" c WHERE c.scope = 'PARTIAL' AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "CheckupItem" i WHERE i."checkupId" = c.id)`.
Nothing needs doing to them.

#### Tests [I3]

- `tests/integration/checkup-resume.test.ts` (new): the real page function,
  the real wizard in jsdom, the real actions spied and PostgreSQL — the
  control (d), (a), (a2), (b), (c), (c2) both ways, the other side's caliper
  (pinned), an imported partial run resumed and finished as the same row, an
  emptied run saved and finished, the old tab's payload refused with nothing
  deleted, an old tab's trimmed full run keeping on the server what it no
  longer sends; and for a guest, in `localStorage`, (a), (c), (c2) and a
  stale scope of the old wizard (R1). 16 cases.
- `components/checkup/Wizard.test.tsx`: the resume rules and the notices,
  FR and EN, on hand-shaped plans.
- `tests/security/checkup-input.test.ts`: the empty scope refused before any
  query; no `deleteMany` when the plan comes back empty; a stored answer the
  payload does not carry kept, and one the bike is not asked deleted.
- `tests/integration/checkups.test.ts`: "removes the row of a question that
  lost one" pinned the deletion the defect used, and is now "KEEPS the row of
  a question the payload does not carry"; beside it, the one deletion left.
- `tests/e2e/checkup-resume.spec.ts` (new), FR and EN, a saved bike: the
  reload, the banner, a full run under `?parts=`, and the two cases of §5.11;
  `tests/e2e/guest-import.spec.ts`'s "carried on through another part" now
  goes through `?resume=full`, and asserts the first visit's question is
  still answered.

**Seen failing** [I3], one change each, the product file put back from a
saved copy; the four test files above, 92 cases:

| Mutation                                                              | Failing                      |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| the wizard resumes with the STORED scope again                        | 11 (9 PostgreSQL, 2 jsdom)   |
| the actions take a parts scope naming no part                         | 2 (1 PostgreSQL, 1 security) |
| the actions forget the stored answers a payload does not carry        | 1 (PostgreSQL)               |
| `writeItems` deletes what the payload did not carry, empty plan too   | 1 (security)                 |
| the page never widens a saved bike's run                              | 2 (PostgreSQL)               |
| the wizard never widens a guest's run                                 | 5 (3 PostgreSQL, 2 jsdom)    |
| the column is never rewritten after the row is created                | 3 (PostgreSQL)               |
| the three at once that made the defect (wizard, schema, `writeItems`) | 12                           |

**In Chromium** [I3], `desktop-chromium`, against a build of this tree with
the old behaviour put back — the wizard resuming on the stored scope, the
schema taking a scope naming no part, `writeItems` deleting what a payload
did not carry, neither the page nor the wizard widening, the planner handing
a kept step on whole: of the five cases of `tests/e2e/checkup-resume.spec.ts`
in French and English and `guest-import.spec.ts`'s "another part", 10 of 12
failed. The two that passed were the resume banner's, and the reload case
failed on its COLUMN (`FULL` where `PARTIAL` was due) with both rows kept —
for one reason worth knowing: that build's server still accepted a payload
against the plan its answers bear out, so the scope naming no part was read
as "not confined" and nothing was deleted. The server's half alone protects
those paths. With the server also planning the posted scope, as before the
fix, the banner's two failed as the verification saw it:
`Expected length: 2 … Received length: 0`. The build was then put back, its
files checked against the commit (`git status` clean), and rebuilt.

### 5.11 A checkup limited to some parts acted on parts outside its scope (seen 2026-10-07, ruled and fixed 2026-10-22)

**Who measured.** **[W]** the launch walk of 2026-10-07 (§5.6, observations
O3 and O6), quoted from its record. **[T]** the triage of those two
observations, on `9ae46bf`: read in the source, nothing run; its two agents'
corrections are folded in below. **[I3]** the implementer of 2026-10-22, on
`w5/partial-checkup` from `0ad0982`: jsdom, the recording fake, PostgreSQL 16
through the real actions, and a probe outside the repository over the seven
presets' real plans.

**Ruling** (the maintainer's, final): fix before the tag — a partial checkup
only writes statuses, closes lines and derives lines for parts inside the
picked scope; a full checkup goes on producing both sides. Out of this unit,
by the same ruling, and now entries of `docs/backlog.md`: a KO stored as
`ATTENTION` where plan §4.2 (b) says `BROKEN`; a hosted part's badge that does
not follow its closed list line; the yes/no refinement control (that entry
already existed).

#### Symptom

- **[W]** Signed in, the seeded gravel bike, a checkup opened with
  `?parts=chain,brake-caliper-rear`: its items were stored against the FRONT
  caliper and the front lever, and both then read "Bon état".
- **[W]** A guest checkup limited to the front tyre (and the chain), answered
  KO: the list held lines for BOTH tyres, each with its retailer links.
- **[T]** read, not seen: the same rear-only OK closes an open line about the
  FRONT pads ("Marqué fait par un contrôle"), on an account and on a guest
  list alike, and turns a front caliper left `ATTENTION` to `OK`.

The questions are worded for both sides ("sur chaque plaquette", "les
pneus"); the product calls the feature "Contrôler les pièces sélectionnées
(n)", and `tests/e2e/checkup-partial.spec.ts` "the questions about that
caliper and no others".

#### Root cause

`planCheckup` kept a step when ANY of its parts (host-expanded) was among the
picked ones (host-expanded), and then handed the step on WHOLE: `partIds`
every fitted host it names, `ko` every consequence about a fitted part. The
scope chose the questions and nothing after. Every reader of a checkup takes
a step's parts and findings from the plan — `answeredItems` stores
`partIds[0]` (the guide lists the front first), `statusByPart` →
`writePartStates` tints every `partIds` entry, `recheckedLines` →
`closeRecheckedItems` and the guest's `markRechecked` close every pair of
`ko`, `deriveBuildList` derives them — so all of them spoke for both sides.
22 of the 64 check steps name both sides of a front/rear or left/right pair
in one `ko[]` [T: counted from the frontmatter, both locales]; 26 name two or
more parts.

#### Fix

- **One definition of "inside the scope"**: `partsInScope`
  (`lib/checkup/scope.ts`) — the picked parts, the host each is reached
  through, the parts a picked host carries. Pads through their caliper, a
  tube through its tyre; NOT the other parts an unpicked host carries (the
  chainring and the bottom bracket both hang off the crankset).
- **The planner applies it, and nothing else does**: a step is asked when it
  names a part inside and, if it has findings, at least one finding about a
  part inside; it is handed on narrowed — `partIds` the hosts of the parts
  inside it names, `ko` the findings about parts inside, `coveredParts` when
  anything was left out. Every reader follows without a change of its own: the
  item's part, the statuses, the lines closed and derived, on an account and
  on a guest bike. A full checkup has no such set: unchanged, field for field
  (`tests/unit/checkup/plan-corpus.test.ts` writes the old rule out and
  compares, over the seven presets).
- **A question whose every finding is outside the scope is not asked.**
  Measured [I3], over the seven presets and every part each carries, picked
  alone: the steps the old rule kept with no finding inside the new scope are
  `check-brakes-disc#hose-leak` for a caliper or its pads (its only finding
  is the brake LINES), `check-drivetrain#shifting-index` for a shifter or the
  shift cables (the derailleurs), `check-frame-bolts#cockpit-bolts` and
  `check-headset#bar-alignment` for the handlebar (the stem), and
  `check-drivetrain#chainring-teeth` for the bottom bracket (the chainring).
  Asked, a KO there could have put nothing on the list — a leak reported to a
  page that then shows no line for it, with no symptom to pick (a KO without
  one does not advance the wizard: read in `lib/checkup/reducer.ts`). Picking the lever, the line, the
  derailleur, the stem or the chainring asks them. The one other change in
  what is asked: the chainring alone no longer asks the two bottom-bracket
  questions (6 presets carry both); the crankset still asks both. **One
  pickable part is left with no question at all** (found in review, §5.12
  RA-5; measured by a probe over the seven presets): the LEFT shifter, on the
  two presets that carry one — its only question was `shifting-index`, whose
  findings are the derailleurs'. "Contrôler les pièces sélectionnées (1)" on
  it now shows "Rien à contrôler ici" and a link to the full checkup. Whether
  such a question should be asked anyway is a product question
  (`docs/backlog.md`).
- **What the visitor is told**: under the prompt, "Contrôle partiel — votre
  réponse ne comptera que pour : Plaquettes ou patins arrière." / "Partial
  checkup — your answer will count only for: Rear pads."; on the summary,
  "Ne compte que pour : …" / "Counts only for: …" — the parts' own names, for
  a step `coveredParts` marks. No guide text was edited.
- **The import follows the same rule**: a checkup whose column is `PARTIAL`
  (by `effectiveScope`, the rule the column already followed) is married to
  the scoped plan, so its items, part states and links are about the parts
  inside.

#### What production rows may look like (nothing is rewritten)

No data migration. A partial checkup FINISHED before this deploy may have
written, on an account: `CheckupItem.partId` naming a part outside its scope
(the front one of a pair, for a rear-only checkup); `BikePartState` `OK` or
`ATTENTION` on parts outside it; `BuildListItem` lines closed `recheck-ok`
about the other side; lines derived for the other side. On a guest's list,
the same lines and closings, which an import carries into the account as the
visitor's own lines. **None of it can be told apart from what a full
checkup writes**: no column records which parts a partial checkup was
limited to (`Checkup.scope` is `FULL | PARTIAL`). The candidates are the
`PARTIAL` checkups completed before the deploy —
`SELECT id, "bikeId", "completedAt" FROM "Checkup" WHERE scope = 'PARTIAL' AND status = 'COMPLETED' AND "completedAt" < '<deploy instant>'`
— and on their bikes a later checkup of the other side is what corrects a
status or reopens a line.

**Browsers hold such checkups too** (§5.12, B6): a guest's run answered
before the deploy was planned by the old rule — whole questions, and
`hose-leak` for a caliper. Nothing in a browser is rewritten either. Read
after the deploy, its scope's plan narrows the questions it asked (its
answers count for the parts it was limited to, which is the ruling), and an
OK or a skip to a question that plan no longer asks — `hose-leak` in a
caliper's checkup — is left out instead of widening the run; a KO there
widens it, and its finding is kept. A guest list derived before the deploy
keeps its lines for both sides; the import carries them as the visitor's own
lines (§5.6).

#### Tests

- `lib/checkup/scope.test.ts` (new): the definition, `effectiveScope`,
  `resumeScope` (the last two serve §5.10; rewritten in §5.12, where
  `resumeScope` became `resumeTarget`).
- `lib/checkup/plan.test.ts`: narrowing on hand-shaped guides — the picked
  side only, the whole step when the scope holds both, findings narrowed, a
  question with every finding outside not asked (and asked through the
  lever), a question with no finding still asked.
- `tests/unit/checkup/plan-corpus.test.ts`: per preset, a full plan equal to
  the old rule written out; a one-part plan that never reports outside its
  scope, over every fitted part.
- `tests/unit/checkup/scope-rule.test.ts` (new): the walk's cases through the
  guest's functions — rear caliper only, all OK: the rear pads' line closed,
  the front one open, the rear caliper alone tinted, every item on the rear
  caliper; front tyre only, KO: one line; pads through their caliper; a tube
  through its tyre; each with its FULL control.
- `tests/integration/checkups.test.ts`, on PostgreSQL through
  `finishCheckupAction`: the same cases on an account, with the front
  caliper's ATTENTION and the front lever's UNKNOWN left as they were, and the
  FULL controls.
- `tests/unit/checkup/line-identity.test.ts`: the two paths' one list, now
  also for a later checkup limited to each side of every pair a question
  names, on the seven presets — and no line or status outside the scope moved.
- `lib/guest/import.test.ts`: the part-state cases rewritten to the rule (a
  rear-caliper checkup's items and statuses on the rear caliper), and a FULL
  control.
- `components/checkup/Wizard.test.tsx`: the sentence, in French and English,
  and its absence for a question recorded whole.

Existing cases changed, and why — each pinned the defect, or a fixture that
needed it: `plan-corpus`'s pad case listed `hose-leak`; three part-state
cases of the import asserted the FRONT caliper and lever for a rear-caliper
checkup; `tests/_helpers/long-answers.ts` limited its "both pads worn"
checkup to the REAR pads, and its front pads' line came from the defect — it
now picks both; `tests/e2e/checkup-partial.spec.ts` listed `hose-leak` among
the calipers' questions; `tests/e2e/build-list-long-answer.spec.ts` picked
the rear pads for a list of both — it now picks both.

**Seen failing** [I3], one change each, the product file put back from a
saved copy: the plan handing a kept step on whole again — 167 cases fail (3
on PostgreSQL, 6 of `scope-rule`, 137 of `line-identity`, 11 of
`plan-corpus`, 5 of `plan`, 5 of the import); the planner asking a question
every finding of which is outside the scope — 33; the import keeping the full
plan's state for a `PARTIAL` checkup — 3.

In Chromium [I3], `tests/e2e/checkup-resume.spec.ts`'s two cases of this
rule — the rear caliper alone, all OK; the front tyre alone, KO — in French
and English, against a build with the planner handing kept steps on whole
(and §5.10's old behaviour with it): 4 of 4 failed — the covered-parts
sentence absent, and `tire-rear` on the list.

#### The gates of §5.10, §5.11 and V1-1 (2026-10-22)

[I3], on the tree of `45d7072` (`w5/partial-checkup`), this Mac, other
